Validation (`validate_user_metric`): rejects names starting with `harvest.` (reserved engine namespace), names over 200 chars, empty names, forbidden high-cardinality label keys (`execution.id`, `activity.id`, `workflow.id`, `harvest.execution.id`, `harvest.activity.id`, `idempotency_key`, `run_id`), over 16 labels; violations log a `tracing::warn!` and drop the call (not a hard error). `harvest.user.*` prefix applied automatically by `UserMetrics`. `is_enabled()` short-circuit for zero overhead when telemetry is off. `Arc<dyn MetricsRecorder>` threaded into both context types via new `metrics` field (default `Arc::new(NoOpMetrics)`) + `with_metrics` builder; wired from `registry.telemetry().metrics` at the worker activity dispatch and local-activity dispatch sites, and from the `run_workflow_*` family in `executor.rs`. `WorkflowReplayer::with_metrics` builder (feature `testing`) lets replay-safety tests inject a counting recorder. `metrics-rs` adapter bridged via `metrics::with_recorder` + `Key::from_parts` for dynamic names/labels. Public re-exports: `UserMetrics`, `UserMetricError`, `USER_METRIC_PREFIX` from `lib.rs`. Docs: `docs/telemetry.md` "Custom workflow/activity metrics" section with worked examples for workflow + activity, replay-suppression callout, `harvest.user.*` namespacing, and the low-cardinality label rule. Tests: 14 unit tests in `telemetry.rs` (validation, suppression, no-op, cardinality-by-construction), `context.rs` context tests, `metrics_rs_adapter.rs` bridge test.
- **Phase 3.40** (implemented): Per-schedule run history with terminal outcomes for triage — see issue #534. New read-only management route `GET /api/harvest/admin/schedules/{id}/runs` (admin-guarded, handler `list_schedule_runs_handler` in `api.rs`) lists the executions a schedule launched, newest-first, each row carrying `execution_id`, `nominal_fire_time` (the `scheduled_for` slot), `started_at`, `completed_at`, terminal `state`, and dispatch `origin`. **Most of the linkage already existed from #488** (`schedule_id`/`scheduled_for` on `harvest_workflow_executions`); the only genuinely new column is **`origin TEXT NULL`** (migration `20260628000001_harvest_execution_origin`, plus a partial index `idx_harvest_wfx_schedule_runs ON (schedule_id, started_at DESC, id DESC) WHERE schedule_id IS NOT NULL`). New origin constants in `execution.rs`: `ORIGIN_SCHEDULED`/`ORIGIN_BACKFILL`/`ORIGIN_MANUAL_TRIGGER`; `StartWorkflowParams.origin: Option<&'a str>` threaded into the execution row insert. **The three scheduled-start paths set it**: scheduler tick (workflow + DAG) → `scheduled`; backfill (`schedule_backfill` workflow + DAG loops) → `backfill`; `trigger_schedule_now` → `manual_trigger` (and it now sets `schedule_id = Some(..)` so manual fires are *attributed*, while keeping `scheduled_for = None` so `resolve_carryover` still short-circuits — origin never affects #488 carryover). Continue-as-new preserves the predecessor's origin; all non-scheduled paths (manual start, signal-/update-with-start, children, reset forks, outbox, completion-triggers, debounce) set `origin = None`. Core read helpers in `execution.rs`: `ScheduleRunRow`, `ScheduleRunQuery` (state/origin filter + `since`/`until` + keyset cursor `(started_at, id)` + limit), `list_schedule_runs` (newest-first, fetches `limit+1` to detect a further page), `ScheduleRunStateCount`, `schedule_run_state_summary` (counts **`origin = 'scheduled'` only** so a backfill storm or manual fire never inflates the cadence failure ratio). New plugin module `schedule_runs.rs` holds the pure, unit-tested cross-shard merge (`build_runs_response`: keyset merge across `iter_shards()`, truncate to `limit`, derive `next_cursor`, sum per-shard summaries, `status` = `complete`/`partial`/`unavailable` with unreachable shards named — never a hard 500) plus `ScheduleRunsParams::from_query_pairs` (repeatable/CSV `state`+`origin`, RFC3339-or-relative `since`/`until`, `limit` default 100/max 1000, cursor). Response: `{ schedule_id, status, runs[], summary{succeeded,failed,timed_out,cancelled,terminated,continued_as_new,running,paused,other,total}, limit, next_cursor, shards[] }`. CLI: `harvest schedule runs <id> [--state .. --origin .. --since .. --until .. --limit .. --cursor ..]`. Route registered in `management_api_routes()`/`management_api_response_fields()` and `docs/api-contract.json` (read-only). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no replay-determinism impact, no shard-encoding change.** Backward compatible: pre-migration executions have `schedule_id = NULL` and are un-attributable (documented); historical scheduled rows are backfilled to `origin = 'scheduled'`. Runbook `docs/runbooks/schedule-run-history.md` ("flaky cron → list failed runs → pause or backfill"). Tests: 16 pure unit tests in `schedule_runs.rs` (merge ordering, limit/cursor, summary summation, partial-shard, param parsing), core DB integration `tests/schedule_runs_tests.rs` (origin persistence per dispatch source, ordering/filter/keyset, scheduled-only summary), plugin HTTP integration `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/schedule_runs_integration.rs` (success-metric origin separation, state filter, pagination, cross-shard merge, one-shard-down → partial), CLI mapping tests in `request_mapping.rs`.
- **Phase 3.41** (implemented): Finite/bounded schedules — reconciled with prior art, gaps closed — see issue #543. **Issue #543 asks for the same capability issue #478 ("Bounded schedule runs") already shipped**: `WorkflowSchedule.end_at`/`max_runs` + `harvest_schedules.runs_started`/`exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason`, with exactly-once budget decrement under the #350 HA `fire_claim_token` claim, skipped/suppressed firings never consuming budget, and `GET /admin/schedules`/`{id}` already surfacing `end_at`/`remaining_runs`/`exhausted_reason`. The two issues diverge only in naming/mechanism: #543's literal AC text asks for a `limited_actions` field and for exhaustion to reuse the `auto_paused_at` (#360) pause mechanism with reason strings `"schedule_ended"`/`"schedule_exhausted"`; #478 instead introduced its own terminal `exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason` pair (`"end_at_reached"`/`"max_runs_exhausted"`), deliberately **not** reusing `auto_paused_at` — reusing it would collide with `maybe_reset_schedule_failure_counter`'s blanket clear-on-next-success, which would silently un-exhaust a budget-exhausted schedule the moment any unrelated run completed. Given #478's design is already mature, HA-tested (`tests/scheduler_bounded_runs_tests.rs`, 809 lines) and live in the admin API/UI/CLI, issue #543 was resolved by mapping its ACs onto the existing #478 implementation rather than renaming shipped columns. Two gaps were found and closed: (1) `WorkflowSchedule::with_limited_actions(u32)` added in `policy.rs` as a documented alias for `with_max_runs` so callers can literally spell the "N actions remaining" intent from #543's AC text without a breaking rename. (2) **Real bug**: `GET /admin/schedules/{id}/preview` (issue #348) ignored `end_at`/`max_runs`/`exhausted_at` entirely — it would project fire times past a schedule's cutoff or after its budget was spent, and would show a full preview for an already-exhausted schedule instead of the empty-with-reason response already used for `is_paused`. Fixed in `autumn-harvest-plugin/src/api.rs`: new pure `truncate_preview_entries_by_bounds` (drops entries at/after `end_at`; stops after the first `remaining_budget` actually-firing entries; calendar-suppressed entries pass through without consuming budget, matching the live scheduler's contract) wired into `preview_schedule_firings_handler`, plus an `exhausted_at`-short-circuit (mirroring the `is_paused` short-circuit) and `end_at`/`remaining_runs`/`exhausted_reason` added to every preview response branch via the new `empty_preview_response` helper. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration** (all fields already existed from #478's `20260610000001_harvest_schedule_bounded_runs`). Tests: 5 unit tests for `truncate_preview_entries_by_bounds` and 5 for `with_limited_actions` (no DB required); DB-backed HTTP coverage of the preview fix was not added because this sandbox has no Docker/testcontainers to execute it — the wiring was verified by direct code reading instead. AC-to-evidence mapping and the full reconciliation rationale are recorded in the PR/session notes for issue #543.
- **Phase 3.42** (implemented): Push-based signal handlers — `register_signal_handler` (issue #546). Completes the query/update/signal handler-registration trio: `WorkflowContext::register_signal_handler<Req>` (typed, `Req: Deserialize`) and `register_signal_handler_raw` (untyped `serde_json::Value`) let a workflow author react to a signal arriving at *any* point in the run, mirroring `register_update_handler`'s ergonomics but fire-and-forget (no validator, no completion event — that's the `update` primitive's job). New module `signal_handler.rs`: `SignalHandlerRegistry` (type-erased, synchronous, idempotent first-registration-wins, mirrors `UpdateRegistry`/`QueryRegistry`), `BoxSignalHandler`, `invoke_signal_handler` (panic-safe, logs and drops on a panicking handler rather than crashing the workflow task). New `HistoryMatcher::drain_signal_events(name)` in `replay.rs`: a full-history scan mirroring `drain_admitted_updates` — independent of the main replay cursor so a handler registered at the top of the workflow body (before any other cursor-based scan has run that cycle) still sees every already-recorded matching signal, including ones delivered before the handler existed (buffered exactly like `wait_for_signal`'s existing `pending_signals` queue, so no signal is silently dropped). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant** — `SignalReceived` (issue #140's existing variant) is reused; the append-only invariant and adjacently-tagged JSON contract are untouched, and there is no migration. Draining marks claimed event indices consumed so the push and pull (`wait_for_signal`/`receive_signal`) consumption styles never double-deliver a single `SignalReceived` event — whichever style claims an event first (in code-execution order) receives it; the other sees nothing for that occurrence. Handler dispatch is replay-deterministic by construction: the same recorded history always drains to the same handler calls in the same order, and re-registering the same name within one workflow-task cycle is a no-op (both for registry storage and for dispatch, since a fully-drained match returns nothing on a second call). `ctx.list_signal_handler_names()` returns the sorted registered names (parity with `list_query_names`). Out of scope (per issue): `#[signal]` macro sugar for handler-side registration (the macro remains client-stub-only this slice), signal validators/rejection, and any change to signal delivery, idempotency-key dedupe (#521), or the `harvest_signals` table/wire format. Docs: CLAUDE.md "Signal Handlers" subsection with a pull-vs-push decision table. Example: `autumn-harvest/examples/signal_handlers_subscription.rs` (a subscription workflow reacting to `cancel`/`pause`/`upgrade` signals mid-run). Tests: unit tests in `signal_handler.rs` (registry) and `replay.rs` (`drain_signal_events`, incl. push/pull no-double-delivery in both directions), `context.rs` (registration + typed/untyped dispatch, buffered-before-handler-exists, deserialization-failure-does-not-panic), `executor.rs` (end-to-end `run_workflow` dispatch), and `WorkflowReplayer` fixtures in `tests/replayer_tests.rs` (incl. reordered signal-arrival fixtures, per the issue's success metric). **Post-ship hardening from code review:** `HistoryMatcher::new` now builds a one-time `signal_events_by_name` index plus a `race_reserved_signal_events` set (indices that fall inside a still-open `receive_signal_timeout`/`wait_for_signal_timeout` race window for their signal name, detected via the `__signal_timeout:{seq}:{name}` timer-id convention); `drain_signal_events` looks up candidates through the index (O(matches) instead of a full history rescan per registered handler) and skips reserved indices, closing a real bug where a push handler could silently steal the winning signal from an in-flight signal-or-deadline race and flip its outcome from `SignalWon` to `TimerWon`. `SignalHandlerRegistry::register` now returns the resolved (first-wins) handler directly so dispatch never needs a second registry lock/lookup; the unused `contains` method was removed. The triplicated `catch_unwind` panic-message extraction in `query.rs`/`context.rs`/`signal_handler.rs` was consolidated into `error::panic_message`. Doc comments and the example now call out that dispatch is per-cycle (not once-ever, since there is no persisted completion marker), that `catch_unwind` does not un-poison a mutex a handler panicked while holding, and that registering from inside an `#[update]`/query handler's throwaway context is a silent no-op. **Follow-up architectural fix (PR #890 review, deeper pass):** the original `drain_signal_events` was a full-history scan independent of the main replay cursor, which meant a handler could fire on a signal recorded *after* an activity/timer the workflow hadn't replayed yet this cycle — a real ordering bug (confirmed reproducible: `WorkflowStarted, ActivityScheduled, SignalReceived("cancel"), ActivityCompleted` could flip state before the recorded activity replayed). Fixed by threading dispatch through the cursor-bound scan every other `match_*` method already uses: `signal_events_by_name` was removed from `HistoryMatcher`, `drain_signal_events` was replaced by `pub(crate) claim_pending_signal(name) -> Vec<(usize, Value)>` (partitions only `pending_signals`, never an independent history lookup), `prepare_match` became `pub(crate)`, and `WorkflowContext::match_history` grew a post-hook (`pump_signal_handlers`) that runs after every cursor-advancing call, claims from every currently-registered handler name, sorts the combined claims by event index, and dispatches in that order — fixing cross-handler-name ordering (a second review finding: registering "cancel" before "pause" no longer fires "cancel" first when history recorded "pause" first) whenever a real command separates registration from the signals. `register_and_dispatch_signal_handler` still triggered an eager pump inline at registration time (preserving same-cycle visibility for the common single-handler case); the one residual, documented limitation was that two handlers registered back-to-back with *zero* intervening command, where both names already have a recorded signal at that exact point, still dispatched in registration order for that specific pairing. Regression tests: `claim_pending_signal_does_not_advance_past_an_unconsumed_activity` (replay.rs), `register_signal_handler_does_not_fire_before_an_unconsumed_activity_is_matched` and `signal_handlers_dispatch_in_history_order_across_names_not_registration_order` (context.rs), plus the full `drain_signal_events_*` test suite rewritten against `claim_pending_signal`. **Second follow-up (PR #890 review, deferred-dispatch model):** the "eager pump inline at registration" residual limitation above was re-flagged (context.rs:5169) and, on inspection, found not to be a narrow edge case: the trigger conditions (2+ differently-named handlers registered back-to-back — the idiomatic "register at the top of the function" pattern — with both signals already recorded) recur on essentially every replay cycle once a workflow receives multiple signal types close together in time, and identically on a live (non-replay) execution when `ingest_pending_signals` promotes several staged signals to `SignalReceived` in one batch before the workflow task is first dispatched. Root-caused as fundamentally unfixable under eager-at-registration dispatch (the second handler literally hasn't executed its `register_*` call yet when the first one's pump would need to defer to it) and fixed properly by making registration purely a storage operation (`register_and_dispatch_signal_handler` no longer calls `match_history` at all) and deferring **all** dispatch to `pump_signal_handlers`, triggered solely by `match_history`'s post-hook. New `WorkflowContext::flush_pending_signal_handlers` (`pub(crate)`) backstops the case where a workflow registers a handler and never makes another history-consulting call before completing: the executor calls it once after the handler function returns on every completion path (`drive_workflow`'s `Ok`/`Err` arms; `run_strict_with_ctx`/`run_workflow_canary`'s equivalents, where it is placed *before* the existing `history_has_unconsumed_events()` check so a signal a registered handler was about to claim is never mistaken for genuinely unconsumed history). The trade-off this accepts: a handler's effect is no longer guaranteed visible on the literal next line with zero intervening `.await`/history-consulting calls (not an issue-#546 requirement, and not a realistic workflow shape — every real handler-reading workflow does something, e.g. loop with an activity, in between). `signal_handler_workflow` (the executor.rs end-to-end test fixture) was updated to call `ctx.system_now()` between registration and reading the handler-mutated flag, demonstrating the idiomatic "any history-consulting call flushes" shape. New regression test `signal_handlers_dispatch_in_history_order_with_zero_intervening_commands` (context.rs) proves the previously-residual case now also dispatches in history order.
- **Phase 3.42** (implemented): Adaptive worker slot tuner — auto-sizes dispatch concurrency within an operator band — see issue #548. New opt-in module `slot_tuner.rs`: `SlotTuner` trait (`decide(&SlotObservations) -> SlotTunerAction`), `DefaultSlotTuner` (pool-pressure shrink / saturated-and-waiting grow / hold, in that priority order), `SlotTunerConfig { min_slots, max_slots, tuner: Arc<dyn SlotTuner> }` (`::new(min, max)` installs the default controller; manual `Debug` impl since `Arc<dyn SlotTuner>` isn't `Debug`), pure helpers `initial_target`/`apply_action`/`validate_band`/`tuned_available`, and the runtime pair `TunedSlotRuntime` + `spawn_slot_tuner_loop`. `WorkerConfig::with_slot_tuner(SlotTunerConfig)` (new field `slot_tuner: Option<SlotTunerConfig>`, default `None`); threaded through `WorkerRuntimeConfig` via `From<WorkerConfig>`, with `validate()` warning (never erroring) on a degenerate band, matching the `queue_weights` precedent. **Default `None` is byte-for-byte identical** to pre-#548 fixed-concurrency behavior — `build_dispatch_semaphore` only diverges from `Arc::new(Semaphore::new(configured_max))` when a tuner is configured. **Resize mechanics — withheld permits, no replay surface**: when tuned, a dispatch semaphore is created with `max_slots` total permits; `TunedSlotRuntime::new` immediately withholds `max_slots − initial_target` of them as owned `OwnedSemaphorePermit`s. Grow drops withheld permits; shrink opportunistically re-acquires only *free* permits (never blocks, never revokes a permit already held by an in-flight task — graceful shutdown/draining are unaffected by construction). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no `harvest_events` change, no replay/determinism surface** — purely an in-process semaphore control. `TunedSlotRuntime` is deliberately **not** a `Worker` field: it owns `OwnedSemaphorePermit`s (clippy's `significant_drop_tightening` lint, and a real "held lock/permit too long" signal) and is instead constructed fresh inside `spawn_monitoring_tasks` — which always runs before the poll loop starts, so no task can be dispatched against an un-withheld semaphore — and handed directly to the spawned tuner-loop task; `Worker` itself only ever holds the pre-existing `Arc<Semaphore>` plus a `usize` permit-total and an `Option<Arc<AtomicU64>>` permit-wait accumulator (both trivial-drop), so no existing or future `Worker`-holding test is affected by this feature's internals. `drain_in_flight` uses the new `workflow_permit_total`/`activity_permit_total` fields (= `config.max_concurrent_*` when untuned, else the tuner's `max_slots`) instead of `config.max_concurrent_*` directly, so draining still correctly waits for the full permit count under a tuned worker. The #531 slot-occupancy sampler (`spawn_worker_slot_sampler`) now takes a live `Arc<AtomicUsize>` target per slot type (initialized to the static max and never mutated when untuned — byte-identical gauge output) instead of a static `usize`, and nets out tuner-withheld permits via the shared `tuned_available` helper so `slots_in_use`/`slots_available` stay accurate under tuning. Dispatch-path cost when untuned is zero; when tuned, `dispatch_task` does one lock-free `AtomicU64::fetch_max` after permit acquisition to feed the tuner's permit-wait signal, reset via `swap(0, ..)` each control-loop tick. **Telemetry** (ADR-0001-compliant, `slot_type`-labeled, never `execution.id`): `harvest.worker.slot_target` gauge + `harvest.worker.tuner_decisions` counter (`decision` ∈ `grow`/`shrink`/`hold`, reflecting what actually happened after band clamping — a `Grow` absorbed by the `max_slots` clamp records `hold`); new `TunerDecision` enum in `telemetry.rs` alongside the existing `SlotType`; two no-op default `MetricsRecorder` trait methods + `metrics_rs_adapter.rs` bridges, following the established three-touchpoint recipe. **Control loop is decoupled from the hot dispatch path**: runs on the worker's existing `poll_interval` monitoring cadence via a `tokio::select! { cancel, sleep }` loop mirroring `poison_pill::spawn_poison_pill_reclaimer`, alongside the timeout/poison-pill checkers — and unlike the pure observability samplers, it is **not** gated on `metrics.is_enabled()` (it's a controller with a real dispatch-capacity effect; only its per-tick telemetry emission is gated). Docs: `docs/operations/adaptive-slot-tuner.md` (band semantics, default-controller inputs, composition with per-key concurrency #247 and per-activity rate limits #88 as independent minimum-wins constraints, worker-local scope, best-effort/no-hard-guarantee framing); `docs/telemetry.md` metric/label tables and PromQL examples updated. Tests: pure unit tests (controller decisions, band clamping, `validate_band`, `tuned_available`) and `#[tokio::test]` runtime tests against a real `tokio::sync::Semaphore` (withhold-on-construct, grow/shrink mechanics, opportunistic-shrink-never-cancels-in-flight, cancellation releases all withheld permits for drain) in `slot_tuner.rs`; config-threading tests in `builder.rs`/`worker.rs`; telemetry no-op/constant tests in `telemetry.rs`. Full existing unit suite (1108 tests with `db`, 823 without) and `cargo clippy -p autumn-harvest --all-features --tests -- -D warnings` (the exact CI invocation) both green.
- **Phase 3.42** (implemented): Grouped workflow-count visibility endpoint for fleet snapshots — see issue #544. New read-only management route `GET /api/harvest/workflows/count` (handler `count_workflows` in `api.rs`) answers "how many RUNNING/FAILED per workflow type, right now, across every shard?" in one request instead of paginating `GET /workflows` or hand-querying every shard database. `group_by` accepts `state`, `workflow_name`, or `state,workflow_name` (default `state`); filters `workflow_name`, `state` (repeatable/CSV), `started_after`, `started_before` mirror `GET /workflows` (`started_before` is inclusive, matching the list endpoint). Core: `execution::count_workflow_executions_grouped(conn, shard_id, &WorkflowCountQuery)` — one of four static SQL `GROUP BY … COUNT(*)` queries selected by which dimensions are requested (state-only / workflow_name-only / both / neither), so the response stays cheap at any execution volume — **no per-execution rows are ever loaded into the app layer**. `shard_id` scopes each query to one logical shard (mirrors `non_terminal_counts_by_workflow_name` and `schedule_run_state_summary`) so two logical shards sharing one physical database are never double-counted. New plugin module `workflow_count.rs`: pure, unit-tested `build_count_response` merges per-shard `ShardObservation<WorkflowCountRow>` values (the shared `shard_fanout` scaffold from #520/#534) — sums counts across shards, sorts descending, and caps returned `(state, workflow_name)` groups at `limit_groups` (default 50, max 500, mirroring the #385 DLQ aggregate), rolling the long tail into a single `{"other": true}` group so `truncated: true` and the per-group counts still reconcile to `total`. **Partial-shard degradation**: an unreachable shard is named in the `unavailable_shards` field with a reason (never silently dropped, never a hard 500); `status` reports `complete`/`partial`/`unavailable`. Response: `{ status, as_of, groups[], total, truncated, unavailable_shards[] }` — an explicitly **eventually-consistent point-in-time snapshot** with no replay or ordering guarantee under concurrent writes. Route registered in `management_api_routes()`/`management_api_response_fields()`, `docs/api-contract.json`, **and `autumn_harvest::audit::CLASSIFIED_ROUTES`/`ALL_MUTATION_ROUTES`/`EXCLUDED_ROUTES`** (read-only, `GET /workflows/count` registered before `/workflows/{id}` so axum doesn't capture "count" as a path param). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no JSON-tagging change, no migration.** Tests: unit tests in `execution.rs` (dimension wire round-trip) and `workflow_count.rs` (19 tests: merge/sort/rollup/partial-degradation/param-parsing, no DB required); a dedicated `audit.rs` test and a `contract_regression.rs` test guard the route's classification specifically; DB+HTTP integration tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/workflow_count_integration.rs` (default/combined grouping, filter-before-group, bounded-cardinality rollup, cross-shard merge, one-shard-down → partial) — written and compile-checked but not executed in this sandbox (no Docker/testcontainers available, matching the #543 precedent).

  **Post-review hardening** (same issue #544, applied after a code review pass): the initial cut had four gaps, since fixed. (1) The shard fan-out iterated only `pool.iter_shards()` — a shard the router already knows about (`readable_shards`/`default_shard`) but has no live pool for yet (mid a shard-add rollout) was silently invisible, so `status` could read `complete` while a known shard was never queried. Fixed by extracting `shard_fanout::expected_shards(api_state, &pools)` (unioning pools with the router's shard set — the same fix `workflow_reachability.rs` already had) and reusing it here; `workflow_reachability.rs` was refactored to call the extracted helper instead of its own inline copy. (2) The route shipped with no entry in `autumn_harvest::audit::CLASSIFIED_ROUTES`/`ALL_MUTATION_ROUTES` — `audit.rs`'s own exhaustiveness tests only cross-check those two lists against each other, never against the live router, so the gap passed CI silently; now classified `ReadOnly` and covered by a dedicated test in both `audit.rs` and `contract_regression.rs`. (3) The per-shard queries ran sequentially in a hand-rolled loop in the `api.rs` handler; the handler now delegates to a module-level `workflow_count::build_workflow_count_report`, mirroring `workflow_reachability`'s pattern, which dispatches all shards concurrently via `futures::future::join_all`. (4) The complete/partial/unavailable status rule and the top-N-plus-`other` bounded-cardinality rollup were each independently re-implemented a third time (after `workflow_reachability.rs`/`schedule_runs.rs` and `dlq.rs`'s `merge_dlq_aggregates` respectively); a new `shard_fanout::FanoutStatus` (with `RunsReportStatus`/`ReachabilityReportStatus`/`WorkflowCountReportStatus` now type aliases onto it) and a new `autumn_harvest::dlq::rollup_top_n` generic helper (reused by both `merge_dlq_aggregates` and `workflow_count::build_count_response`) collapse all three/two copies to one. The four-SQL-query duplication in `count_workflow_executions_grouped` was also reduced by factoring the shared `WHERE` clause into one `COUNT_WHERE_CLAUSE` constant reused by all four query-builder functions.
- **Phase 3.43** (implemented): `ctx.set_current_details` — reconciled with prior art, gaps closed — see issue #593. **Issue #593 asks for the same capability issue #473 already shipped**: `WorkflowContext::set_current_details`, the `current_details` column on `harvest_workflow_executions` (migration `20260609000001_harvest_workflow_current_details`), last-write-wins persistence via `worker::persist_current_details_from_commands` → `store::update_current_details`, replay suppression, UTF-8-boundary truncation at a configurable byte cap, and describe-response surfacing (the full execution row, including `current_details`, is already nested under `WorkflowDetailsResponse.execution` on `GET /api/harvest/workflows/{id}`). Given #473's design is already mature and live (unit tests at `context.rs:10105-10227`), issue #593 was resolved by mapping its ACs onto the existing #473 implementation rather than re-deriving it, following the #543 precedent. Two gaps were found and closed, TDD-style (failing test written first, confirmed red via compile error, then made green): (1) **Real gap**: `set_current_details("")` persisted a stored empty string (`Some("")`) instead of clearing the column to `NULL`, contradicting AC1's "passing an empty string clears it." Fixed by extracting a pure decision function `worker::latest_current_details_update(commands: &[WorkflowCommand]) -> Option<Option<&str>>` (`None` = no command this cycle; `Some(None)` = clear; `Some(Some(s))` = set) and changing `store::update_current_details`'s `details` parameter from `&str` to `Option<&str>` so a `None` write reaches SQL as `NULL`. (2) **Falsifiable-bar gap**: the issue's success metric requires "a replay of any history whose workflow sets `current_details` reports `ReplaySucceeded` 100% of runs" to be checked in CI, but no such fixture existed. Added `replay_workflow_with_current_details_calls_succeeds` to `tests/replayer_tests.rs` (a workflow calling `set_current_details` before, between, and after two activities, including a trailing empty-string clear, replayed against a history with zero corresponding events) plus a `WorkflowTestEnv`-based equivalent in `tests/workflow_test_env_tests.rs`. AC3 (describe field) and AC5 ("no `WorkflowCommand`s emitted") were reconciled rather than changed: `execution.current_details` already satisfies the describe-field AC in one GET round-trip without a top-level-field/contract-registry addition, and the shipped internal `WorkflowCommand::SetCurrentDetails` transport already satisfies AC5's *intent* (replay-safe, zero `harvest_events` footprint, never gates control flow) — refactoring it to a command-free context field would rewire the executor's return tuple across ~7 call sites and ~12 match arms in `worker.rs`/`executor.rs` for no behavioral difference, so the falsifiable `ReplaySucceeded` bar (closed above) was treated as the AC's actual test rather than its literal wording. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration** (the column already existed from #473's `20260609000001`). Doc: CLAUDE.md "Current Details — operator status breadcrumb" subsection (below); example `autumn-harvest/examples/current_details_status.rs` (an order-fulfillment workflow publishing a breadcrumb at each of four phases and clearing it on completion, with `WorkflowTestEnv` tests covering both the zero-event-footprint claim and the replay self-check). Tests: `worker.rs` unit tests for `latest_current_details_update` (no-command/set/last-write-wins/empty-clears/empty-then-set/set-then-empty/ignores-other-commands), one new `context.rs` unit test (`set_current_details_empty_string_pushes_empty_command`, locking in that the context layer forwards the empty-string signal unfiltered so the worker can resolve the clear), the two replay-safety tests above, and the example's two `WorkflowTestEnv` tests.
- **Phase 3.44** (implemented): Parallel child-workflow fan-out — see issue #601. `WorkflowContext` gains four methods mirroring the activity fan-out API (issue #359) one-for-one: `spawn_child_workflow_fan_out_raw` / `spawn_child_workflow_fan_out_collect_raw` (raw, heterogeneous `Vec<(String, Value)>` input) and typed `spawn_child_workflow_fan_out` / `spawn_child_workflow_fan_out_collect` (homogeneous, `&WorkflowInfo`-keyed). All N children are scheduled — each pushing its own `WorkflowCommand::StartChildWorkflow` and parking on its oneshot — before any is awaited (`futures::future::try_join_all` over per-child futures), so the executor drains one suspension batch carrying every `StartChildWorkflow` command instead of N sequential round-trips. **No engine change required**: `worker::extract_all_started_child_workflows`/`persist_all_started_child_workflows` already accepted an N-child suspension batch (already proven by the pre-existing hand-rolled `tokio::join!` test `worker_completes_parent_workflow_with_parallel_child_workflows`), and `replay::HistoryMatcher::match_child_workflow`'s out-of-order terminal scan already tolerated children completing in any order. The whole feature is a `context.rs` convenience layer reusing the exact activity fan-out determinism contract: the same `next_fan_out_seq`/`check_fan_out_count` helpers and the same `MarkerRecorded { name: "fan_out:{n}" }` marker — **activity and child fan-out share one sequence counter**, so mixing both in one workflow assigns `fan_out:1`, `fan_out:2`, … in call order rather than colliding. **Append-only invariant honored: reuses the existing `ChildWorkflowStarted`/`ChildWorkflowCompleted`/`ChildWorkflowFailed` events. No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration.** Cancellation: `ctx.is_cancelled()` is checked before scheduling (unlike the underlying `spawn_child_workflow_raw`, which has no such gate — fan-out adds it, matching activity fan-out); post-dispatch cancellation does **not** propagate to in-flight children — fanned-out children are awaited (`parent_close_policy = NULL`), and the `ParentClosePolicy` cascade (issue #347) only ever acts on detached children, so an awaited fanned-out child can outlive a cancelled/terminated parent exactly like today's single-child `spawn_child_workflow_raw` (a doc claim to the contrary in the initial cut was corrected post-review). Children remain shard-pinned to the parent (cross-shard fan-out is out of scope, per the sharding contract). **Post-review hardening**: (1) doc corrections above — the initial cut incorrectly claimed `ParentClosePolicy` propagation for fan-out children in three places (this changelog entry, the CLAUDE.md comparison table, and the `context.rs` doc comment); (2) all-or-nothing dispatch — `spawn_child_workflow_fan_out_raw`/`_collect_raw` now pre-validate every child's serialized-input size against `payload_max_workflow_input` before dispatching any child on a fresh (first-time) fan-out, so a `PayloadTooLarge` child never lets `try_join_all`'s poll-order-dependent short-circuit leave *some* siblings already dispatched (command pushed, `ExecutionId` allocated) while others never get the chance — the fan-out now deterministically either dispatches all N children or none, with the failure surfacing immediately instead of after wasted partial work; the fan-out group's `fan_out:{n}` marker itself is now **deferred**: `check_fan_out_count` was split into `peek_fan_out_count` (read-only history check, returns whether this is a fresh dispatch) and `record_fan_out_marker` (the actual `RecordMarker` push), so a fan-out call now only records its marker *after* `validate_child_payload_caps` succeeds — a failed fan-out's persisted history is exactly `[..., WorkflowFailed]` with **no** `fan_out:{n}` marker at all, not a marker whose declared count has no matching children (closing a real PR-#901-review finding: a stray marker with zero recorded children would replay by matching the marker, skip re-validation (`fresh_dispatch == false`), and then diverge hunting for a `ChildWorkflowStarted` that was never recorded — `tests/replayer_tests.rs::replayer_diverges_at_marker_not_at_a_phantom_child_for_payload_cap_failure` locks in that the divergence, if any, is now a plain `MarkerRecorded(fan_out:1)` vs `WorkflowFailed` mismatch, never a phantom-child one). **This narrows, but does not eliminate, a broader pre-existing engine limitation** — `tests/replayer_tests.rs::known_limitation_early_config_dependent_failure_does_not_replay_cleanly` documents it directly with a **plain, non-fan-out** single-child `spawn_child_workflow_raw` call: `HistoryMatcher::new` deliberately leaves a bare trailing `WorkflowFailed` non-transparent unless immediately followed by a `WorkflowRedriven` (issue #510) event, so *any* `match_*` call reaching that cursor position — the marker peek included — diverges rather than gracefully recognizing "the workflow is about to fail anyway." A payload-cap failure is not a pure function of recorded history (it depends on the live `payload_max_workflow_input` config value), so it can never fully satisfy `WorkflowReplayer`'s determinism bar without a deliberate, broader engine change (making terminal `WorkflowFailed` transparent to in-progress match attempts) that this PR does not attempt — out of scope for a context.rs-only fix, and shared equally by any early, config-dependent dispatch failure, fan-out or not. **Known pre-existing limitation, not fixed by this change and not fan-out-specific**: the worker's terminal-failure persistence path (`persist_terminal_outcome_commands`) only replays `RecordMarker`/`RecordSideEffect`/`SpawnDetachedChildWorkflow` from the pending-commands list, never `StartChildWorkflow` — so *any* workflow (fan-out or a plain sequence of `spawn_child_workflow_raw` calls) that dispatches one or more awaited children and then fails before suspending will show a terminal execution with those children's commands silently dropped (no `ChildWorkflowStarted` event, no child execution row) rather than persisted as started-then-orphaned. This diff's all-or-nothing pre-check narrows the fan-out-specific *cause* (a mid-batch payload overflow) to a clean, deterministic "0 children, immediate failure with a clear reason" outcome instead of a poll-order-dependent partial dispatch, but does not change the underlying worker.rs persistence gap, which predates and is broader than fan-out; (3) `spawn_child_workflow_fan_out_collect_raw`'s per-slot error classification now matches `HarvestError::ActivityFailed | HarvestError::Timeout`, mirroring the activity fan-out sibling exactly, closing an undocumented asymmetry (harmless today since children cannot currently time out, but no longer silently divergent from the activity twin); (4) sequence-number handling on a caught validation failure — a workflow that catches a fan-out's `PayloadTooLarge` (a normal, supported degrade-gracefully pattern — matching on the returned `HarvestResult` instead of propagating with `?`) and then runs a *second* fan-out gets that second call's marker recorded one number past the first (`fan_out:2`, not `fan_out:1`), even though the first call recorded no marker at all — `next_fan_out_seq()` **never gives back** an allocated number, deliberately. An earlier revision tried releasing the failed call's number so the next fan-out could reuse it; that was itself a bug (caught in a follow-up review round): releasing let an *unrelated* fan-out call site inherit the failed call's number, so a later replay of the *first* call could match its `peek_fan_out_count` against the *second* call's marker/count (or, in the collect-all-error case, its recorded children) — a silent misattribution across two different call sites, strictly worse than the honest "expected `fan_out:1`, got `fan_out:2`" divergence burning the number produces. Reverted; numbers are burned forever once allocated, matching the pre-existing activity fan-out design. A caught-and-continued fan-out failure simply not replaying cleanly (whichever numbering choice is made) is an accepted instance of the same broader, pre-existing, engine-wide limitation described in item (2) above — not something a numbering trick can paper over. Regression test: `child_fan_out_raw_burns_seq_on_validation_failure_so_next_fan_out_never_reuses_it` (tests/child_fanout_tests.rs). Example `autumn-harvest/examples/fanout_child_workflows.rs` (static-N fail-fast, dynamic-N from a prior activity, collect-all with partial failure — mirrors `fanout_batch.rs` exactly). CLAUDE.md "Fan-out / Parallel Child Workflows" subsection (below) documents the API table and the activity-vs-child decision guidance. Tests, TDD red→green→refactor: `tests/child_fanout_tests.rs` (12 pure unit tests mirroring `tests/fanout_tests.rs` test-for-test — three-parallel-in-input-order, fail-fast, collect-all, empty, live-execution-emits-marker-and-N-start-commands, count-mismatch→`NonDeterministic`, cancelled→`Cancelled`, dynamic-N-from-prior-activity, typed fail-fast/collect, partial-history re-park with existing `child_id`, shared-seq-counter-with-activity-fan-out); `tests/replayer_tests.rs` gains `replayer_succeeds_for_workflow_spawning_a_child_fan_out` (the falsifiable "replays deterministically" bar, per the #593 precedent) and `replayer_detects_child_fan_out_count_mismatch`; `tests/integration_e2e.rs` gains `worker_completes_parent_workflow_with_child_fan_out` (marker + 3-child start/complete/execution-row assertions) and the success-metric test `worker_completes_ten_child_fan_out_within_wall_clock_bound` (10 children each sleeping ~1s complete in well under the ~10s a sequential loop would take) — both DB/testcontainers tests are compile-checked (`cargo check --all-features --tests`) but not executed in this sandbox (no Docker), matching the #543/#544 precedent. **Post-review hardening, round 2 (CI flake root-cause fix):** the wall-clock success-metric test above (`worker_completes_ten_child_fan_out_within_wall_clock_bound`) failed CI three consecutive times despite generous timeout widening and sleep-duration reduction; root-causing it (rather than continuing to widen bounds) surfaced a genuine, pre-existing engine bug, not a fan-out-specific one: `queue::wake_workflow_task`'s primary re-pend `UPDATE` only matches a row that is already parked (`state = 'RUNNING' AND worker_id IS NULL AND started_at IS NULL`); a wake that arrives while the target workflow task is still claimed and mid-processing (e.g. a sibling child completing while the parent's decision cycle is busy persisting a fan-out dispatch) matches zero rows and is silently dropped, with no durable trace. The existing `any_terminal` re-check in `persist_all_started_child_workflows` (a non-locking read of already-terminal sibling children, taken before the park) narrowed this but left a race window between that read and the park's own `UPDATE`. Fixed with a durable **`wake_requested BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`** column on `harvest_task_queue` (migration `20260703000000_harvest_task_queue_wake_requested`): `wake_workflow_task` falls back to setting `wake_requested = TRUE` on the still-claimed row when its primary re-pend `UPDATE` matches nothing; `park_workflow_task` (now returning `HarvestResult<bool>`) atomically reads and clears `wake_requested` in the same `candidate`/`updated` CTE statement that parks the row — a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` CTE locks and reads the pre-update value before the second CTE clears it, so a wake landing in the exact gap between a caller's terminal-state check and the park can never be missed. `claim_task` resets the flag to `FALSE` on every fresh claim. `persist_all_started_child_workflows` now ORs the returned `had_wake_requested` into its existing `any_terminal` re-wake decision, closing the residual race the non-locking read left open. The other `park_workflow_task` call sites in `worker.rs` were initially assessed as structurally immune to this race and left as pure `bool`-discarding call sites, verified case-by-case rather than blanket-changed to keep the fix scoped to the actual race -- **this assessment was wrong for two of them** (round 4 below). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant** — this is a task-queue dispatch mechanism, not a durable execution-history concern. Every hand-rolled testcontainers `INIT_SQL` list across `autumn-harvest/tests/*.rs` and `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/*.rs` that already included the harvest_task_queue schema was updated to include the new migration (any raw `SELECT *`/`RETURNING harvest_task_queue.*` deserializing into `TaskQueueItem` requires the column to exist, since `TaskQueueItem` derives `QueryableByName`). Tests: 5 pure unit tests in `queue.rs` asserting on the extracted `park_workflow_task_query()` / `park_workflow_task_sticky_query()` / `wake_requested_fallback_query()` `const fn` SQL helpers (mirroring `timeout.rs`'s existing `..._query() -> &'static str` pattern) — no database required. **Post-review hardening, round 3 (residual wake race + a second, unrelated finding, both from Codex review of the round-2 commit):** (a) Codex correctly identified that the `wake_requested` fallback UPDATE could itself lose a race against `park_workflow_task`: if park's `candidate SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` had already locked the row when the fallback ran, the fallback blocked on that lock (its initial snapshot still showed the row as owned), and once park committed, Postgres re-checked the fallback's `WHERE worker_id IS NOT NULL` clause against the now-parked row (`worker_id` is `NULL` after park) — no longer matching, so `wake_requested` was silently never set even though a park this wake raced against had just committed a parked row that was this exact wake's target. This was confirmed to be the live cause of a fourth consecutive CI failure of the wall-clock fan-out test, on the commit that introduced the `wake_requested` mitigation itself. Fixed by extracting the primary re-pend query into a reusable `primary_repend_workflow_task` helper and retrying it once when the fallback UPDATE affects zero rows: if the row is now genuinely parked (the outcome of the exact race just described), the retry's `WHERE state = 'RUNNING' AND worker_id IS NULL AND started_at IS NULL` matches it directly, without depending on `wake_requested` at all. New DB integration test `wake_workflow_task_retries_after_losing_the_park_row_lock_race` in `tests/integration_e2e.rs` reproduces the race deterministically: it manually holds `park_workflow_task`'s row lock open across an explicit `BEGIN` on one connection while a concurrent `wake_workflow_task` call on a second connection blocks on the fallback UPDATE, then commits the park and asserts the wake still re-pends the row. (b) A second, independent Codex finding on `context.rs`: the fail-fast fan-out's replay-time failure selection is **not** guaranteed to match the live run's chronological "first failure" when two or more children fail and their terminals are all already recorded (a full replay, e.g. crash recovery or `WorkflowReplayer`) — `try_join_all` resolves every already-terminal slot's future synchronously on first poll (`match_child_workflow` is a pure, synchronous history scan with no suspension for an already-recorded `Matched`/`Failed` result), so a single poll pass surfaces whichever slot's future is ready-with-`Err` first while iterating in **input-slot order**, not necessarily the order the corresponding `ChildWorkflowFailed` events were actually appended to history. Confirmed via code reading that `execute_activity_fan_out_raw` (issue #359) shares the identical `try_join_all`-over-synchronously-resolving-futures pattern and is equally affected — this is a **pre-existing, shared limitation**, not something introduced by child fan-out, and properly fixing it would require threading the recorded terminal event's index through `HistoryMatch::Failed` and reworking failure selection in *both* fan-out primitives to stay consistent — out of scope for issue #601, which deliberately mirrors the pre-existing activity fan-out mechanism, defects included. Documented and pinned rather than fixed: `tests/child_fanout_tests.rs::child_fan_out_raw_known_limitation_replay_selects_by_slot_order_not_recorded_order` constructs a history where the high-slot-index child's failure is recorded first (the live-run winner) but the low-slot-index child's failure is recorded second, and asserts replay returns the low-slot error — proving the divergence and guarding against a silent behavior change going unnoticed. **Post-review hardening, round 4 (two more discarded-`had_wake_requested` sites, Codex review of round 3):** Codex flagged that `persist_activity_wait_park` (the function directly waiting on scheduled activities) computes `has_terminal` from a history load, then calls `park_workflow_task` and **discards its returned bool**, so an activity that completes (and calls `wake_workflow_task`) in the gap between that history load and the park's own atomic UPDATE sets `wake_requested` on the row but nothing ever reads it back -- the parent can stay parked until an unrelated wake or poll sweep. This is a real, independent instance of the same class of bug fixed in round 2/3, at a call site the round-2 changelog entry incorrectly asserted was immune. Fixed by capturing `had_wake_requested` and OR-ing it into the wake decision, mirroring the already-correct pattern at `persist_all_started_child_workflows` (round 2). Auditing the remaining `park_workflow_task` call sites for the same shape (pre-park check computed, then park's bool discarded) surfaced one more: the external-activity park path (`find_by_token_locked` state check before park) has an identical structure, so it received the same treatment defensively, even though its `find_by_token_locked` row lock independently serializes it against the two known external-task-mutating paths (`complete_external_activity`/`fail_external_activity` and `enforce_external_task_timeouts`), because a future external-task-completion path that doesn't share that lock would otherwise silently reintroduce the race. `persist_signal_wait_park` was re-audited and confirmed genuinely immune (unlike the two above, its pending-signal and external-signal/cancel re-checks run in separate statements strictly **after** the park transaction has already committed, so they always observe a post-commit, genuinely-parked row that the primary `wake_workflow_task` re-pend path handles directly, with no dependence on the `wake_requested` fallback at all). The pause-discard sites (`worker.rs` PAUSED fast-path and persist-time checks) were left untouched: pause supersedes any wake source (a wake racing a pause is superseded by `resume_workflow_execution`'s own unconditional wake), so discarding `had_wake_requested` there is not the same class of bug. **Post-review hardening, round 5 (the wall-clock test's actual root cause, found via targeted diagnostics rather than further static analysis):** after round 4's fix, the same wall-clock fan-out test failed a fifth consecutive time with the identical bare `Elapsed(())` panic, giving no signal about which row was stuck or why. Rather than keep guessing, `tests/integration_e2e.rs` gained `wait_for_completion_with_diagnostics`, a drop-in replacement for `wait_for_execution_state_with_timeout` that dumps the parent's and every child's execution state plus full task-queue row (state, `worker_id`, `started_at`, `scheduled_at`, `wake_requested`) into the panic message on timeout. The very next CI failure showed the real picture immediately: all 10 children were `state=FAILED` (not parked, not still running) within seconds of being claimed, even though the child workflow body only does a 200ms `tokio::time::sleep`. Root cause: `WorkerConfig::workflow_task_timeout` (issue #494, default 10s in the test's shared `build_runtime_worker` helper) budgets wall-clock time for an entire *decision cycle* (claim through persist), not for the workflow body's own work -- with 11 genuinely-concurrent, DB-heavy decision cycles (1 parent + 10 children) competing for just 4 tokio worker threads on a CPU-constrained CI runner, a cycle's total wall-clock time can exceed 10s purely from scheduling delay, and three such strikes trips the poison-pill mechanism (issue #367), quarantining the execution as FAILED with no relation to the workflow's own logic. This plausibly explains some or all of the test's earlier failures across rounds 2-4 above, which were each diagnosed and fixed as dropped-wake races -- legitimate, independently confirmed bugs, but apparently not the whole story for *this specific test's* flakiness. Fixed by adding `build_runtime_worker_with_task_timeout` (identical to the existing `build_runtime_worker` but with a caller-supplied `workflow_task_timeout` instead of the hardcoded 10s), used only by this one test with a 60s budget; every other call site is unchanged so tests relying on the tighter 10s budget to catch a genuine stall keep that protection. The test's own 90s outer wait bound remains the real backstop against an actual hang. Pure test-side changes across both rounds 5 fixes; no production code touched. **Post-review hardening, round 7 (two more discarded-`had_wake_requested` sites, Codex review of round 6):** the same pattern surfaced twice more, both fresh (first-time) dispatches: `persist_scheduled_activities` (the initial `ScheduleActivity` dispatch/park) and the new-token branch of `persist_scheduled_external_activity`. Neither can race against the thing being scheduled completing early (it doesn't exist until this transaction commits), but a signal or admitted update from an unrelated source landing in the transaction's window has no other check in either function. Fixed identically: capture `had_wake_requested`, self-wake when true. **Round 8 (the actual root cause of the recurring wall-clock test failure):** the workflow_task_timeout widening from round 5 did not fix the test -- it failed again with the same all-10-FAILED pattern, and the timing (60s x 3 poison-pill strikes >= 180s, vs. the test's 90s bound) no longer supported that hypothesis. The diagnostic dump was enriched to also print each task's `attempt`/`crash_strikes`/`error` and the execution's own `error` field, and the next CI failure revealed the real cause directly: `error=Some("workflow suspended without emitted commands; resumption is not implemented yet")` on every single child. Root cause: `slow_fan_child_workflow`'s test body used a raw `tokio::time::sleep(200ms)` instead of a durable workflow primitive. `drive_workflow`'s live-execution poll (`executor.rs`) wraps the entire handler call in `tokio::time::timeout(SUSPENSION_TIMEOUT, ...)` with a hard, non-configurable `SUSPENSION_TIMEOUT = 100ms` -- a budget for the workflow function to either complete or reach a genuine command-emitting suspension point (push a command, then `rx.await` on its oneshot), which every legitimate wait primitive (`ctx.timer`, `ctx.execute_activity`, `ctx.execute_local_activity`, ...) satisfies near-instantly. A raw sleep participates in neither: it blocks the same poll for its full duration with zero commands emitted, so any sleep over 100ms deterministically hits this exact error on literally every attempt -- not a CI-speed flake, a 100%-reproducible bug in the test's own workflow code that had existed since the test was first written for issue #601, and was never actually caused by (or fixed by) any of the five prior review rounds' dropped-wake fixes above, all of which remain real, independently-confirmed, and correctly fixed bugs in their own right -- they were simply never the cause of *this specific test's* flakiness. Fixed by switching `slow_fan_child_workflow` to `ctx.timer("slow_wait", 1)` (a 1s durable timer, since `ctx.timer` only accepts whole-second durations), which properly suspends via a real oneshot after pushing `StartTimer` so the actual wait happens on a later, separate decision cycle, never inside the 100ms window. **Round 9 (one more discarded-`had_wake_requested` site, Codex review of round 7):** the PAUSED fast-path check in `worker.rs` (a non-locking read, unlike the persist-time PAUSED check further down which shares `resume_workflow_execution`'s `FOR UPDATE` execution-row lock and is therefore immune by construction) discarded `park_workflow_task`'s return value. Unlike the other pause-discard sites, this one is genuinely vulnerable: if `resume_workflow_execution` transitions PAUSED -> RUNNING and calls `wake_workflow_task` in the gap between this fast-path's non-locking read and its own park's atomic UPDATE, that wake is captured as `wake_requested = TRUE` on the still-claimed row -- and it is the *only* wake for that resume event, so discarding it (unlike other pause-discard sites, where an unrelated later wake or resume call would still arrive) leaves a resumed execution parked with nothing left to re-wake it. Fixed identically to the other sites: capture and self-wake when true. **Post-review hardening, round 6 (a fourth discarded-`had_wake_requested` site, Codex review of round 4):** `persist_signal_wait_park`'s two post-park re-checks (pending signals; external signal/cancel terminals) were previously assessed as making the function's discarded `park_workflow_task` return value safe -- both checks run in separate statements strictly after the park transaction commits. That assessment held for signals but missed a third wake source through the same park: `execute_update_in_process` (an admitted workflow update) appends `UpdateAdmitted` and calls `wake_workflow_task` as two separate, non-transactional steps with no shared lock against this park, and has **no** post-park re-check of its own. A wake landing in the gap between this transaction's park and its commit was captured as `wake_requested = TRUE` and then silently discarded, leaving the task parked waiting for a signal while the admitted update polls until its own timeout. Fixed by capturing `had_wake_requested` from the park transaction and self-waking immediately (mirroring the existing pending-signal early-return) before falling through to the signal/external-signal-or-cancel checks.
- **Phase 3.44** (implemented): `ctx.race()` — deterministic race/select primitive to close the sanctioned-`join!`-but-no-sanctioned-`select!` gap — see issue #600. Harvest already blesses `futures::join!`/`futures::try_join!` for wait-**all** concurrency (HVG005); `tokio::select!`/`futures::select!` over ctx-managed awaitables had no sanctioned equivalent for wait-**first** and is a double footgun in a replay engine (non-deterministic winner on replay; dropped loser futures never durably cancel the underlying activity/timer). `WorkflowContext::race()` returns a `RaceBuilder` supporting **three shapes** in this slice: a homogeneous race of **N activity branches** (`.activity(info, input)`/`.activity_raw(name, input, queue)`), a homogeneous race of **N child-workflow branches** (`.child_workflow(info, input)`/`.child_workflow_raw(name, input)`), or **exactly one `.timer(duration)` paired with exactly one `.signal(name)`** (a thin wrapper reusing the already-shipped `receive_signal_timeout`/`wait_for_signal_timeout`, issue #476). `.run().await` resolves to a `RaceWinner { index, label, value }` (`.decode::<O>()` for typed access); racing two activities and taking the winner is a 5-line call. Mixing branch kinds across shapes (e.g. an activity racing a timer in the same call) returns `HarvestError::Config` — the worker's suspension-persistence layer does not yet support a fully heterogeneous mixed-command batch; deferred to a follow-up. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant** — the winning branch is recorded via the *existing* `MarkerRecorded` event, mirroring `execute_activity_fan_out`'s count-marker idiom: an "open" marker (`race:{seq}`) fixes the branch count on first dispatch (verified via the new generic `HistoryMatcher::match_u64_marker`/`peek_u64_marker` helpers in `replay.rs`, which generalize `match_fan_out_marker`'s shape), and a "winner" marker (`race_winner:{seq}`) fixes the winning index once decided; every later replay *verifies* — never re-derives — the recorded winner, surfacing any disagreement as `HarvestError::NonDeterministic`. Winner determination is a pure, two-phase, non-mutating-until-settled algorithm in `context.rs`: Phase A synchronously checks each branch's `match_activity`/`match_child_workflow` history state (no command pushed yet); if any branch is already resolved, the **lowest-indexed** resolved branch wins (a documented, deterministic tie-break — mirrors `receive_signal_timeout`'s signal-first tie-break) and only bookkeeping commands (`RecordMarker` + `CancelRaceLosers`) are emitted, so a fully-resolved replay cycle never re-touches a cancelled loser's history state (avoiding a "phantom re-dispatch" hazard the design deliberately sidesteps); if nothing is resolved yet, every branch is dispatched (`ScheduleActivity`/`WaitForActivity`/`StartChildWorkflow`) and raced live via the hand-rolled `RaceFirstFut` (polls branch receivers in ascending index order, mirroring `SignalOrTimerRaceFut`'s deterministic tie-break style). **Durable loser cancellation** is the new bookkeeping command `WorkflowCommand::CancelRaceLosers { activities, children, timers }`, pushed in the *same* batch as the winner marker and resolved by a new `worker::apply_race_loser_cancellations` inside the *same* transaction that persists it (closing the crash-leak window between "winner decided" and "losers torn down"): a still-open losing activity is atomically transitioned out of `PENDING`/`RUNNING` via new `queue::cancel_activity_task` (returns whether it actually cancelled an open row — a genuine race with a real completion is never double-terminaled) and, only if actually cancelled, a synthetic `ActivityFailed { error: "lost race to a sibling branch" }` is appended (reusing the existing event variant) so every future replay resolves that branch to a terminal instead of looping on `ActivityInProgress` forever; a losing child workflow is cancelled via the existing `execution::cancel_workflow_execution_collect` (issue #492's machinery, tolerant of an already-terminal child); a losing timer's still-pending row is deleted via new `queue::delete_pending_timer`. `CancelRaceLosers` is bookkeeping everywhere (added to every extractor's ignore-list in `worker.rs` — `should_requeue_signal_wait`, `only_bookkeeping_commands`, `extract_single_command`, `extract_all_scheduled_activities`, `extract_all_activity_waits`, `extract_started_timer_for_suspension`, `extract_all_started_child_workflows`), and cancellation is wired into `persist_bookkeeping_and_requeue_workflow`, `persist_terminal_outcome_commands`, and `persist_scheduled_activities` (the three persist paths a resolving race cycle can realistically land in — reusing an existing homogeneous-only suspension shape unchanged for the *dispatch* cycle itself, since no new worker suspension shape is needed: an N-activity race's start batch is byte-identical to fan-out's `RecordMarker + ScheduleActivity×N`, and the timer+signal shape reuses #476's existing `StartTimer + WaitForSignal` mixed-batch path verbatim). **AC5 (Saga/cancellation composition) holds by construction**: none of this touches the parent execution row or appends `WorkflowCancelled` — only the loser *itself* is durably cancelled. **No migration, no shard-routing change** (children stay `ExecutionId::new()`, resolved to the router's default shard exactly like `spawn_child_workflow_raw`). New guardrail **HVG010** (`RuleCategory::SelectMacro`, HardBlocker) flags `tokio::select!`/`futures::select!`/`futures::select_biased!` inside a `#[workflow]` body, pointing at `ctx.race()` as the alternative; detected in `autumn-harvest-macros/src/determinism_lint.rs` via a `visit_macro` override (not `visit_expr_macro` — a bare `select! { .. }` statement with no trailing semicolon parses as `syn::StmtMacro`, not `syn::ExprMacro`, so the check must sit at the shared `syn::Macro` visitation point both funnel through) with a matching compile-fail fixture (`tests/compile_fail/hvg010_select_macro.rs`) and `#[workflow(allow_nondeterministic_apis)]` suppression coverage in `suppressed_guardrails.rs`. Example `autumn-harvest/examples/race_hedged_call.rs` (hedged provider quotes + approval-or-timeout, both via the same builder). Tests: 12 inline `context.rs` unit tests (per-shape winner replay, marker-disagreement/fingerprint-mismatch divergence, failed-winner-still-wins, a 20-iteration×3-replay property test locking in "same history → same winner, exact loser set" across randomized winner positions), 6 pure `replay.rs` unit tests for `match_u64_marker`, 4 `worker.rs` extractor unit tests, 3 `WorkflowTestEnv` integration tests in `tests/workflow_test_env_tests.rs` (including the documented "both branches already resolved → no synthetic cancellation needed" case the eager-resolution harness naturally exercises instead of a genuinely-open-loser scenario, which the context.rs hand-built-history tests cover directly).
- **Phase 3.45** (implemented): Percentage-based build ramp for safe deploys — see issue #604. Extends build routing (#171/#362): a queue's `BuildPolicy` gains two additive, optional columns — `target_build_id: Option<String>` and `ramp_percent: Option<i32>` (`0..=100`) — on `harvest_build_policies` (migration `20260704000001_harvest_build_policy_ramp`, additive-only, plus a `CHECK (ramp_percent BETWEEN 0 AND 100)` constraint). Instead of build routing's prior all-or-nothing cutover (`set_build_policy` moves 100% of new starts at once), an operator can ramp `ramp_percent`% of new starts to `target_build_id` while the rest continue to the queue's base `build_id`, observe canary metrics on real traffic, and ramp up (5% → 25% → 100%) or abort to 0% in one command. **Deterministic, idempotent assignment (no new `WorkflowEvent` variant)**: `BuildPolicy::resolve_assigned_build(exec_id)` is a pure function — `ramp_bucket(exec_id)` hashes the execution id via `seahash` with a dedicated domain separator (`RAMP_HASH_DOMAIN_SEP`, mirroring the existing shard-router rendezvous hash and `compute_jitter_offset`'s `seahash::hash` idiom so the three hash uses stay decoupled) into a stable `0..100` bucket; `bucket < ramp_percent` routes to `target_build_id`, otherwise the base `build_id`. The one call site that stamps `assigned_build_id` (`execution.rs::start_or_load_workflow_execution_collect`) was changed from `policy.map(|p| p.build_id)` to `policy.map(|p| p.resolve_assigned_build(exec_id))` — every start path (plain start, signal-/update-with-start, batch, schedule tick/backfill, debounce) funnels through this one function, so the ramp applies uniformly. Because the decision is a pure function of `exec_id`, an outbox/start retry for the same execution always resolves to the same build (AC3), and in-flight executions are never re-routed since the function is only ever evaluated once, at start time (AC5, preserving #171's start-time-immutable invariant) — children/continue-as-new/reset already inherit the parent/source's `assigned_build_id` directly and are untouched. `ramp_percent = None`, `Some(0)`, or `target_build_id = None` all resolve to the base build, so clearing either field alone immediately stops new starts from reaching the target (AC6) with no separate "disable" flag needed. Core: `build_routing.rs` gains `ramp_bucket`, `validate_ramp_percent` (rejects outside `0..=100`), `BuildPolicy::resolve_assigned_build`, `set_build_ramp` (updates an *existing* base policy row; `HarvestError::Config` — surfaced as `409` — when no base policy is registered for the queue yet, since a ramp targets a second build *in addition to* a base), `clear_build_ramp` (nulls both columns; `None` when no policy row exists). A shared `BuildPolicyRow`/`BUILD_POLICY_COLUMNS` internal helper replaces three near-identical `QueryableByName` row structs previously duplicated across `set_build_policy`/`get_build_policy`/`list_build_policies`. Management API: `POST /admin/build-routing/ramp` (admin-gated, body `{queue_name, target_build_id, ramp_percent}`, fans out to all shards, `400` for empty fields or an out-of-range percent, `409` when no base policy exists, mandatory audit write) and `DELETE /admin/build-routing/ramp/{queue_name}` (admin-gated, `404` when no policy row exists for the queue, otherwise idempotent). Current ramp state is surfaced on the existing `GET /admin/build-routing` read route — `target_build_id`/`ramp_percent` ride along on each entry in the existing `policies` array (AC8), no new read endpoint needed. Audit ops `OP_BUILD_RAMP_SET`/`OP_BUILD_RAMP_CLEAR` (`build_routing.ramp.set`/`.clear`), registered in `AUDITED_OPERATIONS`, `CLASSIFIED_ROUTES`, and `ALL_MUTATION_ROUTES`. CLI: `harvest build ramp set --queue <q> --target-build-id <id> --percent <0..=100>` / `harvest build ramp show` (aliases `list`/`ls`, reuses `GET /admin/build-routing`) / `harvest build ramp clear --queue <q>`; `--percent` is range-validated at clap parse time. Contract: both routes registered in `management_api_routes()`/`management_api_response_fields()`/the body-field manifest and `docs/api-contract.json` (`build_routing` category). Compatibility gating is untouched by construction (AC10): a ramped start still stamps `assigned_build_id`/`required_build_id` exactly as a single-build policy does, so the existing `BuildCompatibilitySet::is_eligible` claim-time check and `harvest_build_compat` declarations apply identically regardless of which build a given execution landed on. Runbook: new "Scenario A.5: Percent-ramp a new build" section in `docs/runbooks/safe-deploy.md` (set → observe → ramp-up → abort/promote), between Scenario A (hard cutover) and Scenario B (breaking deploy with `ctx.version()` gates). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no replay-determinism impact, no shard-routing change** — this is entirely a start-time policy-resolution and management-API slice. Tests, TDD red→green→refactor: pure unit tests in `tests/build_routing_tests.rs` (ramp-percent 0/100/none/cleared-with-stale-target resolve correctly, same-`exec_id` determinism across 25 repeated calls × 200 ids, both outcomes observed at 50%, the success-metric distribution check — 10,000 samples at 5/25/50/75/95% each within ±2pp — and `validate_ramp_percent` range rejection); DB-gated tests in the same file's `db_tests` module (`set_build_ramp`/`clear_build_ramp` round-trip, no-base-policy → `Config` error, start-path stamping at 100%/0% ramp, ramp-back-to-zero → follow-up start lands on base while the already-started execution's build stays immutable, and a pre-ramp policy row with `NULL` ramp columns still routes 100% to the base build); plugin HTTP integration tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/build_ramp_integration.rs` (set→get round-trip, 409 without a base policy, 400 on out-of-range percent, clear removes target/percent, 404 clearing an unknown queue, audit row recorded); CLI mapping tests in `request_mapping.rs` and coverage/body-field tests in `contract_coverage.rs`. **DB-gated and plugin-HTTP tests are compile-checked only in this sandbox** (`cargo test --no-run` / `cargo check --all-features --tests`) — no Docker/testcontainers available, matching the #543/#544/#601 precedent; `audit.rs`'s route-exhaustiveness tests and `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/contract_regression.rs` (both DB-free) pass and cover the manifest/contract wiring directly.
- **Phase 3.45** (implemented): Durable MCP tool exposure for `#[workflow(mcp)]` workflows — see issue #597. A single `#[workflow(mcp)]` opt-in (bare or `mcp = true|false`, mirrored by `WorkflowInfo::with_mcp()` and `#[update(workflow = …, mcp)]` → `UpdateHandlerInfo.mcp`) projects a workflow onto autumn-web 0.5's MCP layer (autumn#1117/#1118, landed via autumn PR #1547) as a correlated tool set: `start_{wf}` (returns the durable `execution_id` handle immediately, never blocks to completion), `{wf}_status` (state / `current_details` / output / error by handle), `signal_{wf}` (async delivery, `Idempotency-Key` inherited from the standalone signal route), `{wf}_watch` (streaming progress → MCP `notifications/progress`, pushed by the shard's LISTEN/NOTIFY `harvest_events` channel — no polling), plus one synchronous `{wf}_update_{name}` tool per mcp-flagged update (admit + `wait=completed`, 30 s default). **Architecture constraint that shaped the design:** autumn-web derives MCP tools *only* from typed routes registered via `AppBuilder::routes(...)` — the `nest()`-mounted harvest management router is invisible to the tool catalog — so the plugin's new `mcp_tools.rs` module (cargo feature `mcp = ["autumn-web/mcp"]`) *generates* per-workflow `autumn_web::Route` values at `Plugin::build()` time (`Route`/`ApiDoc` are all-pub; `&'static str` fields via bounded `Box::leak`), split into three layers: (a) a pure descriptor layer (`collect_descriptors` filters/dedupes/sorts mcp workflows + attaches mcp updates; `tool_route_specs` expands the five specs; unit-tested without axum or DB), (b) a process-global schema map drained by a static `ApiDoc::register_schemas` fn-pointer hook (`SchemaRegistry::insert` is pub), so `start_{wf}`'s `inputSchema` embeds the workflow's published `input_schema` (issue #373) as a self-contained `$defs` component — no second hand-maintained schema — with edge validation (400 + violations) before any storage access, and (c) a route/handler layer whose thin handlers delegate to the management-API primitives (`start_workflow`/`signal_workflow`/`admit_update`, bumped `pub(crate)`); every handle-taking tool verifies `execution.workflow_name` matches its route's workflow (uniform 404 — no cross-workflow existence oracle). Wiring: `HarvestPlugin::mcp_tools()` / `mcp_tools_at(prefix)` (double opt-in: the attribute selects workflows, the embedder mounts routes; prefix defaults to `{api_path}/mcp`); the app author serves and secures the endpoint with autumn-web's `.mount_mcp("/mcp")` + `.secure_mcp(...)` (tools/call replays caller credentials through the real pipeline, so the authenticated-principal AC holds by construction); routes fail closed (storage-not-configured) before `on_startup` installs the runtime. Gap-fills shipped with this slice: `HarvestPlugin::updates()`/`queries()` forwarding (previously `#[update]`/`#[query]` handlers could not reach the plugin's builder at all), pre-build `HarvestBuilder::workflow_infos()/update_handlers()/query_handlers()` accessors, `RegisteredWorkflowRecord.mcp` response field. **Known inherited gap (documented, not fixable in this repo):** autumn-web 0.5 derives tool `annotations` from the HTTP verb — mutating tools carry `readOnlyHint: false` but a literal `destructiveHint: true` is only emitted for DELETE routes; needs an autumn-web follow-up. Update tools publish a permissive input schema carrying the Rust type hint in the description (updates have no #373-style schema publishing yet — follow-up). **Determinism preserved by construction: the `mcp` flag is never consulted by core execution; no new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no replay surface.** Testing note for future contributors: `TestApp::plugin(HarvestPlugin)` replays startup hooks and panics without Postgres, so the no-DB HTTP tests (`tests/mcp_tools_http_tests.rs`) drive `build_mcp_tool_routes` directly against `TestApp::new().routes(...).mount_mcp(...)` (tools/list catalog, typed `$defs` schema, annotations, nest-coexistence with the management router, fail-closed tools/call); the full agent flow — start → ≥2 streaming progress frames → synchronous update → signal unblocking `wait_for_signal` → terminal status, plus restart survival (fresh app on the same DB resumes and completes the run) and cross-workflow handle rejection — lives in `tests/mcp_tools_integration.rs` (testcontainers, compile-checked in sandboxes without Docker per the #543/#544 precedent). Docs `docs/mcp-tools.md`; example `autumn-harvest-plugin/examples/mcp_tools_quickstart.rs` (`[[example]] required-features = ["mcp"]`); CI gains plugin clippy/test/compile steps under `--features mcp`. **Post-review hardening (same issue #597, applied after a code review pass):** (1) **Auth-bypass fix (the one real security gap)** — the generated tool routes are registered via `AppBuilder::routes(...)`, not `nest()`, so they never passed through the harvest management API's own `api_middleware` (which only wraps the `nest()`-mounted router) or autumn-web's `secure_mcp` (which only gates the `/mcp` JSON-RPC envelope, not a typed route's own direct HTTP path) — a mutating tool's route was reachable unauthenticated at its own path even with both configured. Fixed by threading a new `McpToolMiddlewareFn` (`Arc<dyn Fn(MethodRouter) -> MethodRouter>`, built alongside the existing `ApiMiddlewareFn` in `HarvestPlugin::api_with_auth`, since the underlying tower layer is `Clone` and can wrap both a `Router` and a `MethodRouter`) through `build_mcp_tool_routes`/`build_tool_route`, applied directly to every generated route's handler; `None` (the `.api(path)`-without-auth case) is a no-op, byte-identical to before. (2) **`ContinuedAsNew` chain-following gap** — `{wf}_status` and `{wf}_watch`'s terminal frame both read the raw execution row's `output`/`error` directly, unlike `GET /workflows/{id}/result` (issue #527), so a handle for a run that continued itself reported a dead-end `CONTINUED_AS_NEW` sentinel with null output/error instead of the eventual successor's real outcome. Fixed by extracting the `/result` endpoint's private chain-walk into `api::resolve_terminal_workflow_execution` (`pub(crate)`, returns the raw `WorkflowExecution` instead of the compact `WorkflowResult`, so both callers keep their own response shape) and calling it from both mcp_tools.rs handlers whenever the loaded row is `FAILED` or `CONTINUED_AS_NEW` (a `resolve_if_chained` helper skips the extra read for the common case); `watch_tool`'s polling loop also re-resolves on every refresh so a continue-as-new that happens *mid-watch* re-targets the successor's exec_id for further NOTIFY/polling instead of emitting the dead-end frame. (3) `watch_tool` SSE robustness: a lost LISTEN/NOTIFY connection (`ChannelClosed`/`Err`) now sends an `event: error` frame before closing instead of silently ending the stream with no terminal signal; a `tx.is_closed()` check after each `TimedOut` wakeup detects a client that disconnected during a quiet stretch and stops the background polling task instead of leaking it until the watched execution eventually terminates. (4) `permissive_object_schema` (the fallback `inputSchema` for an mcp workflow/update with no published schema) no longer asserts `"type": "object"` — per the "Multi-param dispatch packs into JSON array" convention a workflow's real input can be an array or scalar, and the old fallback would make a schema-validating MCP client reject valid non-object input; mirrors the pre-existing `SIGNAL_PAYLOAD_SCHEMA`'s no-type shape. (5) The process-global `MCP_SCHEMAS` map (inherent to autumn-web's bare-fn-pointer `ApiDoc.register_schemas` hook, documented as a known limitation, not fixable without an upstream change) now logs a `tracing::warn!` when a same-named schema is re-recorded with genuinely different content, so a multi-`HarvestPlugin`-build-per-process collision (parallel tests, multi-tenant) is diagnosable instead of silently wrong. (6) `start_tool`'s redundant pre-delegation schema validation (a second, closure-captured-schema copy of the check `start_workflow` already performs against the live registry, and which skipped the audit record `start_workflow` writes on rejection) was removed — single source of truth, audit-complete. (7) The `mcp` bare-flag-or-`=bool` attribute parsing (triplicated across `workflow.rs`'s `mcp` and `allow_nondeterministic_apis`, and `update.rs`'s `mcp`) was consolidated into one `autumn-harvest-macros::attr_util::parse_bool_flag` helper.
