Regression tests: `mcp_tool_routes_apply_the_configured_middleware` / `..._are_unwrapped_when_no_middleware_is_configured` (mcp_tools.rs, a real deny-all tower layer proves the wrapping), `permissive_object_schema_carries_no_type_constraint`, `record_schemas_is_idempotent_and_last_write_wins_on_divergence` (all mcp_tools.rs unit tests), and `status_and_watch_follow_the_continue_as_new_chain_to_the_real_result` (`tests/mcp_tools_integration.rs`, testcontainers, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent as the rest of this suite). **Post-review hardening, round 2 (PR #908 automated review, Codex):** (1) **CI gap** — the plain (no-`mcp`-feature) `cargo clippy -p autumn-harvest-plugin --all-targets -- -D warnings` lint job failed: `StartWorkflowRequest::from_input`/`AdmitUpdateRequest::new` (in `api.rs`, always compiled) are only called from `mcp_tools.rs`, which is itself `#[cfg(feature = "mcp")]`-gated at the `lib.rs` `pub mod mcp_tools;` declaration — so a default build genuinely has zero callers and `-D warnings` promotes the resulting `dead_code` warning to a hard error. Fixed with `#[cfg_attr(not(feature = "mcp"), allow(dead_code))]` on both. (2) **Deferred-start (debounce/batch) workflows broke the MCP handle contract** — an `#[workflow(mcp)]` workflow that also carries a `.debounce(...)`/`.batch(...)` policy delegates `start_{wf}` to the normal admission path, which can return `202 Accepted` with **no** `execution_id` (the scanner fires it later); every other generated tool (`{wf}_status`/`signal_{wf}`/`{wf}_watch`/updates) requires that handle immediately, so such a workflow's `start_{wf}` tool was silently broken for agents. Fixed at the `collect_descriptors` layer (not per-request): a workflow with `info.debounce.is_some() || info.batch.is_some()` is excluded from MCP exposure entirely, with a `tracing::warn!`, mirroring the existing duplicate-registration warn-and-skip precedent — keeps "the handle is the correlation token" true for every *exposed* workflow rather than reactively erroring per call. (3) **`signal_{wf}`/`{wf}_update_{name}` didn't follow the `ContinuedAsNew` chain** — `status_tool`/`watch_tool` already resolved a sealed predecessor to its live successor (round 1 above), but `signal_tool`/`update_tool` only used `load_owned_execution` for the ownership check and then delegated with the **original** (possibly now-terminal) handle string; the underlying `signal_workflow`/`admit_update` paths reject a terminal execution, so an agent holding the original durable handle could never signal or update a workflow again after it continued-as-new, even though `{wf}_status` reported it as still `RUNNING`. Fixed by routing both through the same `resolve_if_chained` helper and delegating with the **resolved** execution's id (`execution.id.to_string()`) instead of the caller's handle. Tests: `collect_descriptors_excludes_debounced_and_batched_workflows` (mcp_tools.rs unit test) and `signal_and_update_follow_the_continue_as_new_chain_to_the_live_successor` (`tests/mcp_tools_integration.rs`, testcontainers, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent). **Post-review hardening, round 3 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P1):** the round-1 auth fix closed the gap only for embedders who call `HarvestPlugin::api_with_auth`; an embedder who instead relies solely on autumn-web's `AppBuilder::secure_mcp(...)` (or configures no auth at all) still has the generated `start_{wf}`/`signal_{wf}`/update tool routes reachable **unauthenticated at their own direct HTTP path** — `secure_mcp` only gates the `/mcp` JSON-RPC envelope, not a route registered via `AppBuilder::routes(...)`. This is not mechanically fixable from inside `HarvestPlugin`: `secure_mcp` is configured on the outer `AppBuilder` *after* `Plugin::build()` returns, so the plugin has no hook to detect or intercept it. Given the trade-off between an airtight-but-breaking hard requirement (panic unless `api_with_auth` is configured) versus a non-breaking diagnostic, resolved (user-directed) in favor of the latter: `Plugin::build()` now logs a `tracing::warn!` naming the exact risk whenever `mcp_tools_enabled` is true and no `api_with_auth` middleware was configured, regardless of whether `secure_mcp` is set — the decision itself is the pure, unit-tested `mcp_tools_unprotected(mcp_tools_enabled, has_tool_middleware)` helper in `plugin.rs` (kept separate from the `tracing` call so it's testable without a subscriber). `docs/mcp-tools.md`'s Auth principal section spells out that `secure_mcp` alone is not enough. Tests: `mcp_tools_unprotected_flags_enabled_without_middleware_only`, `harvest_plugin_api_with_auth_also_sets_mcp_tool_middleware` (both `plugin.rs` unit tests). **Post-review hardening, round 4 (PR #908 automated review, Codex):** (1) **Update validators were never consulted** — `admit_update` (the standalone `POST /workflows/{id}/update/{update_name}` handler, shared by both the plain HTTP route and the new `{wf}_update_{name}` MCP tool) durably appended `UpdateAdmitted` and woke the worker without ever checking the registered `UpdateHandlerInfo::validator`, unlike the sibling `update_with_start_workflow_execution` path which already runs its validator before admission. An invalid payload therefore became durable history instead of being rejected at the edge, and would only surface as a failure deep inside the workflow (or never, if the handler didn't itself re-validate). Fixed by mirroring `update_with_start`'s exact check inside `admit_update`: load the execution row to resolve `workflow_name` (validators are scoped by `(workflow, update_name)`, not `update_name` alone, since two different workflows can register same-named updates), look up the matching `UpdateHandlerInfo` in `runtime.registry.update_handlers`, and run its validator (if any) before `store::admit_update_event` — a rejection returns `422 Unprocessable Entity` with `{"error": "update rejected by validator", "reason"}`, matching `update_with_start`'s response shape. This is a fix to the shared primitive, not an MCP-only patch, so both the standalone HTTP update route and the MCP update tool benefit. No audit record is written for the rejection (mirroring `admit_update`'s existing no-audit posture — unlike `update_with_start`, this route was never wired into the audit system to begin with, so adding audit here would be new, out-of-scope coverage). (2) **The quickstart example undersold its own auth requirement** — it showed only `.secure_mcp(...)` as "the production auth story," but `secure_mcp` gates only the `/mcp` JSON-RPC envelope; the generated tool routes' own direct HTTP paths (e.g. `POST /api/harvest/mcp/workflows/document_review/start`) need `HarvestPlugin::api_with_auth` too (per round 3 above). `examples/mcp_tools_quickstart.rs` now configures both (`api_with_auth` + `secure_mcp`, both using `autumn_web::auth::RequireAuth` as an illustrative stand-in) with a comment explaining why both are needed. Tests: `mcp_update_tool_rejects_invalid_payload_via_registered_validator` (`tests/mcp_tools_integration.rs`, testcontainers, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent), new `set_priority_validated` mcp-update fixture with a `validate_priority` validator. **Post-review hardening, round 5 (PR #908 automated review, Codex): cross-workflow generated tool-name collisions.** Operation ids are derived purely from a workflow's (and its updates') name via fixed string templates (`start_{wf}`, `{wf}_status`, `signal_{wf}`, `{wf}_update_{name}`, `{wf}_watch`) — two differently-named workflows can still collide, e.g. workflow `invoice_status`'s start tool (`start_invoice_status`) is the same string as workflow `start_invoice`'s status tool (`start_invoice_status`); the same is true across a workflow's own update-derived id and a sibling workflow's core tool name (e.g. workflow `a` with update `watch` generates `a_update_watch`, colliding with workflow `a_update`'s own watch tool). autumn-web's tool derivation keeps only the first registration and silently drops later duplicates, so an unchecked collision would make one exposed workflow quietly lose a tool from `tools/list`/`tools/call` while its direct HTTP route still exists and works. Fixed at the `collect_descriptors` layer (the same place the debounce/batch and duplicate-workflow-name checks already live): a new pure `tool_operation_ids(descriptor) -> Vec<String>` helper computes the full set of operation ids a candidate workflow's tool set would register (kept in sync by hand with `tool_route_specs`'s `operation_id` fields, since operation ids don't depend on the mount prefix); `collect_descriptors` now tracks a `seen_operation_ids: HashSet<String>` across the whole workflow list and excludes (with a `tracing::warn!`) any candidate whose generated ids collide with an already-accepted workflow's — first-seen wins, consistent with the existing duplicate-registration precedent, and the whole colliding workflow is dropped rather than partially exposed. Tests: `collect_descriptors_excludes_workflows_with_colliding_generated_tool_names` (the exact `invoice_status`/`start_invoice` scenario) and `collect_descriptors_excludes_workflows_whose_update_id_collides` (update-derived vs. sibling-core-tool collision), both `mcp_tools.rs` unit tests. **Post-review hardening, round 6 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P1): no-DB test asserted the wrong thing for the wrong reason.** `tests/mcp_tools_http_tests.rs::tools_call_dispatches_through_the_real_pipeline_and_fails_closed` sent a schema-violating `start_order_flow` body and asserted the response text contained `"400"` or `"validation"`, intending to prove issue #373 schema rejection at the tool edge. Round 1's fix (removing `start_tool`'s redundant pre-delegation schema validation, item (6) above) silently invalidated that assertion: with no runtime installed, `start_workflow` now fails closed on `harvest runtime is not started` (an HTTP 400 problem-details response) *before* ever reaching its own schema check, and `"400"` also appears in that response's `"status":400` field — so the assertion kept passing, but for an unrelated reason, and would never have caught a real schema-validation regression in this no-DB harness. Confirmed empirically (temporary debug print, reverted) that the actual response is `{"detail":"harvest runtime is not started", "status":400, ...}`, not a schema-violation payload. Fixed by (a) correcting the no-DB test to assert on the real, reachable guarantee — fail-closed on `"harvest runtime is not started"` — with a comment explaining why schema-violation rejection cannot be exercised without a live runtime, and (b) adding `mcp_start_tool_rejects_input_that_violates_the_published_schema` to `tests/mcp_tools_integration.rs` (testcontainers, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent) as the guarantee's actual coverage: `start_agent_approval_flow` with a non-string body against `agent_approval_flow`'s published string schema is rejected with the issue #373 violation response. **Post-review hardening, round 7 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P2): duplicate workflow-name resolution didn't match the runtime's own dedupe.** `HandlerRegistry::with_state_and_telemetry` collapses the builder's `Vec<WorkflowInfo>` into a `HashMap<String, WorkflowInfo>` via `.collect()`, so on a duplicate workflow name the runtime executes the **last** registration (`HashMap` last-wins). `collect_descriptors` instead filtered to `mcp`-flagged entries first and then kept the **first** same-named descriptor — so `.workflows([foo.with_mcp(), foo])` (a second, non-`mcp` `foo` registered afterward) still exposed MCP tools for `foo` derived from the first entry's schema/description, even though the runtime's last-wins `HashMap` would resolve `foo` to the second, non-`mcp` `WorkflowInfo` it actually executes — a tool surface advertising a workflow the runtime does not run that way. Fixed by resolving the same last-wins `HashMap` collapse (with a `tracing::warn!` naming the duplicate) *before* filtering by `mcp`, so tool exposure and schema derivation always read the exact `WorkflowInfo` the runtime will execute. Tests: `collect_descriptors_resolves_duplicate_names_last_wins_like_the_runtime_hashmap` (a duplicate that loses `mcp` on the later registration must not be exposed) and `collect_descriptors_duplicate_last_registration_with_mcp_and_schema_is_exposed` (the reverse: the later registration's schema/description must be what's advertised), both `mcp_tools.rs` unit tests. **Post-review hardening, round 8 (PR #908 automated review, Codex): one doc fix, one finding investigated and found not to apply.** (1) `docs/mcp-tools.md`'s primary "Opt-in" snippet showed plain `.api("/api/harvest")` alongside `secure_mcp(...)` commented "gate agent access in production," which (like the quickstart example before round 4's fix) undersells the requirement: `secure_mcp` only gates the `/mcp` JSON-RPC envelope, not a generated tool route's own direct HTTP path -- fixed by switching the snippet to `.api_with_auth(...)` with a comment pointing at the "Safety posture" section. (2) A P2 finding claimed `api_with_auth`'s `M: Clone` bound was a new requirement introduced by this PR's `McpToolMiddlewareFn` threading, unnecessarily forcing `Clone` on embedders who never use the `mcp` feature. Investigated by diffing against `origin/trunk-dev` directly: `api_with_auth<M>` already required `M: Clone` **before** this PR touched the function at all, because the underlying call `router.layer(middleware)` goes through `autumn_web::reexports::axum::Router::layer`, whose own signature is `fn layer<L>(self, layer: L) -> Router<S> where L: Layer<Route> + Clone + Send + Sync + 'static` -- `Clone` was never optional for this method, independent of MCP tool routes. Confirmed empirically: splitting `api_with_auth` into an `mcp`-feature-gated (`Clone`-requiring) overload and a lighter non-`mcp` overload that dropped `Clone` failed to compile with exactly the pre-existing `Router::layer` bound error. No code change made; the finding does not describe an actual regression. **Post-merge fixup (trunk-dev merge, not a review finding):** merging `origin/trunk-dev` (which landed issue #603's `nd_block_tests.rs` concurrently with this branch's `mcp: bool` field addition to `WorkflowInfo`) surfaced a missing-field compile error at `autumn-harvest/tests/nd_block_tests.rs:285` -- that file's `wf_info()` helper builds a raw `WorkflowInfo { .. }` literal predating the `mcp` field and the merge cannot auto-add a struct field to a literal. Fixed with `mcp: false,`. Caught by the CI Lint job (`cargo clippy -p autumn-harvest --all-features --tests -- -D warnings`) on the post-merge push; verified with a full re-run of every CI lint/test command listed in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` for the non-DB-requiring jobs. **Post-review hardening, round 10 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P2): duplicate update-handler resolution didn't match the runtime's own dedupe.** Same bug class as round 7, this time for `(workflow, update_name)` pairs: `admit_update`/`update_with_start_workflow_execution` resolve the declarative update handler to validate/admit against via `registry.update_handlers.iter().find(|h| h.workflow == workflow_name && h.name == update_name)` -- `Iterator::find` is first-wins. `collect_descriptors` instead filtered to `mcp`-flagged updates first and then kept the first *mcp-flagged* one for a given `(workflow, name)` pair -- so if the true first registration for that pair was not `mcp`-flagged but a later duplicate was, the generated `{wf}_update_{name}` tool advertised the later registration's schema/hint while the runtime validates/admits against the earlier, non-mcp one. Fixed by resolving first-wins across the whole unfiltered `updates` list (mirroring `Iterator::find`, with a `tracing::warn!` naming the duplicate) *before* filtering by `mcp`, exactly mirroring round 7's fix for workflow names (last-wins there, since `WorkflowInfo` goes through a `HashMap::collect()`; first-wins here, since `UpdateHandlerInfo` stays a `Vec` resolved via `.find()`). Tests: `collect_descriptors_update_resolves_duplicates_first_wins_like_the_runtime_find` (a duplicate that gains `mcp` on the later registration must not be exposed) and `collect_descriptors_update_first_mcp_registration_wins_over_later_duplicate` (the first registration's hint must be what's advertised), both `mcp_tools.rs` unit tests. **Post-review hardening, round 11 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P2): a self-inflicted regression from round 7's own fix.** Round 7 introduced `names.sort_unstable()` (alphabetical) before the operation-id collision-detection loop, to make the workflow-name-dedup rewrite easier to reason about. This silently broke the collision tie-break's documented and tested contract -- "first-registered wins" -- by replacing it with "alphabetically-first wins": registering `start_invoice` before `invoice_status` (a real, valid registration order) would incorrectly exclude `start_invoice` and keep `invoice_status`, since "i" sorts before "s", even though `start_invoice` was registered first. The pre-existing collision test (`invoice_status` registered before `start_invoice`) didn't catch this because registration order and alphabetical order happened to coincide in that example. Fixed by building the collision-loop's processing order from first-occurrence position in the original `workflows` slice instead of sorting alphabetically; the already-present final `descriptors.sort_by(name)` still sorts the *returned* vector, so no test asserting sorted output needed to change. Test: `collect_descriptors_collision_tie_break_follows_registration_order_not_alphabetical` (registers `start_invoice` first, `invoice_status` second -- the reverse of the existing test -- and asserts `start_invoice` wins). **Post-review hardening, round 12 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P2): round 11's own fix needed a second pass.** Round 11 fixed the collision tie-break by ordering candidates by first-mention index in the raw `workflows` slice, but that is subtly wrong whenever a name is registered more than once: a name's first mention can be a registration later *superseded* by `effective`'s last-wins resolution, so ordering by first-mention can still let a superseded, irrelevant early registration's position win a collision over a different workflow's only (and thus effective) registration that came later in wall-clock registration order but earlier among registrations that actually matter. Concretely: `[invoice_status(non-mcp), start_invoice(mcp), invoice_status(mcp)]` -- `invoice_status`'s *effective* registration is the third entry, entirely superseding the first (non-mcp) one, so `start_invoice`'s only registration (the second entry) is effectively the earlier of the two registrations that matter; ordering by first-mention (round 11's fix) would still process `invoice_status` first anyway, using its long-dead first mention's index, letting it incorrectly win the `start_invoice_status` collision. Fixed by tracking `effective_index: HashMap<&str, usize>` in lockstep with `effective` (same insert-per-iteration loop, same last-wins overwrite) and ordering the collision loop by *that* index instead of first-mention -- the index of the registration `effective` actually resolved to, which is exactly the one the runtime would use. This coincides with round 11's fix whenever no name repeats (the common case), so round 11's own regression test still passes unchanged. Test: `collect_descriptors_collision_order_uses_effective_registration_not_first_mention` (the exact three-entry scenario above, asserting `start_invoice` still wins). **Post-review hardening, round 13 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P2): cross-app tool-name collisions, documented rather than code-fixed.** A finding noted that Harvest's own operation-id collision check (rounds 5/11/12) only compares Harvest-generated names against each other, so a generated tool name colliding with an unrelated MCP tool the host app registers itself could still be silently dropped. Investigated against the vendored `autumn-web` 0.5.0 source: (1) confirmed `AppBuilder` exposes no accessor to introspect already- or later-registered routes' operation ids, so there is no way for `HarvestPlugin::build()` to see the app's other MCP tool names at the time it generates its own routes -- this cross-cutting collision is not mechanically fixable from inside this plugin, the same class of limitation as the `secure_mcp`-detection gap (round 3). (2) Confirmed the practical consequence is milder than "silently dropped": `autumn_web::mcp::derive_tools` (the final assembly point for the *whole* app's route set into the MCP tool catalog) already logs its own `tracing::warn!` on any duplicate operation id -- Harvest-vs-Harvest or Harvest-vs-host-app alike -- and keeps the first-registered one; a cross-app collision falls through to that existing, non-silent fallback instead of Harvest's own stricter, whole-workflow-exclusion check. (3) Also caught and fixed a pre-existing doc inaccuracy while investigating: `docs/mcp-tools.md` previously described autumn-web's tool derivation as keeping the first registration "silently" on a collision, which the source doesn't support (`derive_tools` warns). Fixed by expanding the collision paragraph in `docs/mcp-tools.md` to (a) correct the "silently" claim, (b) explain the scope limit of Harvest's own check, and (c) point authors at checking their app's full MCP route list before enabling `mcp_tools()`. No code change -- this finding's underlying risk was already covered by an existing, non-silent autumn-web safety net; only the documentation needed correcting. **Post-review hardening, round 14 (PR #908 automated review, Codex, P2): update-validator check could be silently skipped during plugin startup.** `Plugin::build`'s `start_harvest_runtime` calls `api_state.install_storage_pool(...)` (plugin.rs:677) before a genuine `.await` -- the boot-time admission-gate load -- that yields control to the async runtime, and only calls `api_state.install(runtime)` afterward (plugin.rs:754). A concurrent request landing in that window could reach `admit_update` with a working storage pool (`db_conn_for_execution` only needs `storage_pool()`) while `api_state.runtime()` still returned `Err`. The round-4 validator check used `if let Ok(runtime) = api_state.runtime() && ...` inside one combined `&&`-chain, so a runtime-`Err` silently short-circuited the whole condition to `false` and fell through to `store::admit_update_event` unconditionally -- a validator-bearing update could be durably admitted, unvalidated, in that startup window. Fixed by requiring `api_state.runtime()` unconditionally and failing closed (`map_error(e).into_response()`) before any validator lookup, exactly mirroring `update_with_start_workflow`'s existing hard `let runtime = match api_state.runtime() { Ok(r) => r, Err(e) => return map_error(e).into_response() };` pattern -- the reference implementation round 4's fix was supposed to mirror in the first place. `db_conn_for_execution`/`db_conn_for_shard` were confirmed (by reading their bodies) to depend only on `storage_pool()`, never `runtime()`, so this is a new hard requirement specific to `admit_update`, not a change to those shared helpers. Since `admit_update` is the shared handler for both the classic `POST /workflows/{id}/update/{name}` route and the MCP `{wf}_update_{name}` tool, both surfaces are fixed together. Test: `admit_update_fails_closed_when_storage_pool_ready_but_runtime_not_installed` (a new `api.rs`-internal testcontainers unit test, following the file's existing `workflow_result_immediate_snapshot_...` pattern) constructs `HarvestApiState::new()`, calls `install_storage_pool` only -- deliberately never `install(runtime)` -- and asserts `admit_update` returns `400` with the `"harvest runtime is not started"` message and that no `UpdateAdmitted` event was appended to history; compile-checked per the #543/#544 sandbox precedent (no Docker available here).
- **Phase 3.45** (implemented): Non-terminal blocking on replay non-determinism — see issue #603. **Behavior change (documented contract note):** an engine-detected replay divergence (`HarvestError::NonDeterministic`) on an in-flight execution no longer writes a terminal `WorkflowFailed` event or transitions the run to `FAILED` — the exact Temporal "a workflow-task failure must never fail the workflow" property. Instead an **early gate** in `worker::process_workflow_task` (placed right after the fast-path PAUSED check, *before* the cascade count, history-cap fail, terminal metrics, and — critically — the pre-terminal event appends in `persist_terminal_outcome_commands`, whose markers/update-results from a divergent cycle would poison history against the rolled-back build) diverts `WorkflowOutcome::Failed { non_deterministic_details: Some(_) }` to the new `block_workflow_for_non_determinism`: one row-locked transaction (mirroring the pause-guarded persist txn; an operator pause observed under the lock supersedes the block and re-parks instead) that stamps three new nullable/additive columns on `harvest_workflow_executions` (`nd_blocked_at`, `nd_block_reason`, `nd_block_count`), stamps the structured diagnostic into `search_attrs` via the extracted-and-reused `nd_search_attrs_patch` (`failure_cause=non_determinism`/`expected`/`actual`/`event_index`/`workflow_type`/`build_id` — same keys the #480 terminal path stamped), and re-pends the workflow task with `scheduled_at = NOW() + nd_block_backoff(count)` via the new Diesel-DSL `queue::requeue_workflow_task_nd_blocked` (also unpins sticky affinity — the pinned worker runs the divergent build — and clears `wake_requested` so a captured wake can't short-circuit the backoff). **Zero events appended, all pending commands discarded, no `fail_task`, no parent-close cascade, no completion triggers, no parent wake** — root and child executions block uniformly; a blocked child's parent simply stays suspended and resumes when the child completes post-rollback. State stays `RUNNING`, so cancel/terminate/pause escape hatches and the #243 execution-timeout scanner compose unchanged, and **no claim-side change** is needed (deferral falls out of `claim_task`'s existing `scheduled_at <= NOW()` predicate; signals arriving while blocked are durable and processed on the next backoff dispatch, ≤ one backoff period late). **AC3 crux (engine ND vs author `Err`) needed no new plumbing** — `ctx.nd_details` (populated only by the engine's `nd_error`/`record_deferred_nd` factories) already threads `NonDeterministicDetails` through `WorkflowOutcome::Failed`; an author `Err(...)` carries `None` and still fails terminally byte-for-byte as before (pinned by new executor tests `author_error_produces_failed_without_nd_details`/`engine_divergence_produces_failed_with_nd_details`). **Backoff bound (AC4):** capped exponential `5s * 2^count` → **300s ceiling**, retried **indefinitely** (rate-limited, not attempt-capped — a rollback at any later time resumes the run; constants `ND_BLOCK_BACKOFF_{BASE,CAP}_SECS`, pure fn `nd_block_backoff`); `crash_strikes` is reset by the requeue so poison-pill (#367) never trips, and workflow tasks have no attempt ceiling (verified: task `attempt` is only consumed by activity retry logic). **Recovery clears atomically:** a clean cycle for a previously-blocked execution runs `clear_nd_block` inside the pause-guarded persist transaction (guarded on the claim-time snapshot, zero cost for never-blocked rows) — columns → NULL/0 and the six diagnostic keys deleted from `search_attrs` (`nd_search_attrs_clear_patch`, key-symmetry unit-tested); belt-and-braces column resets were also added to `update_workflow_execution_{failed,completed}` for terminal paths that bypass that transaction (history-cap/dispatch-error). Workflow-level retry (#523) interplay unchanged (`nd_details.is_none()` guard stays; block never increments `workflow_attempt`); `schedule_counter_action` gains a defensive `Failed{Some(_)} => None` arm (a block is neither schedule success nor failure). **Operator surface (AC5):** `GET /workflows?nd_blocked=true` (`WorkflowFilters.nd_blocked`, applied on both the standard and stalled loaders as `nd_blocked_at IS NOT NULL AND state = 'RUNNING'` — plain column predicates, cross-shard safe; the state predicate hides stale markers left by cancel/terminate, which intentionally don't clear them); the three columns + `search_attrs` diagnostic surface automatically on list rows and `WorkflowDetailsResponse.execution`. **Metric (AC6):** `harvest.workflow.nondeterministic_block{workflow, queue}` (`METRIC_WORKFLOW_ND_BLOCKED`, no-op `MetricsRecorder` default + `metrics_rs_adapter` bridge), emitted post-commit on **every** block entry; the #480 detection counter `harvest.workflow.non_determinism` still fires alongside (emitted by the block path; the original terminal-metrics arm keeps a defensive copy that is unreachable post-gate), and `harvest.workflow.terminal{outcome=failed}` is **not** emitted for a block. **Append-only invariant preserved (AC7):** no new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no spurious terminal event; one additive migration `20260704000000_harvest_workflow_nd_block` (three columns + partial discovery index `WHERE nd_blocked_at IS NOT NULL`); all 66 hand-rolled test INIT_SQL bundles updated (scripted, #601 precedent). **Known limitation (documented in the runbook):** if the divergent build's cycle appended inline events (local-activity/external-signal batch) *before* diverging, post-rollback replay can re-diverge against them and stay blocked — escalation is reset-from-history (#148); the common deploy case diverges in the prefix before any append. Docs: runbook `docs/runbooks/nondeterminism-block.md` (detect → identify build → roll back → confirm resume → escalation; AC8), `harvest_workflow_non_determinism` alert updated in `docs/alerts/starter-pack-v0.1.0.json` (block semantics, `?nd_blocked=true` first-action, second PromQL on the new counter) + `docs/runbooks/harvest-alerts.md` section rewritten, `nd_blocked` param registered in `docs/api-contract.json`, metric added to `docs/telemetry.md`. Tests (TDD red→green→refactor; runnable red phases confirmed via compile errors per the #593 precedent): worker.rs unit tests (`nd_block_backoff` growth/cap/saturation; `nd_search_attrs_patch` full/sparse; clear-patch key symmetry), telemetry/adapter const+no-op+bridge tests, executor AC3 pins, plugin `parse_workflow_filters_parses_nd_blocked_flag`; DB integration `tests/nd_block_tests.rs` (5 scenarios: block-not-fail with diagnostic/backoff/sticky assertions, **rollback-resume money test** with original-output + marker-cleared assertions, author-Err-still-FAILED, blocked-child-never-notifies-parent, re-block count/backoff growth — each with RecordingMetrics counter assertions) and `workflow_filter_integration.rs::workflow_list_filter_nd_blocked` (incl. stale-marker-on-CANCELLED exclusion) — DB/testcontainers tests are compile-checked (`cargo check --all-features --tests`) but not executed in this sandbox (no Docker), matching the #543/#544/#601 precedent. **Post-review hardening (8-angle code review, applied after initial ship):** the "belt-and-braces column resets... cover the bypassing paths" claim above was accurate for the three `nd_block_*` columns but **not** for `search_attrs` — `update_workflow_execution_completed` reset the columns but left stale ND diagnostic keys in `search_attrs`, and `update_workflow_execution_failed` only *stamped* a fresh diagnostic when `nd_details.is_some()`, never *cleared* a stale one when `nd_details.is_none()`; both now also call `store::update_search_attrs(..., &nd_search_attrs_clear_patch())` so all three terminal-writer paths (`_completed`/`_failed`/`_timed_out`) are symmetric with the transactional `clear_nd_block` path. Eight further gaps closed: (1) `requeue_workflow_task_nd_blocked` now also clears `activity_name`, closing a bypass where a stale `mixed_signal_suspension` sentinel let a blocked execution's re-pend skip the ND backoff entirely; (2) the six ND diagnostic `search_attrs` keys (`failure_cause`/`event_index`/`expected`/`actual`/`workflow_type`/`build_id`) were added to `RESERVED_SEARCH_ATTR_KEYS` (`context.rs`), closing a collision where a workflow author's own business attribute of the same name would be silently clobbered by the next ND stamp/clear; (3) `continue_as_new` no longer inherits a predecessor's stale ND-block columns/diagnostic onto the successor — the successor is mutated pre-transaction (capturing a `was_nd_blocked` guard bool before mutating, mirroring the codebase's existing pre-transaction `pending_cmds` search-attrs-patch pattern) via a new `apply_raw_search_attrs_patch_in_memory` in-memory-parity helper; (4) reset-fork executions (`reset.rs::insert_fork_execution`) strip the same six keys unconditionally, closing the identical stale-diagnostic-inheritance gap on the reset path; (5) the PAUSED-race branch in `block_workflow_for_non_determinism` now always emits `record_workflow_non_determinism` telemetry (previously skipped entirely when superseded by a concurrent pause, undercounting detections — mirrors the pre-existing "speculative-then-guarded" telemetry pattern already used by the terminal-metrics block); (6) `update_workflow_execution_timed_out` (`timeout.rs`) now also resets the three `nd_block_*` columns and clears the `search_attrs` diagnostic, closing the fourth (TIMED_OUT) terminal-writer gap; (7) `debug_assert!`s added to the two "never reached" defensive match arms (`schedule_counter_action`'s `Failed{Some(_)}` arm, the terminal-metrics block's equivalent) documenting the invariant they encode, matching the codebase's existing defensive-`debug_assert!` idiom; (8) a shared `check_paused_and_park` helper deduplicates the byte-identical PAUSED-check-and-park sequence between the two call sites, `requeue_for_retry`/`requeue_workflow_task_nd_blocked` now share a `PendingRequeueChangeset` `AsChangeset` derive struct for their common SET fields, and `nd_block_backoff` is reimplemented as a thin wrapper over `policy::compute_retry_delay`. **Known test-coverage gaps** (both are best-effort telemetry/cleanup paths, not correctness-critical, and reproducing the exact race/bypass conditions deterministically was judged not worth the complexity): the PAUSED-race telemetry branch in fix (5) and the history-cap/dispatch-error bypass paths in the `search_attrs`-clearing fix above have no dedicated DB regression test — covered indirectly by the existing pure-function unit tests for `nd_search_attrs_clear_patch` and `check_paused_and_park`'s extraction, but not by an end-to-end race reproduction. **Second round of PR-review hardening (Gemini + Codex bot reviews on PR #907), plus a CI-caught gap:** (9) `PendingRequeueChangeset` (item 8 above) was missing `treat_none_as_null = true` on its `#[diesel(table_name = ...)]` attribute — Diesel's default `AsChangeset` behavior treats a `None` field as "omit this column from `SET`" rather than "set it to `NULL`", which silently regressed the pre-refactor code's explicit `.eq(None::<T>)` calls (those bypass the default because they're expression-level, not derive-level): `worker_id`/`started_at`/`last_heartbeat_at` were never actually cleared on a requeue or ND-block re-pend, confirmed via a red→green `debug_query`-based unit test (`queue::tests::pending_requeue_changeset_nulls_out_none_fields_in_generated_sql`) that fails without the attribute and passes with it. (10) The three belt-and-braces `search_attrs` clears added in gap (6) above ran **unconditionally** on every terminal transition, not just ones that were actually ND-blocked — on a deployment upgrading from before this migration, a workflow's pre-existing legitimate business `search_attrs` under one of the six now-reserved names (e.g. `build_id`) would be silently deleted the moment that execution completed, even though it was never ND-blocked (the reserved-key validator added in gap (2) only protects *future* writes, not rows already sitting in the database). Fixed by gating all three call sites (`update_workflow_execution_completed`, `update_workflow_execution_failed`'s `None` arm, `update_workflow_execution_timed_out`) on a pre-update `nd_blocked_at IS NOT NULL` read captured before the terminal `UPDATE` runs — a best-effort, unlocked read (a race against a concurrent (re-)block is harmless: the block path stamps its own diagnostic independently of this check, and the primary `clear_nd_block` transactional path, which is unaffected by this gating, already handles the common recovery case). Regression test `nd_block_tests.rs::completion_never_touches_search_attrs_on_a_row_that_was_never_nd_blocked` seeds a legacy `build_id` business attribute via raw SQL (bypassing the validator, simulating a pre-migration row) and asserts it survives an ordinary completion. (11) CI caught a real gap the "all 66 hand-rolled test INIT_SQL bundles updated" claim in the original entry above missed: `integration_e2e.rs`'s `LEGACY_INIT_SQL` (a hand-maintained, deliberately-partial `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` list mirroring exactly the columns `start_or_load_workflow_execution` touches, used only by `legacy_workflow_uniqueness_schema_can_be_upgraded_for_idempotent_starts` to drive a real historical-upgrade scenario) was not updated with the three new `nd_block_*` columns, so that test failed in CI with `column harvest_workflow_executions.nd_blocked_at does not exist`. Fixed by appending the three columns to `LEGACY_INIT_SQL` following the file's existing pattern. (Two other CI failures in the same run — `worker_completes_parent_workflow_with_child_fan_out` and `worker_completes_ten_child_fan_out_within_wall_clock_bound` — are the pre-existing, extensively-documented issue #601 wall-clock flake described in the Phase 3.44 entry below; unrelated to this PR's changes and out of scope here.)
- **Phase 3.46** (implemented): Durable completion callbacks — push terminal results to a URL (issue #605). An embedder starting a workflow from their Autumn app has no push notification when it finishes today — only polling `GET /workflows/{id}/result` (issue #527) or a long-poll. This feature lets an embedder register a **completion callback target** (URL + terminal-state `EventFilter`) either as a per-execution start option (`StartWorkflowParams.completion_callbacks: Option<serde_json::Value>`, threaded through the ~95 `StartWorkflowParams`/`NewWorkflowExecution` construction sites workspace-wide) or a `HarvestBuilder`-wide default (`completion_callback_default`/`_allowlist`/`_allow_http`/`_allow_ip_literals`/`_secret`/`_retry_policy`/`_deliverer`), and Harvest durably POSTs a fixed, HMAC-signed JSON envelope (`{delivery_id, execution_id, workflow_name, workflow_id, state, result?, error?, completed_at}`) the moment the workflow reaches a terminal state. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, zero replay-determinism impact**: registration is execution metadata (`completion_callbacks jsonb NULL` on `harvest_workflow_executions`), delivery is a post-terminal operational side-channel through its own table, `harvest_events` is untouched, and a callback-less workflow behaves byte-for-byte identically to before. New core module `completion_callback.rs`: pure SSRF validator (`validate_target_url`/`SsrfPolicy`/`HostAllowlist` — HTTPS-only + allowlist-required by default, exact-host and `*.suffix` wildcard matching, IP-literal loopback/private/link-local/CGNAT/ULA blocking, DNS intentionally not resolved to avoid a TOCTOU trap, machine-readable `#[serde(tag="code")]` `SsrfRejection`), HMAC-SHA256 envelope signing (`X-Harvest-Signature: sha256=<hex>` + `X-Harvest-Timestamp`, secret never persisted, `Debug`-redacting `CallbackSecret` newtype), `EventFilter` (`CompletedOnly`/`AnyTerminal`/`States([...])`) + `resolve_all_targets`/`resolve_effective_targets` (per-exec-first-then-builder-defaults union, deduped by `(url, filter)`, stable `callback_index` per target), a boxed-future `CompletionCallbackDeliverer` trait (core ships no HTTP client, mirroring `PayloadStore`/`HistoryArchiver`), `classify_outcome` (pure retry/backoff/dead-letter decision), and a `GLOBAL_CALLBACK_CONFIG: RwLock<Option<CallbackRuntimeConfig>>` static (mirrors `GLOBAL_WORKFLOW_METADATA`) set at plugin/runner startup. **Enqueue is folded into `evaluate_triggers_for_execution`** (`completion_trigger.rs`) — the one function already called inside the transaction at all ~15 terminal call sites (Completed/Failed/Cancelled/Terminated/TimedOut; never `ContinuedAsNew`) — so covering every terminal path required zero new terminal-site edits; `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (workflow_exec_id, callback_index) DO NOTHING` makes re-entry (e.g. the parent-close cascade re-evaluating triggers) idempotent. New table `harvest_completion_deliveries` (migration `20260705000000_harvest_completion_deliveries`; frozen `payload jsonb` envelope snapshot immune to later row mutation/retention, frozen `retry_policy jsonb`, `UNIQUE(workflow_exec_id, callback_index)`, partial index on `(shard_id, next_attempt_at) WHERE state IN ('PENDING','INFLIGHT')`). Scanner `fire_due_completion_deliveries` is folded into `timeout::enforce_timeouts_once` (no new spawn, no new poll interval) and is **two-transaction by construction** (never holds a row lock across network I/O): tx#1 claims + marks `INFLIGHT` (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, short in-flight lease), the POST happens unlocked, tx#2 records the outcome (delivered / backoff-requeue / dead-letter via the new `DeadLetterReason::CallbackDeliveryExhausted { delivery_id, attempts, last_status, target }`, distinct from `PoisonPill`/clean task-retry exhaustion). Default retry policy: 10 attempts, 30s initial, ×2 backoff, 600s cap, frozen into the delivery row at enqueue time. Plugin (`autumn-harvest-plugin`) ships `ReqwestCallbackDeliverer` (`callback_deliverer.rs`) — `redirect::Policy::none()` since an allowlisted host could otherwise 302 to an internal address, bypassing the SSRF guard — auto-wired by `HarvestPlugin`/`HarvestRunner`'s `PreparedHarvestRuntime::build()` (covers both the web-app and standalone-runner paths in one place), overridable via `completion_callback_deliverer(...)`. Management API: `GET /workflows/{id}/completion-deliveries` (read-only, no audit, parity with `GET /dead-letters`) and `POST /workflows/{id}/completion-deliveries/{delivery_id}/redrive` (admin-guarded, audited under `OP_CALLBACK_REDRIVE`/`TARGET_CALLBACK_DELIVERY`; self-contained — resets only the delivery row to `PENDING` with the *same* `delivery_id` so a receiver's idempotency key still matches, and clears any `harvest_dead_letters` row; idempotent-shaped, a non-`FAILED` delivery returns `200 {outcome: "not_failed"}` rather than erroring). Both routes registered in all four parallel registries (`harvest_api_router`, `management_api_routes`, `management_api_request_fields`, `management_api_response_fields`) and `docs/api-contract.json`. HTTP start route (`api.rs`) validates each per-execution target against the live SSRF policy and rejects the start with `422` on a non-allowlisted URL; a builder-default target that fails validation fails `try_build()` with `HarvestBuilderError::CallbackTargetRejected`. CLI: `harvest completion-delivery list <execution_id> [--state ...]` / `harvest completion-delivery redrive <execution_id> <delivery_id>` (aliases `completion-deliveries`, `callbacks`; `--state` filtered client-side, since the per-execution row count is always small). `completion_callbacks` propagation across the engine: inherited (not dropped) on workflow-level retry (#523), continue-as-new, and reset forks (`reset.rs`) — all three are "the same logical run" continuing; `None` for children/detached children (which get builder-defaults only, matching the concurrency-key/SLA precedent for internal start sites). Docs: `docs/completion-callbacks.md` (envelope schema, signing scheme, SSRF allowlist semantics, at-least-once + `delivery_id` idempotency contract, retry/backoff/DLQ, management + CLI surfaces, explicit no-`WorkflowEvent`-variant/zero-replay-impact statement). Tests, TDD red→green→refactor: ~49 pure unit tests in `completion_callback.rs` (SSRF validator, HMAC/envelope round-trip, event-filter/config resolution, outcome classification, builder-config validation) plus builder/DLQ/CLI unit tests; `tests/completion_callback_tests.rs` (testcontainers-shaped, `TEST_SERIAL` mutex guard around the process-wide `GLOBAL_CALLBACK_CONFIG` static: enqueue-on-terminal creates one row per matching target, idempotent re-evaluation, filter-mismatch → zero rows, no-targets-configured → zero rows, scanner 200→DELIVERED / non-2xx→backoff-requeue / exhaustion→FAILED+DLQ, scanner skips not-yet-due rows, list ordering, redrive resets a FAILED row and clears its DLQ entry, redrive no-ops on an unknown/non-FAILED id) — compile-checked per the #543/#544/#601 precedent (no Docker in this sandbox), but the M9 management-API routes were additionally verified against a real local Postgres instance in this sandbox (migrations applied directly, full enqueue → list → redrive(202) → re-redrive(200 not_failed) → redrive-unknown(404) round trip confirmed end-to-end through the actual HTTP handlers before the scratch verification code was deleted). **Post-review hardening (PR #921, Codex P2):** `GET /workflows/{id}/completion-deliveries` was registered without the `require_admin` route layer the redrive endpoint and `GET /dead-letters` both carry, so a non-admin caller who could reach workflow-scoped routes and knew an execution id could enumerate callback receiver URLs plus last-attempt status/error text — operational endpoint details otherwise kept behind the admin DLQ surface. Fixed by adding the same `.route_layer(require_admin.clone())` used by every other admin-only management route.
- **Phase 3.46** (implemented): Inbound HTTP webhook receiver primitive — see issue #344. Redirected from the issue's original design (a bespoke `WebhookVerifier` trait + HMAC/replay code inside Harvest) to mirror the existing outbound-webhook extension slice and the `#[workflow(mcp)]` MCP tool slice (issue #597): autumn-web 0.5 already owns first-class inbound webhook verification (`[security.webhooks.endpoints]`, `autumn_web::webhook::SignedWebhook` — signature, timestamp tolerance, replay-duplicate rejection, secret rotation, boot-time fail-fast on a missing/weak secret), so Harvest's slice sits entirely downstream of that and only answers "which workflow does this already-verified delivery trigger, and how do we dispatch it idempotently?" New core module `webhook_trigger.rs` (unconditional, not `db`-gated — the macro's companion function must always compile): `WebhookCtx` (verified metadata: `path`/`endpoint`/`provider`/`delivery_id`/`event_type`/`raw_body`), `WebhookHandlerError` (`Deserialize`/`Rejected`), `WebhookHandlerFn` (a plain `fn` pointer mirroring `WorkflowHandlerFn`'s design decision #6), `WebhookTarget::{Starts{workflow}, SignalsWithStart{workflow, signal_name}}`, `WebhookTriggerInfo`, pure `validate_webhook_triggers` (duplicate/malformed-path rejection, run at `HarvestPlugin::build` time). New `#[webhook(path = "…", starts = "…" | signals = "…" + signal_name = "…", queue = "…")]` macro in `autumn-harvest-macros` (mirrors `workflow.rs`'s companion-function + public-alias pattern) generates `__autumn_webhook_info_{fn}()` + `{fn}_info()`; the mapping function is **synchronous**, takes `ctx: &WebhookCtx` + exactly one typed payload parameter, and returns `Result<WorkflowId, E>` — the shim deserializes the verified JSON body into the typed parameter and classifies deserialize vs. user-rejection failures. `verifier =`/`idempotency_header =` attributes from the original issue spec are rejected at compile time with a pointer to `[security.webhooks]` (autumn-web owns that now). `webhooks![…]` collector mirrors `workflows!`/`activities!`. New plugin module `autumn-harvest-plugin/src/webhook_receiver.rs` behind the existing `webhooks` cargo feature: `HarvestPlugin::webhooks(Vec<WebhookTriggerInfo>)` builder method; `Plugin::build` generates one app-level `autumn_web::Route` per binding (mirroring `mcp_tools.rs`'s route-generation template — **not** wrapped in the `api_with_auth` middleware, since the HMAC signature *is* the auth for these routes) and **fails fast** (panics) on a duplicate binding path or a trigger targeting a workflow not registered via `.workflows(...)`. Handler control flow: check the `Result<SignedWebhook, AutumnError>` extraction result **before** the `api_state.runtime()` fail-closed check (a sender's bad signature must be reported 401/400 even during the boot window, not masked as "runtime not started" — caught by the no-DB HTTP test suite, which is why this ordering is called out explicitly) → parse JSON body (400 `parse_failed`) → for a `SignalsWithStart` target, require a resolved delivery id (400 `missing_idempotency`; a `Starts` target needs none — its own returned `WorkflowId` is the idempotency mechanism) → run the mapping shim (400 `parse_failed`/`mapping_rejected`) → dispatch by **delegating to the existing `start_workflow`/`signal_with_start_workflow` HTTP handlers** (bumped to `pub(crate)`, plus new `StartWorkflowRequest::from_webhook`/`SignalWithStartRequest::from_webhook` constructors mirroring the MCP tool layer's `from_input` precedent) so schema validation, the admission gate, debounce/batch/SLA/execution-timeout resolution, and audit apply identically to a webhook-triggered start — then reshapes the delegate's `201`/`200` response into `{"status": "accepted"|"idempotent_replay", "workflow_exec_id", "workflow_id"}` (`202`/`200`). New metrics `harvest.webhook.received`/`harvest.webhook.rejected` (`METRIC_WEBHOOK_RECEIVED`/`METRIC_WEBHOOK_REJECTED`, bounded `path`/`outcome` labels via the new `WebhookOutcome` enum; `rejected` never fires for `accepted`/`idempotent_replay`) and audit operation `webhook.trigger` (`OP_WEBHOOK_TRIGGER`, written only for dispatch attempts that pass verification — an unauthenticated sender cannot generate audit rows). **No `CLASSIFIED_ROUTES`/`ALL_MUTATION_ROUTES`/`docs/api-contract.json` entry**: webhook binding paths are user-defined, app-level routes outside `harvest_api_router`, invisible to (and correctly excluded from) those management-router manifests, same treatment as the MCP tool routes. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no shard-routing change.** Doc chapter `docs/getting-started/12-webhooks.md`; example `autumn-harvest-plugin/examples/webhook_receiver_quickstart.rs` (`[[example]] required-features = ["webhooks"]`). Tests, TDD red→green→refactor: core `tests/webhook_trigger_tests.rs` (13 tests, no-DB); macro `tests/macros_webhook.rs` (7 expansion tests) + 6 trybuild compile-fail fixtures (`tests/compile_fail/webhook_*.rs` with committed `.stderr` snapshots) registered in `macros_compile_fail.rs`; plugin no-DB HTTP tests `tests/webhook_receiver_http_tests.rs` (7 tests against a real `TestApp` + genuinely HMAC-signed requests through the actual `SignedWebhook` extractor — this suite is what caught the runtime-check-ordering bug above) plus 4 inline `webhook_receiver.rs` unit tests for the outcome-classification helpers; testcontainers integration `tests/webhook_receiver_integration.rs` (2 scenarios: the issue's literal AC — two identical signed deliveries → first `202`, second `200`, same `workflow_exec_id`, exactly one execution row, one audit row — and the `SignalsWithStart` dedupe variant; compile-checked in this sandbox via `cargo test --no-run`, no Docker available, matching the #543/#544/#601 precedent). **Post-review hardening (Codex review, PR #918):** `signal_with_start`'s dedupe is scoped to `(workflow_name, workflow_id, idempotency_key)` only — it has no notion of which webhook endpoint or `signal_name` produced the key — so passing a `SignalsWithStart` binding's raw provider delivery id straight through as the idempotency key let two *different* `#[webhook(signals = ...)]` bindings that both target the same `(workflow_name, workflow_id)` pair (an entity workflow fed by more than one provider/endpoint) collide whenever their raw delivery ids happened to match (plausible for naive/generic senders using small sequential ids, or the same event redelivered to two configured paths), silently dropping the second binding's signal as a misdiagnosed idempotent replay of the first. Fixed by namespacing the key with stable webhook-binding metadata before dispatch: `{path}:{signal_name}:{delivery_id}` (`handle_webhook` in `webhook_receiver.rs`) — `path` alone already disambiguates every binding (`validate_webhook_triggers` forbids duplicate paths), `signal_name` is included for defense-in-depth. A same-binding redelivery of the same event still maps to the same key, so exactly-once dedup for the common case (and the audit row's `idempotency_key`, which intentionally still records the raw provider delivery id for operator correlation with the provider's own dashboard) is unchanged. **Second finding, same review round:** autumn-web's JSON-body `"id"` fallback (`resolve_delivery_id`/`json_string_field`) has no non-empty guard the way its header-based lookup does, so a naive/generic sender's `{"id": ""}` payload resolved `Some("")` rather than `None` -- the pre-existing `ctx.delivery_id.is_none()` missing-idempotency guard let it through, and every such blank-id delivery collided on the identical namespaced key `{path}:{signal_name}:`, silently dropping all but the first unrelated event for a `SignalsWithStart` target. Fixed with a pure `normalize_delivery_id(raw: Option<&str>) -> Option<String>` helper (`webhook_receiver.rs`) that treats a blank/whitespace-only resolved id as missing (a genuine non-blank id is passed through untrimmed) before `WebhookCtx` is even constructed, so the existing `missing_idempotency` guard now catches it without any change to the guard itself. Tests: 3 pure unit tests for `normalize_delivery_id`, plus `blank_delivery_id_is_rejected_as_missing_for_signals_target` in `webhook_receiver_integration.rs` (testcontainers, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent as the rest of this suite). **Third finding, same review round (architectural — fixed in the shared primitive, not just the webhook layer):** a `SignalsWithStart` webhook copies the verified event body into both `signal_payload` *and* `start_input` (there is no other candidate input for a fresh start triggered by a signal-only webhook), and the pre-existing `signal_with_start_workflow` HTTP handler validated `start_input` against the target workflow's published schema (issue #373) **unconditionally**, before the core primitive's lock even ran — deliberately, to close a TOCTOU window (the handler's pre-scan for an existing execution is unlocked, so the observed execution could complete before the core resolver's `FOR UPDATE` lock, escalating `AllowDuplicate` to a fresh start that writes `start_input` without ever having validated it). The unconditional check closed that race at the cost of a real, broader bug: it also rejected a **pure attach** to an already-running execution whenever the signal payload didn't match the workflow's *start*-input schema, even though `start_input` is never written on an attach — spuriously 400-ing every subsequent signal to a schema-enabled workflow after its first. Not webhook-specific: any direct caller of `POST /workflows/{name}/signal-with-start` hit the identical behavior. Fixed at the root: `signal_with_start_workflow_execution`'s `SignalWithStartParams` gained a `workflow_info: Option<&WorkflowInfo>` field (mirroring the existing issue #252 payload-cap deferral pattern — same `if started.created { ... }` / `if fresh.created { ... }` branches, both already the exact fresh-start checkpoints under the lock); a new pure `check_sws_input_schema` helper calls `WorkflowInfo::validate_input` there and maps a violation to the new `HarvestError::InputValidationFailed { violations }` variant, propagated as `400 { "error": "input validation failed", "violations": [...] }` (identical shape to the old response) by a new match arm in the HTTP handler. The unconditional pre-lock check in `api.rs` was deleted entirely — validation now runs exactly once, inside the lock, only when `started.created`/`fresh.created` is true, eliminating the TOCTOU window by construction rather than working around it (a schema-invalid *escalated* fresh start is now caught by the same in-lock check, since it hits the identical `fresh.created` branch). The three other `SignalWithStartParams` construction sites (the typed client-stub macro, `signal_with_start_tests.rs`, the `signal_with_start_webhook.rs` example) all pass `workflow_info: None` — schema validation stays an HTTP-JSON-boundary-only concern by design (a typed Rust stub's `input: I` is already checked by the compiler; every pre-existing `validate_input` call site was already HTTP-handler-only). Tests: `signal_with_start_does_not_validate_start_input_schema_when_attaching_to_a_running_execution` and `signal_with_start_validates_start_input_schema_on_a_genuine_fresh_start` (both new, `signal_with_start_tests.rs`, testcontainers, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent). **Fourth and fifth findings, same review round:** (a) `HarvestPlugin::webhooks(...)` assigned `self.webhook_triggers = triggers` instead of extending it, unlike the adjacent `.workflows`/`.activities`/`.queries`/`.updates` methods, which all append — an app registering webhook bindings from separate modules via more than one `.webhooks(...)` call silently lost every earlier call's bindings, mounting only the last module's routes. Fixed to `self.webhook_triggers.extend(triggers)`. Test: `webhooks_accumulates_across_repeated_calls` (`plugin.rs`, no DB). (b) With the default `unified-dag-execution` feature, `.dags(...)` auto-registers a shadow `WorkflowInfo` for each unified DAG (issue #256), so `build_webhook_routes`'s workflow-name-only assertion accepted a `#[webhook(starts = "some_dag")]` target even though `start_workflow`/`signal_with_start_workflow` both reject a registered DAG (`runtime.is_registered_dag`) at request time — the app booted successfully with a webhook route that could never dispatch, only failing on the first live request instead of at `HarvestPlugin::build` time. Fixed by threading `HarvestBuilder::dag_infos()` into `build_webhook_routes` (new parameter, filtered to `workflow_handler.is_some()` — exactly the set `is_registered_dag` checks, matching `mcp_tools.rs::collect_descriptors`'s identical filter) and asserting a trigger's target isn't a registered DAG *before* the existing registered-workflow assertion (a classic DAG, with no shadow `WorkflowInfo`, was already caught by the pre-existing assertion on its own). Test: `trigger_targeting_a_registered_dag_panics_at_build_time` (`webhook_receiver_http_tests.rs`, no DB). **Sixth finding, same review round:** the boot-window `api_state.runtime()` failure ("harvest runtime is not started") was mapped via `map_error(e)` to a `400`, but when `[security.webhooks].replay_protection` is enabled the `SignedWebhook` extractor has already reserved that delivery's replay key before the handler runs, and autumn-web's `webhook_replay_cleanup_middleware` (wired automatically into every app's router) only releases a reserved key on a `5xx` response. A `400` here permanently consumed the delivery id on the very first attempt even though nothing was ever dispatched, so a provider's automatic retry arriving after the app finished booting would 409 as a false duplicate forever. Fixed by returning `503 Service Unavailable` directly for this one case in `handle_webhook` (not via the shared `map_error`, which many other routes rely on returning `400` for `HarvestError::Config` and must not change) — a transient boot-window condition is a `5xx`, not a bad request, and now correctly releases the reserved key so a genuine retry is re-evaluated rather than falsely rejected. The existing no-DB test asserting `400` for this path (and the two others whose assertions incidentally depended on it: the coexistence test and the replay-duplicate test) were updated to `503`; the replay-duplicate test was renamed and rewritten to assert what it can actually prove now that the bug is fixed -- both deliveries release their key and neither is ever misdiagnosed as a duplicate (a genuine-409-after-successful-processing scenario needs a live runtime and belongs to autumn-web's own test suite, not this no-DB harness).
- **Phase 3.46** (implemented): Expose unified DAGs as MCP tools — see issue #601 follow-up (completes the MCP tool surface issue #597/PR #908 deliberately left DAGs out of). A unified DAG (Phase 3.9, `unified-dag-execution`) already lowers onto a shadow `WorkflowInfo` registered in `registry.workflows` via `HarvestBuilder::dags()` -> `DagInfo::as_workflow_info()`; the only thing that kept it out of `mcp_tools::collect_descriptors`'s `.filter(|w| w.mcp)` scan was a hardcoded `mcp: false` on that lowered `WorkflowInfo`. New `#[dag(mcp)]` attribute (bare or `mcp = true|false`, parsed via the shared `attr_util::parse_bool_flag` helper — same idiom as `#[workflow(mcp)]`/`#[update(..., mcp)]`) and `DagInfo::with_mcp()` builder method opt a DAG into the same MCP tool machinery. **Two DAG-specific differences from an ordinary mcp workflow, both driven by a new `McpWorkflowDescriptor.is_dag` flag** (set in `collect_descriptors` from a `dag_names: &HashSet<&str>` parameter — `HarvestBuilder::dag_infos()`, a new pre-build accessor mirroring `workflow_infos()`/`update_handlers()`): (1) **`start_{dag}` preserves the DAG trigger contract** — a new `ToolKind::DagStart` routes the generated start tool through the existing `trigger_dag_run` HTTP handler (bumped `pub(crate)`, alongside `DagTriggerRequest` and its new `from_conf` constructor, and `StartWorkflowResponse`) rather than the generic `start_workflow` path, so DAG-specific admission gates (issue #377) and `max_active_runs`/paused enforcement (`trigger_unified_dag`) apply identically to an MCP-originated start — the generic path has no notion of either and would have silently let an agent spawn more concurrent DAG runs than the operator-configured policy allows. (2) **no `signal_{dag}` tool and no update tools** — the generated level-walking DAG handler never waits on or handles a signal and DAGs have no update handlers, so `tool_operation_ids`/`tool_route_specs` omit both for a DAG descriptor; a unified DAG surfaces exactly three tools: `start_{dag}`, `{dag}_status`, `{dag}_watch`. Status/watch need no DAG-specific handling — they already work by execution id regardless of whether the id names a plain workflow or a DAG run. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no replay surface** — entirely a macro-attribute, descriptor-selection, and route-generation change, mirroring #597's own determinism-neutral scope. CI: the plugin's `mcp`-feature clippy/test jobs now also enable `unified-dag-execution` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) so the DAG branch of `mcp_tools.rs` and its test fixtures are actually compiled and linted, not silently `cfg`'d out. Docs: `docs/mcp-tools.md` gains a "DAGs as MCP tools" section. Tests: macro-level `#[dag(mcp)]`/bare/`mcp = false` parsing and shadow-`WorkflowInfo` propagation in `autumn-harvest/tests/macros_dag.rs`; `DagInfo::with_mcp` and `as_workflow_info` mcp-propagation unit tests in `autumn-harvest/src/info.rs` (replacing a now-inverted `dag_as_workflow_info_is_never_mcp` test that encoded the old behavior); `collect_descriptors`/`tool_operation_ids`/`tool_route_specs` DAG-awareness unit tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/src/mcp_tools.rs`; a no-DB `tools/list` + `tools/call` HTTP test pair in `tests/mcp_tools_http_tests.rs` proving the three-tool set and DAG-trigger-pipeline dispatch; and a Docker-requiring end-to-end pair in `tests/mcp_tools_integration.rs` (a real DAG run driven through `start_{dag}` reaches `COMPLETED`, a concurrent second start is rejected citing `max_active_runs`, and the tool catalog has no `signal_{dag}`) — compile-checked in this sandbox per the #543/#544/#601 precedent (no Docker available here). **Post-review hardening (8-angle parallel code review, applied immediately after the initial ship):** (1) **Real correctness bug** — `collect_descriptors`'s `dag_names: &HashSet<&str>` parameter was built by the caller (`Plugin::build()`) from *every* registered `DagInfo`, including a classic (pre-unified) DAG whose `workflow_handler` is `None` and which therefore never produces a shadow `WorkflowInfo` and is never covered by `validate_dag_workflow_name_collisions` (which only cross-checks `auto_registered_dag_workflows`). An unrelated, independently-registered `#[workflow(mcp)]` workflow sharing a classic DAG's name would be silently mistagged `is_dag = true` — routing its start tool through the DAG trigger contract for a DAG it has nothing to do with, and dropping its legitimate signal tool. Fixed by changing `collect_descriptors`'s third parameter from `dag_names: &HashSet<&str>` to `dags: &[DagInfo]` and computing the `workflow_handler.is_some()`-filtered name set *inside* the function — the caller now just passes `builder.dag_infos()` directly, which also collapses `Plugin::build()`'s call site back to a single line and removes the now-unneeded `#[allow(clippy::implicit_hasher)]`. Regression test: `collect_descriptors_classic_dag_does_not_mistag_a_same_named_workflow`. (2) The audit record for a DAG trigger always attributed the call to the classic HTTP route string (`"POST /dags/{dag_name}/trigger"`), even when it came through the new `start_{dag}` MCP tool. Fixed by extracting `trigger_dag_run`'s body into `trigger_dag_run_inner` (`pub(crate)`, takes an explicit `route: &'static str`); the classic HTTP handler passes its own route string unchanged, `start_dag_tool` passes a distinct `"MCP tool: start_{dag}"` label. (3) `HarvestPlugin::mcp_tools()`'s doc comment was updated to mention DAG support and the reduced three-tool set, since it still read as workflow-only. (4) `tool_route_specs` simplified: the `start_summary_suffix` indirection (a separately-built string spliced into the description template) was replaced with two complete, directly-readable description strings, and the three separate `descriptor.is_dag` checks (start kind, description suffix, signal-spec inclusion) were consolidated into one `(start_kind, start_description, signal_spec)` tuple computed once, with `specs.extend(signal_spec)` replacing the conditional `push`. (5) **Unrelated pre-existing compile error, fixed while here** — `autumn-harvest/src/scheduler.rs`'s `classic_scheduled_dag_info()` test helper (`#[cfg(all(feature = "db", not(feature = "unified-dag-execution")))]`) was already missing `owner`/`runbook_url`/`severity` before this slice touched anything; adding the required `mcp` field would have been a fourth missing field. This combination was invisible to every CI job (none exercises `db` without `unified-dag-execution`), so it had silently rotted. Fixed by adding all four missing fields and adding a dedicated `Run classic (non-unified) DAG tests` CI step (`cargo test -p autumn-harvest --no-default-features --features db --lib`) so this combination is never silently broken again.
- **Phase 3.46** (implemented): Worker sessions -- co-locate an activity pipeline on one physical worker for machine-local state (a downloaded artifact, a warmed cache, GPU memory) -- see issue #606. `WorkflowContext::create_session(SessionOptions)` returns a `Session` handle; `Session::execute_activity`/`execute_activity_raw` hard-pin member activities to the acquiring worker for the session's life, and `Session::complete()` releases it. **Acquisition reuses the entire activity suspend/persist/replay machinery**: `create_session` records a deterministic session id via the existing `MarkerRecorded` mechanism (`session:{seq}`, sharing `WorkflowContext`'s fan-out/race sequence-counter idiom) and dispatches a reserved internal activity (`__harvest_session_acquire`, always registered in `HandlerRegistry` via `session_internal_activity_info` so the enqueue-time handler lookup never hard-fails on it) bounded by a per-call `schedule_to_start_override` (new field on `ScheduleActivity`/`ScheduledActivityCommand`, defaulting to `None` everywhere else). A worker claims that activity only when `try_acquire_session_slot` (a plain `Arc<AtomicI64>` counter bounded by `WorkerConfig::max_concurrent_sessions`, default `0` = disabled) succeeds; on success it durably records a `harvest_sessions` `ACTIVE` row (`host_worker_id`, `expires_at`) and completes with its own worker id as output -- the value every replay recovers the session's physical binding from, without any live routing state. **Hard pin, not sticky routing**: member activities carry new `session_id`/`session_worker_id` fields (threaded through `ScheduleActivity`/`ScheduledActivityCommand`/`EnqueueParams`/`NewTaskQueueItem`); the worker persists `sticky_worker_id`/`sticky_timeout` as bookkeeping (satisfying the existing `sticky_worker_id IS NULL <=> sticky_until IS NULL` check) plus the new `session_id` column, and `queue::claim_task`'s SQL gains `AND (session_id IS NULL OR sticky_worker_id = $1)` -- unlike ordinary sticky routing (#235), this **never** fails over to a different worker even after the lease bookkeeping value elapses, because session-local state genuinely only exists on the acquiring host. **Broken-session reclaim**: `sessions::enforce_broken_sessions` (folded into `timeout::enforce_timeouts_once`, no new poll loop; threads a `session_worker_stale_secs` param reusing the poison-pill reclaimer's `2 x worker_heartbeat_interval` convention) scans `ACTIVE` sessions whose host has no live `harvest_workers` heartbeat, is `Draining`/`Stopped`, or whose lease (`expires_at`) elapsed, and reclaims them in one transaction per session: the row transitions to `BROKEN` and every `PENDING`/`RUNNING` member task fails **non-retryably** with the reused `ActivityFailed { error_type: "SessionBroken" }` event (new `ERROR_TYPE_SESSION_BROKEN` constant) -- non-retryable because a hard-pinned task on a dead host can never fail over, so a retryable failure would re-pend forever. `Session::broken_session_error`/`WorkflowContext::create_session`'s error-mapping rewrite that typed `ActivityFailed` into `HarvestError::SessionBroken`, distinct from `HarvestError::SessionAcquireTimeout` (mapped from a `ScheduleToStart` timeout on the acquire dispatch) so author code can tell "never got a host" from "lost the host mid-pipeline." **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no replay-worker-dependence**: session identity replays via `MarkerRecorded` (new `HistoryMatcher::match_session_marker`, mirrors `match_fan_out_marker`); the physical binding is resolved fresh from the acquire activity's recorded `ActivityCompleted` output on every replay cycle, proven by `tests/replayer_tests.rs` fixtures that replay the identical history clean under three different recorded host worker ids. **Fleet plumbing**: `harvest_workers.max_concurrent_sessions`/`in_use_sessions` (additive columns, migration `20260706000000_harvest_worker_sessions`, alongside new `harvest_sessions` table and `harvest_task_queue.session_id`), threaded through `WorkerRegistration`/`register_worker`/`WorkerConfig::with_max_concurrent_sessions`/`WorkerRuntimeConfig`; auto-surfaced on `GET /workers`/`GET /workers/{id}` via `WorkerRow`'s existing `#[serde(flatten)] HarvestWorker` (no handler change). **Observability**: `harvest.session.acquisition{queue, outcome}` counter (`outcome` in `acquired`/`timed_out`/`broken`, `execution.id` never a label per ADR-0001) -- `acquired` fires in the worker's `handle_session_acquire` (a live-only code path, replay-safe by construction); `timed_out`/`broken` fire in `context.rs`'s error-mapping arms guarded by `!ctx.is_replaying()` so a later replay of the same recorded terminal outcome never double-counts. See the "Worker Sessions" decision matrix in this file (session vs. local activity vs. plain activity vs. claim-check/`PayloadStore` #524) and `examples/worker_session_pipeline.rs`. Tests: unit tests for `SessionOptions`/typed errors/marker determinism/slot-counter/broken-session pure predicates (`sessions.rs`, `context.rs`, `replay.rs`), `WorkflowTestEnv` auto-resolution of the two reserved activity names (`testing.rs`), `WorkflowReplayer` fixtures (`tests/replayer_tests.rs`), and DB/worker-loop integration tests (`tests/worker_session_tests.rs`: enqueue/claim hard-pin gate, hard-pin-vs-sticky-failover distinction, five broken-session-scanner scenarios, and a two-worker co-location test proving all member activities share one `worker_id`) -- compile-checked only in this sandbox (no Docker/testcontainers available), matching the #543/#544/#601 precedent.
- **Phase 3.47** (implemented): Reversible per-execution pause/resume for incident containment — reconciled with prior art, gaps closed — see issue #609. **Issue #609 asks for the capability issue #383 (Phase 3.23) already shipped**: `POST /api/harvest/workflows/{id}/pause`/`/resume` (admin-guarded, audited under `OP_WORKFLOW_PAUSE`/`OP_WORKFLOW_RESUME`), claim-layer enforcement (`queue::claim_task` skips workflow tasks of `PAUSED` executions) backed by the authoritative persist-time `FOR UPDATE` re-check in `worker::process_workflow_task`, `PAUSED` as a non-terminal active state everywhere active runs are enumerated, in-flight activities running to completion with results queued behind the pause, updates rejected 409 / queries still served, cancellation-beats-pause, SLA-clock suspension (`deadline_at` #243 and `sla_deadline_at` #487 shifted forward by the clamped pause span on resume, with an SLA deadline already elapsed before the pause deliberately left in the past), bounded pause via `WorkerConfig::max_workflow_pause_duration` (default 24h, `actor = "auto-resume(timeout)"` via `auto_resume_expired_pauses`), metrics `harvest.workflow.paused`/`harvest.workflow.pause_duration`, pause metadata (`paused_at`/`pause_reason`/`pause_actor`) surfaced on `GET /workflows/{id}`, and migration `20260607000002_harvest_workflow_pause`. Following the #543/#593 precedent, issue #609 was resolved by mapping its ACs onto the existing #383 implementation rather than re-deriving it. **AC-level reconciliations (kept as shipped, not renamed):** (a) the issue's "no new `WorkflowEvent` variant" AC conflicts with #383's shipped replay-transparent `WorkflowExecutionPaused`/`WorkflowExecutionResumed` events — reconciled as intent-satisfied: the AC's underlying bar is replay determinism, already proven by `tests/integration/pause_tests.rs` (pause→timer→resume→fire replays deterministically), and removing already-stored variants would itself violate the append-only invariant; (b) audit op names stay `OP_WORKFLOW_PAUSE`/`OP_WORKFLOW_RESUME` (`workflow.pause`/`workflow.resume`) rather than the issue's `OP_EXECUTION_PAUSE`/`OP_EXECUTION_RESUME` — renaming would orphan stored audit rows; (c) response field naming stays `actor` (not the issue's `paused_by`), with additive `paused_at` added to `PauseWorkflowResponse` instead of a rename — and the issue's requested `paused` boolean response field was likewise **not added**: `state == "PAUSED"` is the stand-in, since an execution's pausedness is already unambiguous from the `state` field carried on every pause/resume/describe response (flagged by review as a previously-undocumented reconciliation); (d) AC6's "pause works for RUNNING and SUSPENDED" is satisfied by RUNNING-only gating because `SUSPENDED` is not a persisted state (the state CHECK constraint forbids it — a suspended run's row is `RUNNING`); (e) AC1's "no task-queue rows claimed" is workflow-task-scoped by deliberate #383 design — already-dispatched activities run to completion rather than being yanked mid-flight. **Four genuine gaps found and closed, TDD red→green:** (1) **CLI** — `harvest workflow pause <execution_id> [--reason <text>]` and `harvest workflow resume <execution_id>` mapping 1:1 onto the existing endpoints (previously only schedule/DAG pause existed in the CLI); CLI mapping tests in `autumn-harvest-cli` (unit tests in `src/lib.rs` plus integration tests in `tests/integration/request_mapping.rs` and `tests/integration/contract_coverage.rs`). (2) **Resume idempotency consistency fix (behavior change)**: `resume_workflow_execution` previously returned 409 (`HarvestError::Config`) for a non-`PAUSED` execution; it now returns a success no-op (`newly_resumed: false`, pause duration 0) for any *existing* non-`PAUSED` execution (RUNNING or terminal), keeping 404 for unknown ids — idempotent operator retry, mirroring the #504 `newly_terminated: false` precedent. `ResumeWorkflowResponse` gains additive `newly_resumed`; both new response fields registered in `management_api_response_fields()` and `docs/api-contract.json`. (3) **`schedule_to_close` (#378) × pause — AC5 completion**: the `ScheduleToClose` timeout scanner now excludes tasks whose owning execution is `PAUSED` (the exclusion is `ScheduleToClose`-only: per-attempt start-to-close/heartbeat/schedule-to-start enforcement deliberately stays pause-blind — in-flight work still times out on its own merits); the worker's pre-requeue `schedule_to_close` deadline check requeues instead of deadline-failing when the owning execution is paused; and `resume_workflow_execution` shifts `schedule_to_close_at` forward by the clamped pause span for the execution's deadline-bearing `PENDING`/`RUNNING` task rows — an **unconditional** shift mirroring `deadline_at`, deliberately *not* `sla_deadline_at`'s elapsed-before-pause carve-out (no carve-out is needed: shifting by exactly the pause span never grants extra budget, since a deadline already elapsed before the pause began is still elapsed after resume by arithmetic — `deadline + span < paused_at + span = resumed_at`) — so an investigation pause can never itself trip a cross-retry activity deadline. DB integration tests in `tests/integration/pause_tests.rs` (compile-checked, no Docker in this sandbox, per the #543/#544/#601 precedent). (4) **Docs** — operator runbook `docs/runbooks/contain-runaway-execution.md` (three-way pause/cancel/terminate decision matrix extending #504's two-way cancel-vs-terminate contrast with reversibility/body-cooperation/in-flight-activity/terminal-state/completion-trigger rows, deadline-shift semantics, the auto-resume ceiling, and which-lever scoping vs schedule pause #229 / admission gates #377 / circuit breakers #369) plus a `runaway-execution-containment` drill in `docs/runbooks/synthetic-incident-drills.md` implementing the issue's success metric (pause mid-flight → zero further dispatch progress → resume → output identical to an un-paused control run). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration** (all columns already existed from #383's `20260607000002`). **Post-review hardening (multi-angle review, same issue #609):** three reviewer sign-offs on the overall design surfaced five findings, fixed TDD-style. (1) **P1 — stale `schedule_to_close_at` snapshot in the worker retry path**: `schedule_to_close_deadline_exceeded` (worker.rs) evaluates the claim-time `TaskQueueItem` snapshot, and this branch introduced the first post-enqueue mutator of that column (resume's pause-span shift) — an attempt claimed pre/mid-pause that failed retryably *after* the matching resume completed was deadline-failed against the stale pre-shift value even though the real (shifted) deadline was in the future: exactly the paused-time-charged-to-budget outcome AC5 exists to prevent.
