Fixed race-proof inside `record_schedule_to_close_activity_timeout`'s transaction — it already takes the execution row lock via `lock_workflow_execution_and_load_history`, the same lock `resume_workflow_execution` holds while shifting, so the two serialize — which now re-reads the task row's current `(state, schedule_to_close_at)` `FOR UPDATE` (new `task_state_and_deadline_for_update`) and aborts the timeout with the new `ScheduleToCloseTimeoutOutcome::DeadlineShifted` when the fresh deadline is no longer exceeded; `handle_activity_result` then falls through to a normal `requeue_for_retry`. The decision rule is the extracted pure `deadline_would_be_exceeded(deadline, now, retry_delay)`, shared verbatim by the snapshot gate and the in-transaction fresh re-check. Tests: `deadline_would_be_exceeded_{none_is_unbounded,true_when_retry_lands_past_deadline,false_after_a_resume_shift}` (worker.rs unit tests) and the worker-driven DB test `retry_path_requeues_when_a_concurrent_resume_shifted_the_deadline` (pause_tests.rs), which constructs the stale-snapshot state deterministically by having the activity itself shift its own row's deadline mid-attempt before failing retryably. (2) **P2 — external-task `schedule_to_close` was pause-blind**: `enforce_external_task_timeouts` (timeout.rs) scanned `harvest_external_tasks` with no PAUSED exclusion — it could transition a paused execution's external task to TIMED_OUT and append `ActivityTimedOut { ScheduleToClose }` mid-pause — and `resume_workflow_execution` shifted only `harvest_task_queue.schedule_to_close_at`, never the external table's. Fixed by mirroring the task-queue treatment: the scan (and its in-transaction race-guarded `UPDATE`, which also closes the pause-lands-after-scan race) now excludes external tasks whose owning execution is `PAUSED` — a *blanket* exclusion is deliberately correct there, because that scan enforces only the `schedule_to_close_at` wall-clock deadline (external tasks have no heartbeat/start-to-close reason whose in-flight semantics would argue for pause-blind enforcement) and only `PENDING` external rows are open (all other states are terminal) — and the new `shift_external_schedule_to_close_on_resume_query` (execution.rs, same extracted-`const fn`-SQL style, wired into `shift_schedule_to_close_for_resume` so it joins the resume transaction) shifts the execution's still-`PENDING` external tasks' deadline forward by the same clamped pause span. Tests: `external_resume_shift_query_targets_open_external_tasks_only` (execution.rs SQL unit test), `external_task_timeout_scanner_skips_paused_executions` and `resume_shifts_external_task_schedule_to_close_by_pause_span` (pause_tests.rs DB tests). (3) **P2 — pause-blind `ScheduleToStart` could terminally kill the frozen-PENDING state this branch created**: the pause-aware `ScheduleToClose` treatment plus the pre-existing claim condition (`schedule_to_close_at > NOW()`) produce a new state — a `PENDING` activity row of a paused execution past its (unshifted) deadline, frozen-unclaimable until resume shifts it — and if such a row also carries `schedule_to_start`, the pause-blind `ScheduleToStart` scanner would terminally fail it mid-pause once `scheduled_at + schedule_to_start < NOW()`. Resolved with the review's option (b), the narrowest precise fix, rather than a blanket paused-execution exclusion or a documented residual: `schedule_to_start_timeout_query` now spares exactly the frozen rows (owner `PAUSED` **and** `schedule_to_close_at IS NOT NULL AND schedule_to_close_at <= NOW()`) — an unfrozen pending activity of a paused execution is still claimable by design (activities are not pause-gated), so its schedule-to-start signal (worker capacity) remains genuine and stays enforced — and the resume shift query also pushes `scheduled_at` forward by the pause span for exactly those frozen rows, via a `CASE` that reads the pre-update deadline (SQL `UPDATE` semantics: `SET` expressions see the old row). The `scheduled_at` shift is harmless (a frozen row was unclaimable throughout, so claim ordering — `ORDER BY priority, scheduled_at` — is only perturbed for rows that were out of the running anyway) and restores both the schedule-to-start budget and the retry-backoff position relative to the resume instant, closing option (a)'s instantly-killed-on-the-first-post-resume-scan problem. Tests: `schedule_to_start_timeout_query_excludes_only_frozen_paused_rows` (timeout.rs), `resume_shift_query_restores_scheduled_at_for_frozen_rows_only` (execution.rs), `schedule_to_start_scanner_spares_frozen_rows_but_enforces_unfrozen_paused_rows` and `resume_shifts_scheduled_at_for_frozen_rows_only` (pause_tests.rs DB tests). (4) **P2 — Vantage UI flash could not distinguish a no-op resume**: the resume flash said "Workflow resumed" unconditionally even for a `newly_resumed: false` no-op; the pause flash had the symmetric gap. New pure `resume_flash_message`/`pause_flash_message` helpers (ui.rs) branch on the result ("Workflow was not paused; nothing to resume" / "Workflow was already paused"); audit-row fidelity is deliberately unchanged — a no-op still writes `STATUS_SUCCEEDED`, precedent-consistent with pause/terminate. Test: `pause_and_resume_flashes_distinguish_noops` (ui.rs). (5) The `conflict_from` doc comment in api.rs dropped its now-stale `"not paused"` 409 example (resume no longer 409s on a non-paused run). (6, **follow-up bot-review round**) the pause-span→microseconds conversion in `shift_schedule_to_close_for_resume` (execution.rs) used a raw `num_microseconds().unwrap_or(i64::MAX)` fallback for an unrepresentable span — binding `i64::MAX` into the `... * INTERVAL '1 microsecond'` shift SQL would raise a Postgres "timestamp/interval out of range" error and roll back the resume transaction; practically unreachable (needs a >292k-year span between two real timestamps) but now clamped to a documented finite bound (`MAX_PAUSE_SHIFT_MICROS`, 100 years — far below Postgres's interval/timestamp range) via the pure `clamped_pause_shift_micros` helper, pinned by `pause_shift_micros_clamps_unrepresentable_span_to_finite_bound`/`pause_shift_micros_passes_normal_spans_through_untouched` (execution.rs unit tests). (7, **follow-up bot-review round**) the retry path's `owning_execution_is_paused` gate (worker.rs) is a non-locking read taken *before* `record_schedule_to_close_activity_timeout` opens its transaction and takes the execution row lock — a pause committing in that gap was observed by the transaction as a now-PAUSED execution, but the transaction only re-checked task state and the row-current deadline (finding 1's resume-shift fix), so it would append `ActivityTimedOut { ScheduleToClose }` and terminally fail the task *while paused*, violating the pause-suspends-`schedule_to_close` contract for that race window. Fixed authoritatively under the lock: the lock helper already loaded the full execution row and discarded it, so the new `lock_workflow_execution_row_and_load_history` returns it for free (the original helper is now a thin wrapper), and the whole in-transaction bail-out decision is the extracted pure `schedule_to_close_recheck_outcome(execution_state, task_row, now, retry_delay)` — concurrently-resolved task → `Handled` (wins over everything; never requeue), execution `PAUSED` → new `ScheduleToCloseTimeoutOutcome::ExecutionPaused` variant (wins over the deadline arithmetic; the caller falls through to the normal `requeue_for_retry`, where the `PENDING` row is frozen by the claim gate and its deadline shifted on resume), fresh deadline no longer exceeded → `DeadlineShifted`, else proceed to enforce. The non-locking gate's doc comment no longer frames the race as an accepted "already-dispatched work" boundary — it is a fast-path optimization; the locked re-check is the guarantee. Tests: 5 pure unit tests for `schedule_to_close_recheck_outcome` (worker.rs, incl. `recheck_paused_execution_wins_over_an_exceeded_row_deadline` and `recheck_concurrently_resolved_task_is_handled_even_when_paused`) and the DB integration test `retry_path_requeues_when_a_concurrent_pause_committed_after_the_gate` (pause_tests.rs), which reproduces the race window via the round-3 lock-holding pattern (an explicit-`BEGIN` `FOR UPDATE` hold with the real `pause_workflow_execution` queued behind it, released only after the retryable failure passes the gate) and asserts the task is requeued `PENDING`, no `ActivityTimedOut` is appended, and the execution stays `PAUSED`. (8, **second bot-review round**) two more instances of the same scan-vs-enforce race class as item (7), both in `timeout.rs`'s scanners, both P2: **(a)** the `ScheduleToClose` scanner's PAUSED exclusion protected only the `find_timed_out_tasks` SELECT snapshot — `enforce_activity_timeout` locked the execution row (via the module's own lock helper) but never re-checked whether the locked row was now `PAUSED`, so a pause committing after the scan (or while enforcement waited on the very lock `pause_workflow_execution` holds) still appended `ActivityTimedOut { ScheduleToClose }` and failed the task mid-pause; **(b)** the external-task scanner's PAUSED check was a non-locking `NOT EXISTS` subquery inside the claiming UPDATE — it never serialized with `pause_workflow_execution` (which locks only the execution row), so the UPDATE could snapshot a still-RUNNING execution, flip the external task to `TIMED_OUT`, and append the event after the pause committed. Both fixed with the same shape as item (7): the enforcement decision moves under the execution row lock. `enforce_activity_timeout` now uses a timeout.rs sibling of worker.rs's `lock_workflow_execution_row_and_load_history` (the original `pub(crate)` helper is a thin wrapper, so `sessions.rs` and the outbox scanners are untouched) plus a new `task_state_and_deadline_for_update`, and bails without mutation when the extracted pure `pause_suppresses_timeout_enforcement(reason, execution_state, row_schedule_to_close_at, now)` says the pause suppresses the reason. Per-reason scoping mirrors the scan queries exactly: `ScheduleToClose` → always skip while paused (AC5); `ScheduleToStart` → skip **only** when the row is now *frozen* (row-current `schedule_to_close_at` set and elapsed — the same scan-vs-enforce race applies to the item-(3) frozen-row carve-out, since a row unfrozen at scan time can become frozen before enforcement locks it and would then be terminally schedule-to-start-failed mid-pause; an unfrozen pending row of a paused execution stays claimable by design, so its worker-capacity signal stays enforced); `Heartbeat`/`StartToClose` → stay pause-blind (in-flight work still times out on its own merits, per the #383 boundary). `enforce_external_task_timeouts`'s per-task transaction now takes the execution row `FOR UPDATE` *first*, re-checks `PAUSED` under it via the same pure helper (a skipped task is left genuinely `PENDING`/untouched for the resume shift; a vanished execution row falls through to the claiming UPDATE's own filters, matching prior behavior), then runs the claiming UPDATE with the now-redundant `NOT EXISTS` subquery removed — the state flip and the event append happen under the same lock the pause path holds; the function's return value now counts only tasks actually timed out (a locked-re-check skip or 0-row UPDATE no longer inflates it). Tests: 4 pure unit tests for `pause_suppresses_timeout_enforcement` (timeout.rs: `pause_recheck_never_suppresses_a_running_execution`, `pause_recheck_suppresses_schedule_to_close_while_paused`, `pause_recheck_suppresses_schedule_to_start_only_for_frozen_rows`, `pause_recheck_keeps_in_flight_reasons_pause_blind`) and two DB integration tests (pause_tests.rs: `activity_schedule_to_close_enforcement_yields_to_a_pause_committed_after_the_scan`, driving the full `enforce_timeouts_once` path, and `external_task_enforcement_yields_to_a_pause_committed_after_the_scan`), both reproducing the exact race window with the shared `hold_execution_row_lock` helper — hold the execution row lock on one connection (explicit `BEGIN` + `FOR UPDATE`), queue the real `pause_workflow_execution` behind it, start the scanner so its non-locking scan snapshot sees the still-RUNNING execution while its enforcement transaction queues on the lock *behind* the pause, then release — and asserting the task stays `PENDING`/untouched, no `ActivityTimedOut` is appended, and the execution stays `PAUSED` (pause_tests.rs's `INIT_SQL` gained the `20260618000001_harvest_debounce` migration since `enforce_timeouts_once` scans that table). (9, **third bot-review round**) item (8b)'s own fix introduced a classic ABBA lock inversion, P2: the external-task scanner's per-task transaction took the **execution** row lock first and then updated the **external-task** row, while the pre-existing external-task completion paths (`external_task.rs`'s `complete_externally`/`fail_externally`/`extend_deadline`) lock in the opposite order — task row first via `lock_task` (`FOR UPDATE`), then the execution row inside `store::append_single_event` — so a scanner tick racing a valid external completion could deadlock, with Postgres aborting one transaction and surfacing a spurious error/500 to the completion caller. Fixed by restoring task-row-first ordering in `enforce_external_task_timeouts`: the per-task transaction now locks the external-task row `FOR UPDATE` and re-verifies it under that lock via the new pure `external_task_timeout_still_due(state, schedule_to_close_at, now)` (still `PENDING` **and** deadline still elapsed — the locked re-read replaces trusting the scan snapshot, so a completion/extension/resume-shift that won the race is skipped: no flip, no event, not counted), *then* takes the execution row lock for the item-(8b) PAUSED re-check (the pause-suppression guarantee is unchanged — the flip and the `ActivityTimedOut` append still happen with the execution lock held), then flips the row with a plain `UPDATE` (no re-filters needed under the held lock). The per-table lock-ordering convention is now documented in a code comment at the scanner's transaction: **`harvest_external_tasks`: task row → execution row** (completion paths + scanner); **`harvest_task_queue`: execution row → task row** (`enforce_activity_timeout`, `worker::record_schedule_to_close_activity_timeout`, `finalize_activity_completion`/`_failure` — safe because no task-queue writer locks task-then-execution: `queue::requeue_for_retry` touches only the task row; both branch-touched task-queue paths were audited and left as-is this round). Auditing the branch for the same hazard also surfaced that item (2)'s `shift_external_schedule_to_close_on_resume_query` runs inside the execution-locked resume transaction — necessarily the inverted order for `harvest_external_tasks` — and could therefore ABBA against a completion (or the now-task-first scanner) holding a task row while waiting on the execution lock; fixed by rewriting the shift as `UPDATE ... WHERE id IN (SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED)` so resume never *waits* on a task row and cannot join a lock cycle — skipping a locked row is safe by construction (a completer's row goes terminal once resume commits, an `extend_deadline` row gets a fresh `NOW()`-anchored deadline, and a scanner-locked row was already expired *before* the pause began — the scan excludes PAUSED executions — so the shift would not have rescued it anyway). A deterministic DB deadlock-reproduction test was deliberately not attempted (hard to do meaningfully); the ordering rationale is comment-documented at both sites instead. Tests: 3 pure unit tests for `external_task_timeout_still_due` (timeout.rs: enforce-still-open-expired, skip-concurrently-resolved, skip-deadline-shifted-into-future incl. the exact-`NOW()` boundary) and `external_resume_shift_query_never_waits_on_a_locked_task_row` (execution.rs SQL unit test); the item-(8b) DB test `external_task_enforcement_yields_to_a_pause_committed_after_the_scan` holds unchanged under the restructure (the scanner takes the uncontended task-row lock first, then queues on the execution lock behind the pause exactly as before). All new pause_tests.rs DB integration tests are compile-checked only in this sandbox (no Docker/testcontainers), per the #543/#544/#601 precedent.
- **Phase 3.47** (implemented): Operator read-path decoding of codec-encrypted payloads — see issue #608. New tolerant core API on `PayloadCodecs` (`payload_codec.rs`): `decode_value_lossy` (infallible, recursive, single-pass — never re-scans decoded plaintext; ignores `_harvest_offload_envelope`/`_harvest_erased`/malformed near-envelopes; shares one private `codec_envelope_parts` shape check with the strict `decode_payload` so the two paths can never disagree about what an envelope is), `decode_error_string_lossy` (TEXT `error` columns: rewrites only when the string parses as JSON that IS exactly an envelope; a decoded plain-string payload comes back unwrapped), `LossyDecodeOutcome { decoded, failed }` (`touched()`/`merged()`; counts only, never values), `undecodable_marker` + `UNDECODABLE_MARKER_KEY` and four bounded `UNDECODABLE_REASON_*` constants (`{"_harvest_undecodable":{"codec_id","reason"}}` per-field graceful degrade — one bad envelope never 500s a history, and the marker never echoes ciphertext or codec error text). Opt-in via `HarvestPlugin::decode_payloads_on_read()` (const-fn flag, mirrors `mcp_tools()`; default **off** — the decoder gate returns `None` and every handler executes its pre-existing code path byte-for-byte). **Decode-only-when-admin (AC6)**: the plugin mirrors the codec registry + flag onto `HarvestApiState` (`set_payload_codecs`/`set_decode_payloads_on_read`, the SSRF-policy mirror precedent), and handlers obtain a decoder only through `api.rs::read_path_decoder`, which requires the flag AND `has_harvest_admin_access` (the same predicate as the `require_admin` layer, pinned by the pure `decode_gate` truth table) — ungated describe routes are never retro-gated and a non-admin caller sees today's bytes. Applied per-handler to `/result` (zero-wait + long-poll via `respond_with_workflow_result`), history export single+batch (**Full policy only** — Redacted skips decode and audit entirely), describe (`decode_workflow_execution_fields` on execution `input`/`output`/`memo`/`search_attrs`/`error` + embedded history + carryover convenience fields), `/history`, `/stack` (heartbeat checkpoints decoded before the #503 cap projection, plus a **new optional `input` field on `PendingActivity`** — `skip_serializing_if` and populated only when decoding is active for the request, so flag-off responses stay byte-identical; nested field, so no route/params/contract change), `GET /dead-letters` (JSONB `input` + TEXT `error`), the SSE stream (the `sse_data` closure became the shared `sse_frame_data(event_data, decoder)` used by both backfill and live loops, decoder resolved once at stream open), and the Vantage UI (workflow detail incl. timeline/attempts/signals panels + blocked-on activities/signals; DLQ page rows incl. last-10 events). Audit op `OP_PAYLOAD_DECODE_READ = "payload.decode_read"` registered in `AUDITED_OPERATIONS` (a read audit like `OP_EXECUTION_STREAM_OPEN` — no `CLASSIFIED_ROUTES`/`ALL_MUTATION_ROUTES` change): one best-effort row per request that decoded or marked ≥1 envelope (`audit_decoded_read` helper, never fails the read, never records payload content); SSE audits once at stream open whenever decode mode is active (a deliberate, documented superset since frame counts are unknowable up front); a request that finds no envelopes is audit-silent. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no route/contract change, no store/replay change — stored rows are never mutated** (decoding operates on the in-memory response copy only). Docs `docs/operations/read-path-decode.md` (opt-in, same-URL-differs-by-session semantics, `api_with_auth` boundary implication, marker/audit contracts, DLQ `failure_signature`-over-ciphertext known limit) + ADR-0002 addendum. Tests (TDD red→green, red = compile error per the #593 precedent): 20 pure unit tests in `payload_codec.rs` (incl. single-pass no-rescan, offload/tombstone/malformed passthrough, no-ciphertext-echo, outcome semantics), audit-registry test in `audit.rs`, `decode_gate` truth table + api-state default-off + `sse_frame_data` unit tests in plugin `api.rs`, plugin flag test in `plugin.rs`; 14 HTTP integration tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/read_path_decode_integration.rs` (byte-identical-when-off, decode-only-when-admin, Redacted-skip, per-field degrade → 200 + marker, audit-once-per-request with content-free rows, SSE decode + stream-open audit, stored-rows-untouched) + 2 Vantage UI tests in `ui_integration.rs` — testcontainers, compile-checked only in this sandbox (no Docker), matching the #543/#544/#601 precedent — CI runs both suites Docker-backed on Linux (dedicated `read_path_decode_integration`/`ui_integration` steps in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`); envelope rows are seeded directly since engine write paths use identity codecs. **Known limits (documented in the ops doc):** `GET /workflows` (list, incl. the stalled loader) and `GET /dags/{dag_name}/runs` deliberately remain undecoded (unbounded full-row lists on the navigation path; the detail surfaces decode), and decoded values are **not authenticated** — envelopes are self-describing and the identity codec is always registered, so envelope-shaped caller-influenced data can masquerade as platform-decrypted plaintext (provenance caveat in `docs/operations/read-path-decode.md`; raw stored bytes remain readable via flag-off / non-admin / undecoded surfaces). **Post-review hardening (multi-angle review):** UI-originated decode audit rows now carry `source: ui` via an `audit_decoded_read` source-override parameter (matching every other ui.rs audit row); the codec-registry mirror moved from `start_harvest_runtime` into `Plugin::build` next to the flag mirror (new pre-build `HarvestBuilder::payload_codecs()` accessor) so no boot window sees an identity-only registry; `LossyDecodeOutcome::touched()` is addition-free (consistent with the saturating `merged()`); and executed unit coverage was added for the describe field census (`decode_workflow_execution_fields`), `respond_with_workflow_result`'s decode legs, the flag-off `PendingActivity.input` key-omission serde contract, `decode_error_string_lossy`'s failure/non-string branches, and the envelope-shaped-business-plaintext known limit, plus integration coverage for `/result?wait=` long-poll decode, flag-off `/stack` byte-identity, and the UI blocked-on panel (heartbeat checkpoint + timeline + signal fixtures). **Post-review hardening, round 2 (PR #936 Codex review):** (1) **P1 — export loaded history through the strict identity-only codec path**: both export handlers (single + batch) called `store::load_history`, which strictly decodes with `PayloadCodecs::default()` and hard-errors `UnknownPayloadCodec` on the first non-identity envelope — so on a real encrypted deployment every export (Full AND Redacted) failed before the read-path decode/redact policy ever ran. Fixed with a new core loader `store::load_history_undecoded` (raw `serde_json::from_value` per row, no codec transform — envelopes ride along verbatim as opaque `Value`s inside payload fields; byte-identical to `load_history` on identity deployments, which store no envelopes; **never for replay**, doc-pinned) wired into `export_workflow_history`, `export_history_candidate` (batch), and `load_continue_as_new_successor` (the `/result` + MCP status/watch ContinuedAsNew chain walk, which reads only the typed `new_exec_id` and previously failed the same way on encrypted histories). Flag-off contract: a Full export now carries the stored envelopes verbatim (the same ciphertext describe/history/SSE already surface) and a Redacted export replaces payload fields wholesale, envelope included — identity deployments byte-identical, encrypted deployments go from hard-500 to working. The other strict-`load_history` read callers (`retry_dag_run`, `hydrate_ctx_for_query`, update-result polling) are pre-existing trunk behavior on surfaces this feature never touched — left alone. Tests: `full_history_export_preserves_codec_envelopes_verbatim`/`redacted_history_export_replaces_codec_envelopes_without_decoding` (pure, `history_export.rs`), `codec_envelope_survives_plain_event_serde_round_trip` (pure, `payload_codec.rs`, the loader's mechanism), `history_export_flag_off_returns_stored_envelopes_verbatim` (integration). (2) **P2 — `audit_decoded_read` acquired a second pool connection while callers still held their read connection** (a size-1 pool would stall until the pool timeout and then silently skip the audit): the helper now takes `conn: Option<&mut AsyncPgConnection>` — the five callers that hold an execution-shard connection across the await (export single, describe, `/history`, `/stack`, the UI workflow-detail page via a new `conn` param on `decode_and_audit_workflow_detail`) pass it through so the audit row is written on the caller's own connection (mirroring the SSE stream-open audit precedent), while conn-free callers (batch export, `/result`, DLQ list API + UI, whose per-shard loads are scoped inside their `_from_shards` helpers) pass `None` and keep the pool-acquiring branch, with the must-not-hold-a-connection constraint documented on the helper. Test: `decode_audit_reuses_callers_connection_under_single_connection_pool` (integration, size-1 pool: all four conn-holding surfaces return promptly and their audit rows land). **Post-review hardening, round 3 (PR #936 Codex review, P2): export size enforcement ran on the pre-decode document.** With `payload_policy=full` + decoding enabled, both export handlers decoded events *after* `export_history` had already measured the serialized document against `max_bytes` and stamped `size_limit.actual_bytes` — so a decoded response could exceed `max_bytes` while reporting the stale pre-decode size, and an over-limit *encoded* envelope rejected an export whose decoded form would have fit (batch path identical). Fixed decode-before-measure, keeping one source of truth: new core `history_export::export_history_decoded(request, decoder: Option<&PayloadCodecs>, outcome: &mut LossyDecodeOutcome)` runs `decode_value_lossy` on each exported event **before** the document is assembled and measured (Full policy only — the decoder is ignored under Redacted; `export_history` is now the `None`-passing wrapper, byte-identical flag-off), so `max_bytes` enforcement and `actual_bytes` always describe the decoded bytes the caller actually receives. Both api.rs handlers resolve the decoder up front (still gated on Full, so Redacted never even resolves it) and thread it plus the outcome accumulator through `export_history_for_execution`/`export_history_for_candidate` and the batch chain (`load_history_exports_from_shards` → `export_selected_history_candidates` → `export_history_candidate`); the `&mut outcome` signature (rather than a return value) deliberately survives a `SizeLimitExceeded` rejection, so a decode that happened for an export subsequently rejected for size is still audited. Tests: three executed pure unit tests in `history_export.rs` (decoded-over-limit rejected with the decoded `actual_bytes` + outcome retained on error; over-limit envelope with a small decoded form now exports, with `actual_bytes` equal to the serialized returned document; Redacted ignores the decoder entirely) plus `history_export_size_limit_is_enforced_on_the_decoded_document` in `read_path_decode_integration.rs` (flag-off 413 on the encoded doc vs decode-enabled 200 whose `actual_bytes` equals the response body length — testcontainers, compile-checked in this sandbox per the same precedent). **Post-review hardening, round 4 (PR #936 Codex review, P2): stack activity inputs were unbounded under decode.** With decoding enabled, `/workflows/{id}/stack` added the *full* decoded task input to every pending activity, but the task query is unbounded and per-activity inputs can be up to the #252 input cap (2 MiB default) — a large fan-out could serialize hundreds of MiB in one response, unlike `heartbeat_details`, which is projected per-field and then bounded by the cumulative #503 response budget. Fixed by applying the exact same two-layer policy to `input`: (a) per-field, the decoded input is projected through the same pure `project_heartbeat_details` projection (doc-updated to note it is a generic bounded-payload projection) against the activity's effective *input* cap via the new `HandlerRegistry::activity_input_cap(name)` (core, mirrors `activity_result_cap` — per-activity `max_input_bytes` override raised against the global ceiling); (b) cumulatively, the new `apply_input_response_budget` (reusing the shared `checkpoint_budget_decisions` core, global input cap as the budget, first-entry-always-kept rule included) withholds inputs past the per-response total. Truncation signaling mirrors the checkpoint trio with three new `PendingActivity` fields — `input_truncated`, `input_omitted_for_budget`, `input_bytes` — plus a response-level `inputs_truncated_for_budget`; all four are `Option`-typed + `skip_serializing_if` (unlike the always-present checkpoint bools) purely so flag-off/non-admin responses omit the keys and stay byte-identical to pre-#608 builds — they are populated whenever decoding is active, and a withheld input still reports its observed size. Tests: `activity_input_cap_resolves_per_activity_override` (core worker.rs), four `apply_input_response_budget` unit tests mirroring the checkpoint-budget suite plus the extended serde key-omission contract test (plugin api.rs, executed), and budget-field assertions added to `stack_pending_activity_input_is_decoded` (integration, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent). **Post-review hardening, round 5 (PR #936 Codex review, P2): the UI workflow-detail page decoded hidden fields.** With decoding enabled, `decode_and_audit_workflow_detail` (ui.rs) decrypted every pending activity's `input`, every blocked-on pending-signal payload, and the attempts/signals panel event copies (`activity_events`/`signal_update_events`) and counted them in the `payload.decode_read` audit outcome — but the renderers never display any of them (`render_pending_activities_table` shows name/state/attempt/timestamps/checkpoint only; the pending-signals table shows name/received-at only; the attempts and signals panels render names/error strings/timestamps, never payload fields), so this was wasted codec/KMS work plus audit rows for plaintext never shown. Fixed by decoding only what the page renders — extracted into the pure, unit-testable `decode_workflow_detail_rendered_fields(codecs, execution, timeline_events, blocked_on)`: execution payload fields (Input/Output/Memo/Search-attributes cards + error banner), the timeline page's event payloads, and blocked-on heartbeat checkpoints; the audit outcome now counts exactly the surfaced fields, so a page whose only envelopes are hidden writes no audit row. Input rendering was deliberately NOT added (skipping is the right scope; the stack API remains the bounded input surface, round 4). The DLQ UI page was audited and left unchanged — it renders everything it decodes (full error, full input, last-10 event payloads). Ops-doc decoded-surface table updated to spell out the exact rendered set. Tests: `hidden_detail_fields_are_not_decoded_and_never_touch_the_audit_outcome` + `rendered_detail_fields_are_decoded_and_counted_exactly` (executed ui.rs unit tests on the extracted helper, with a local reverse-codec fixture); `workflow_detail_ui_renders_decoded_input` gains hidden-plaintext-never-appears assertions and the new `workflow_detail_ui_writes_no_audit_row_when_only_hidden_fields_carry_envelopes` (seeds envelopes *only* in hidden surfaces, isolating the panel event copies from the timeline via `?event_page=1`, and asserts a 200 render with zero `payload.decode_read` rows — integration, compile-checked per the same sandbox precedent).
- **Phase 3.47** (implemented): `ctx.patched()` / `ctx.deprecate_patch()` — ergonomic boolean two-state code-evolution lifecycle — see issue #687. The overwhelmingly common workflow-logic change has exactly two states (before/after); `ctx.version(id, min, max)`'s integer bookkeeping is overkill for it. `WorkflowContext::patched(patch_id) -> bool` fences the change (`if ctx.patched("id") { new } else { old }`): on the live frontier it records a `patch:{patch_id}` marker via the **existing** `MarkerRecorded` event (numeric `details = 1`, consistent with version markers; the `patch:` prefix is new and unclaimed alongside `version:`/`side_effect:`/`fan_out:`/`race:`/`race_winner:`/`session:`) and returns `true`; on replay it returns `true` when the marker is at the cursor (consumed) and `false` for pre-patch histories (cursor untouched, so the recorded event still matches the old branch's next command). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no new `WorkflowCommand` variant, no migration, no worker.rs change** (`RecordMarker` already rides the full bookkeeping persistence path). New three-state `replay::PatchMarkerMatch` (`Recorded`/`Absent`/`NewlyPatched`, re-exported from `lib.rs`) returned by `HistoryMatcher::match_patch_marker`, which mirrors `match_version`'s exact scan discipline (pre-drain live check → `drain_early_signals` → post-drain recorded-position check → marker-at-cursor consume) but deliberately avoids mirroring its "return max and let the caller re-check `is_replaying()`" boolean-ambiguity trick (note: `match_version` has a latent duplicate-marker edge when the consumed marker is the final history event — left unchanged, deliberately not replicated). **`version()` interop**: `match_patch_marker`/`deprecate_patch` also consume `version:{id}` markers — presence alone decides (regardless of the recorded number, since `version()` only records when it returned max on live execution), so a two-version `ctx.version(id, 1, 2)` gate migrates to `ctx.patched(id)` in place. **Deprecation (deploy 2)**: `WorkflowContext::deprecate_patch(patch_id)` → `HistoryMatcher::deprecate_patch` does a full-history scan marking every `patch:{id}`/`version:{id}` marker consumed via the existing `consumed_out_of_order_events` set (positional matching cannot apply — phase-2 code calls it at a different, usually earlier, position than the phase-1 `patched()` call that recorded the marker, so the marker must become transparent wherever it sits or it would trip the next `match_*` as a divergence) and memoizes presence in a new `deprecated_patches: HashMap<String, bool>` matcher field; the memo makes a *residual* `patched(id)` call deterministic (`true` for phase-1 histories, `false` for phase-0 histories AND — the documented footgun — for new executions started post-deprecation, which record nothing; the fix is deleting the residual call). Emits zero commands/events; idempotent. Three-deploy lifecycle: (1) `if ctx.patched(id)` fence → (2) once pre-patch runs drain (`docs/runbooks/version-gate-retirement.md`), `ctx.deprecate_patch(id)` + unconditional new code → (3) once marker-bearing runs drain, delete the call. `ctx.version()` remains the explicit escape hatch for >2 concurrent versions. New `NonDeterminismKind::PatchMarkerMismatch` in `testing.rs` (`classify_kind` recognises `MarkerRecorded(patch:…)` actuals, mirroring the version-marker special case) surfaces a too-early deploy 3. Tests: 9 `context.rs` unit tests (live marker emission, replay with/without marker, version-marker interop, deprecate-consumes-anywhere + residual-patched determinism, the pinned footgun, repeated-call determinism), 6 `replay.rs` matcher unit tests, `parse_nd_message`/`classify_kind` tests, 5 `WorkflowReplayer` integration tests in `tests/integration/replayer_tests.rs` (the success-metric money test — the SAME phase-1 handler replays pre-patch and post-patch histories down opposite branches with zero integer bookkeeping — plus too-early-removal → `PatchMarkerMismatch`, version-history interop, and the deploy-2/residual-patched lifecycle proofs). Example `examples/patched_workflow.rs` (introduce → deprecate → remove walkthrough with `WorkflowTestEnv` + cross-phase `replay_check` tests). Docs: `docs/getting-started/07-reliability-knobs.md` versioning section now leads with `patched`/`deprecate_patch` (three-deploy sequence) with `version()` as the escape hatch; `docs/workflow-determinism-guide.md` remediation item updated; "Patched / Code Evolution" usage subsection below. **Post-review hardening (four-reviewer pass on the initial ship):** (1) **Real correctness bug — the sandwich flip** (`patched(id)` → `deprecate_patch(id)` → residual `patched(id)` in one body, confirmed empirically): on the LIVE cycle the first call's marker exists only as a pending `WorkflowCommand::RecordMarker`, invisible to `deprecate_patch`'s full-history scan, so the memo latched `false` and the residual call returned `false`; on the next cycle the marker is in history, the memo latched `true` → live/replay branch flip → permanent nd-block (issue #603 semantics); the same flip occurred via a live `ctx.version(id, ..)` before `deprecate_patch(id)`. Fixed with a `patch_ids_recorded_this_cycle: HashSet<String>` latch on `HistoryMatcher`, inserted by `match_patch_marker`'s `NewlyPatched` arm and `match_version`'s live-frontier branch (exactly the two cases where the context pushes a marker command) and ORed into `deprecate_patch`'s presence computation — the sandwich now yields `(true, true)` on both passes with exactly one recorded marker. TDD red→green: `matcher_deprecate_patch_sees_marker_recorded_this_cycle`, `matcher_deprecate_patch_sees_version_marker_recorded_this_cycle`, `context_patched_then_deprecate_then_patched_is_consistent_live`, plus the replayer proof `replay_patched_deprecate_patched_sandwich_is_replay_consistent`. (2) **Signal-with-start caveat documented, behavior deliberately unchanged** (exact parity with `match_version`, conservative by design): a fresh run whose first-task history ends in un-awaited signals at the gate point — canonically every signal-with-start run — takes the old branch and records no marker forever; documented on the rustdoc/CLAUDE.md/reliability-knobs surfaces and pinned by `matcher_patch_marker_trailing_signal_history_is_absent`. (3) **Drain-tooling blindness closed at the docs layer**: `harvest version-usage`/`version-gate-retirement --check` filter `LIKE 'version:%'` and return a FALSE "safe" for patch gates — new "Patched gates (issue #687)" section in `docs/runbooks/version-gate-retirement.md` with raw SQL drain queries (marker-presence before deploy 3; the inverse no-marker query before deploy 2, which is also what catches the marker-less signal-with-start runs), the tooling extension named as the follow-up slice, and all three doc pointers softened so none implies the CLI covers patches. (4) **Shared-namespace warning** (docs-only guard): patch ids and version change-ids share one namespace — `deprecate_patch(id)` interop-consumes `version:{id}` markers, so calling it while a still-live `ctx.version(id, ..)` gate exists wedges that gate onto `min`. (5) Empty patch ids now `assert!` (mirroring `version()`'s argument asserts) on both `patched` and `deprecate_patch`, with `#[should_panic]` tests; `context.rs` `version()` swapped to the `replay::version_marker_name` helper. (6) New pinning tests: `matcher_patch_marker_later_marker_does_not_match_at_cursor` (positional semantics — a later marker never satisfies an earlier call), `replay_trailing_stale_patch_marker_classifies_as_early_completion` (a trailing stale marker surfaces as the generic `EarlyCompletion`, not `PatchMarkerMismatch`, since no later command trips over it), and the positive deploy-3 proof `replay_phase3_gate_free_code_replays_marker_less_phase2_history`. (7) **AC-6 "caught in CI" actually holds now**: the testing-gated replayer integration suite previously never ran in CI (compile-only, a pre-existing repo-wide gap) — new cheap no-DB CI step `cargo test -p autumn-harvest --no-default-features --features testing --test integration` runs the whole target.
- **Phase 3.47** (implemented): Workflow-start throttle — pace admissions, defer the excess (issue #607). Scheduled fan-outs, backfills (#337), webhook storms (#344), and batch starts routinely admit hundreds of legitimate starts in one tick; when every one hits the same rate-limited downstream, none of the existing primitives fit — activity RPS limits (#332) throttle *after* N workflows already started, debounce (#499) *drops* redundant triggers, the admission gate (#377) is a *binary halt*, per-key concurrency (#247) bounds *in-flight count* not *rate*. `ThrottlePolicy { refill_per_sec, burst, key_expr: Option<&'static str>, schedule_to_start: Option<Duration> }` (`throttle.rs`) declares a per-workflow-type start rate via `#[workflow(throttle(rate = "100/m", burst = 20, key = "input.tenant_id", schedule_to_start = "5m"))]` (all but `rate` optional) or the `WorkflowInfo::with_throttle` builder. **Admission model (confirmed design choice — consume-then-defer, not always-defer):** `throttle::reserve_or_defer` first checks a FIFO fast-path guard (an existing pending backlog for the key means the new start must append, not jump the queue), then attempts a single-token debit via the **existing** activity-limiter machinery (`queue::try_consume_rate_limit_token` against `harvest_rate_limit_buckets`, keyed `"start-throttle:{workflow}:{key}"`, auto-registered `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) — a reserved token lets the start proceed **immediately** (`201`, byte-identical to an unthrottled start except for the token spend); an empty bucket **durably defers** the start into a new `harvest_start_throttle` row **before any `WorkflowStarted` event is appended** and returns `202 {throttled: true, workflow_name, workflow_id, throttle_key, deferred_at}`. **Contrast with debounce is intentional and load-bearing**: debounce *collapses* a key's burst into one run (upsert, one row per key); a throttle *preserves every start* (plain `INSERT`, **many rows per key**, no unique constraint), drained oldest-first by a scanner as tokens refill — nothing is dropped, nothing is rejected. `throttle::fire_due_throttled_starts` mirrors `debounce::fire_due_debounced_starts`'s scanner shape exactly (per-shard `to_regclass` existence probe → one transaction: `SELECT ... ORDER BY deferred_at ASC LIMIT N FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` → per row: expired (`expires_at < now`, from `schedule_to_start`) → drop, **AC**: "a start deferred past `schedule_to_start` times out rather than running stale"; else attempt a token debit → success starts via `start_or_load_workflow_execution_collect(in_outer_transaction=true)` + deletes the row (refunding the token via `queue::refund_rate_limit_token` if the reuse policy resolves to a non-created attach/`AlreadyExists`, satisfying **AC**: "an id-reuse short-circuit consumes no token"); failure leaves the row for the next tick) and is folded into `timeout::enforce_timeouts_once` — no new background task. Migration `20260706000001_harvest_start_throttle` (`workflow_name`, `throttle_key`, `bucket_key`, `workflow_id`, `queue_name`, `input`, `start_options` (reuses `debounce::DebounceStartOptions` verbatim, extended with three additive fields `schedule_id`/`scheduled_for`/`origin` so a throttled *scheduled* fire preserves its carryover (#488) and run-history (#534) lineage across the deferral — debounce/batch construction sites all pass `None` for these, unaffected), `deferred_at`, `expires_at`, `shard_id`; indexes on `deferred_at` (drain order) and `bucket_key` (FIFO-guard probe)); reuses `harvest_rate_limit_buckets` for tokens — **no new bucket table**. **Producer coverage (confirmed design choice — all three, not HTTP-only)**: (1) HTTP `start_workflow` — the pre-existing admission-gate check (issue #377) already runs before this on the same shard, so **halt-beats-pace (AC) is automatic**, no gate-check duplication needed; a throttle admission block sits after gate + before the normal `start_or_load` call, with a mutual-exclusion `400` against debounce/batch (collapse-vs-pace has no coherent combined semantics). (2) Batch-start handler (`api.rs`, best-effort items only — atomic batches are all-or-nothing synchronous and incompatible with deferral) — a deferred item reports the new `BatchStartItemStatus::Deferred` variant (additive, `batch_start.rs`), not a rejection. (3) Scheduler tick + buffered/backfill fire paths (`scheduler.rs`) — a deferred scheduled fire still counts as "dispatched" (advances the slot so it isn't re-fired) with its `schedule_id`/`scheduled_for`/`origin=ORIGIN_SCHEDULED` captured into the pending row. `harvest.workflow.start_throttled` counter (`METRIC_WORKFLOW_START_THROTTLED`, label `workflow` only — the resolved key is high-cardinality tenant input, so per ADR-0001 §7 it is deliberately **not** a label, matching the debounce/batch/DLQ-aggregate precedent; **documented deviation** from the AC's literal `labels: workflow, key` — per-key visibility is instead delivered by the admin read, satisfying the AC's actual intent "count of currently-deferred starts per key observable via an admin read"). Admin read `GET /admin/start-throttle` → `throttle::throttle_backlog_by_key` (per-shard `GROUP BY workflow_name, throttle_key` → `deferred_count`, `oldest_deferred_at`), fanned out across shards, `require_admin`-gated (raw throttle keys resolve from tenant input), registered in `management_api_routes()`/`docs/api-contract.json`/`audit::CLASSIFIED_ROUTES` (`ReadOnly`). Typed-client-stub rejection mirrors debounce's (`HarvestError::Config` pointing at the HTTP route — a deferred start has no `exec_id`-keyed handle for the typed stub to return). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no replay-determinism impact, no shard-routing change** — buckets and pending rows are shard-local (consistent with per-key concurrency #247 and the activity limiter); a **known, documented limitation** (explicitly out of scope per the issue): cross-shard global rate coordination is not attempted, so a key's effective rate across a multi-shard cluster is `per-shard-rate × shards`. Rate strings (`"<count>/<unit>"`, unit ∈ `s`/`m`/`h`) are parsed by a new pure `throttle::parse_rate` (no prior rate-string parser existed in the codebase — the activity limiter takes a raw numeric RPS); `ThrottlePolicy::from_rate_str` defaults `burst` to the rate's per-period count (e.g. `"100/m"` → burst 100) when unset. Example `autumn-harvest/examples/throttle_fanout.rs` (keyed per-tenant throttle, unkeyed/global throttle with `schedule_to_start`, and a non-throttled control workflow). Tests, TDD red→green→refactor: 25 pure unit tests in `throttle.rs` (`parse_rate`/`from_rate_str`/`resolve_throttle_key`/`bucket_key`/`compute_expiry`, no DB); `tests/throttle_tests.rs` (6 Docker-backed integration tests, actually executed in this sandbox: the issue's success metric — a burst of 10 starts against burst-3 admits ≤burst per scanner tick, zero dropped/rejected, all eventually run; durability across a simulated connection-drop "restart"; the id-reuse-short-circuit token refund; the `schedule_to_start` timeout drop; independent per-key throttling; the admin backlog read); `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/start_throttle_integration.rs` (2 Docker-backed HTTP tests: under-limit-then-defer-then-admin-read round trip, and cross-tenant bucket independence over the real HTTP route) — Docker was available in this sandbox (unlike several prior phases) and all 8 DB/HTTP tests were run to completion, not just compile-checked.
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): In-place schedule update — edit cron and input without recreate (issue #771). Harvest's schedule lifecycle had create/pause/resume/backfill/trigger-now/delete but **no edit**: the only way to change a live schedule's cron, input, queue, or overlap/catchup policy was DELETE + re-POST, which mints a new `schedule_id` — and `schedule_id` is load-bearing (#488 carryover lineage, the `sched:{schedule_id}:{name}:{slot}` workflow-id namespace, #534/#762 run-history joins), so a routine cadence tweak silently severed incremental-job lineage and opened a missed-fire window. New management route **`PATCH /api/harvest/admin/schedules/{id}`** (handler `update_schedule_handler` in plugin `api.rs`) applies a **partial** update keyed by the existing row id — UPDATE only, never delete+insert, `schedule_id` never changes. Body fields (ALL optional, only-provided-fields-change, mirroring the create vocabulary): `schedule_expr` (+`timezone` — a timezone-only patch re-anchors the stored cron via the pure `reanchor_schedule_timezone`), `input`, `queue_name`, `catchup`, `max_active_runs`, `jitter_secs`, `overlap_policy` (+`buffer_all_max`), `skip_policy`, `calendar`, `consecutive_failure_limit`, `end_at`, `max_runs`, `catchup_policy` (+`catchup_window_secs` — a window-only patch re-windows an already-`window` policy, ignored otherwise per create's leniency), `retry_policy`. **Tri-state convention for nullable fields** (`calendar`/`consecutive_failure_limit`/`end_at`/`max_runs`/`catchup_policy`/`retry_policy`): absent = unchanged, explicit JSON `null` = clear — serde double-`Option` (`Option<Option<T>>` via the `deserialize_tristate` helper), documented in the contract entry. **Workflow type NOT editable**: `workflow_name` in the body → targeted 400 (the field exists on `UpdateWorkflowScheduleRequest` solely to produce that rejection); DAG schedule rows (`dag_name IS NOT NULL`) → 400 pointing at `PATCH /dags/{dag_name}`. **Core** (`scheduler.rs`): the post-find UPDATE body of `upsert_workflow_schedule` was extracted into a shared `apply_workflow_schedule_update(conn, ws, existing, guard_live_fire_claim)` so the `next_run_at` recompute-on-cadence-change rule, #241 buffered-runs preservation/trim/reset, #484 legacy-`catchup`-bool mirroring, **#478 exhaustion reconciliation in both directions** (raising `max_runs`/`end_at` on an exhausted schedule clears `exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason` and repopulates `next_run_at`; lowering them below `runs_started`/past the next slot exhausts immediately), and the #360 `auto_paused_at` clear rule stay single-source — the upsert path passes `guard=false` and is **byte-identical** to before (proven by the untouched scheduler_bounded_runs/ha/catchup/carryover/auto_pause/schedule_runs suites plus the full 66-test `api_scheduler_integration` HTTP suite). New public API: `WorkflowSchedulePatch` (all-`Option` struct, double-`Option` tri-state), `ScheduleUpdateOutcome` (`Updated(Box<HarvestSchedule>)`/`NotFound`/`DagSchedule`/`ClaimLive`), `update_workflow_schedule(conn, id, patch)` — one transaction: load row by id → reject DAG rows → `merge_schedule_patch` (pure, unit-tested; re-parses the stored expr with the tick loop's lenient rules, reconstructs the stored catchup policy **without** the legacy-bool fallback so an unrelated patch never converts a NULL-policy row, normalizes `max_runs` 0→None) → `validate_schedule` on the **merged** spec **before any write** (invalid cron/tz/zero-interval → `HarvestError::Config` → 400, row byte-for-byte unchanged) → shared apply. **Anti-race with the scheduler tick (#350)** — four cooperating mechanisms (the last three added by post-review hardening, below): (1) the PATCH's guarded UPDATE adds `AND (fire_claim_token IS NULL OR fire_claimed_until < NOW())`, evaluated against the **DB clock** (`diesel::dsl::now`, matching the #350 machinery — never the API host's app clock); 0 rows while the row exists = a fire is in flight → `ClaimLive` → **409** with a "schedule is firing right now … retry shortly" body (the claim lease is ≤30 s). (2) The PATCH's initial row load takes **`FOR UPDATE`**, serializing the whole read-merge-write against the tick's claim/advance/finalize cycle and against peer PATCHes — without it, a full fire cycle committing between the SELECT and the guarded UPDATE would let the merge write back a stale `next_run_at` (re-arming an already-fired slot → double fire), stale `buffered_runs`, and a stale `runs_started` basis. (3) When the guard passes over an **expired** claim (crashed/straggling replica), the PATCH also **nulls `fire_claim_token`/`fire_claimed_until`** so a straggler fire's token-guarded finalize matches 0 rows and can never clobber the edited row with old-spec values — the same fencing a peer claim-steal performs. (4) The tick never fires from its pre-claim due-list snapshot: the claim+fire body was extracted into `#[doc(hidden)] pub claim_and_fire_workflow_schedule(conn, snapshot, …)`, which **re-reads the row fresh after winning the claim** and re-derives input/queue/parsed-schedule/catchup/overlap/retry policies from the fresh row (mirroring the pre-existing fresh `consecutive_failure_count`/`auto_paused_at` re-read for the same stale-snapshot bug class, #360/Codex #1928) — the claim's `next_run_at` guard only fences *cadence* edits (which rewrite `next_run_at`); an input/queue/policy-only edit committing between the due-list SELECT and the claim leaves `next_run_at` unchanged, so the claim still succeeds and only the post-claim re-read stops the slot firing with pre-edit values (AC7 for non-cadence edits — an earlier revision incorrectly claimed the pre-claim window needed no extra work; that reasoning held only for cadence edits). **Identity & bookkeeping preserved**: `is_paused` + pause metadata (editing a paused schedule leaves it paused), `runs_started`, `last_run_at`, `consecutive_failure_count`, and `buffered_runs` (per the shared rules) are never reset; a non-cadence patch preserves the pending `next_run_at`; a cadence change recomputes it anchored at now (elapsed slots are never retroactively fired — backfill remains the explicit tool for that). Validation surface mirrors create exactly: `parse_schedule_expr_with_tz` (new expr + merged tz), `OverlapPolicy`/`SkipPolicy`/`CatchupPolicy::from_user_input` (same 400 messages), calendar existence via `get_calendar` on the owning shard, per-failure best-effort audit rows (`schedule_update_audit_failed`). Success returns 200 with the full `ScheduleEntry` (the construction was deduped into `schedule_entry_from_row`, now shared by list/get/create/patch). Audit op `OP_SCHEDULE_UPDATE = "schedule.update"` registered in `AUDITED_OPERATIONS`/`CLASSIFIED_ROUTES` (Mutating)/`ALL_MUTATION_ROUTES`; route registered in `management_api_routes()`/`request_fields()`/`response_fields()` and `docs/api-contract.json`. CLI: `harvest schedule update <id> [--cron <expr> | --interval-secs <n> | --manual] [--tz] [--input-json] [--queue] [--overlap-policy] [--buffer-all-max] [--catchup-policy] [--catchup-window-secs] [--jitter-secs] [--max-active-runs] [--calendar | --clear-calendar] [--end-at | --clear-end-at] [--max-runs | --clear-max-runs]` — only-provided-flags body, `--clear-*` sends explicit JSON `null`, clap enforces expr-flag and set-vs-clear mutual exclusion. **Zero new `WorkflowEvent` variants, zero `harvest_events` writes, no migration** (all editable columns already existed). Tests, TDD red→green→refactor (red confirmed as compile/runtime failures before implementation): 7 pure `merge_schedule_patch` unit tests in `scheduler.rs`; 12 Docker-executed core DB tests in `tests/integration/schedule_update_tests.rs` (partial merge, cadence-vs-non-cadence `next_run_at`, pause/bookkeeping preservation, exhaustion clear + immediate-exhaust, live-claim `ClaimLive` + row-unchanged, expired-claim proceed, DAG row, unknown id, validate-writes-nothing, tri-state); 9 Docker-executed plugin HTTP tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/schedule_update_integration.rs` incl. the AC11 money test (create → real worker+tick run → PATCH cron+input → id unchanged, #488 carryover from the pre-edit run resolves on the next fire, next fire uses new cadence+input; plus invalid-cron 400 leaves the row byte-identical, workflow_name 400, unknown-calendar 400, 404/400 ids, 409-until-claim-expires, succeeded+failed audit rows, HTTP tri-state null semantics, `GET /admin/schedules` reflects the edit immediately); CLI mapping tests in `request_mapping.rs` (6) + `contract_coverage.rs` coverage/body-field tests. Out of scope (per issue): editing the workflow type, retroactive backfill of pre-edit slots, the DAG `PATCH /dags/{name}` path, a Vantage UI edit form, and versioned schedule history/revert. **Post-review hardening (three-reviewer pass on the initial ship, all fixed TDD-style):** (P1) the pre-claim-snapshot fire described in mechanism (4) above — pinned by `tick_fires_fresh_row_not_pre_claim_snapshot` (core DB test hands the extracted fn a deliberately stale snapshot while the row already carries the edited input/queue, asserts exactly ONE execution starts with the NEW input on the NEW queue, the slot advances, and the claim releases); the extraction leaves the non-edited path byte-identical (verified by re-running the touched scheduler suites: bounded_runs, ha, catchup, carryover, schedule_runs, auto_pause). (P2) `FOR UPDATE` on the PATCH's initial load — pinned by `patch_blocks_on_row_lock_and_applies_against_post_commit_row` (the pause_tests `hold_execution_row_lock` precedent: explicit `BEGIN` on a second connection advances `next_run_at` under the row lock, the PATCH must visibly block, then apply against the post-commit row). (P2) expired-claim token fencing — pinned by `patch_on_expired_claim_fences_straggler_finalize` (seeded expired token, PATCH nulls the claim columns, a finalize UPDATE using the old token matches 0 rows). (P3s) claim-liveness guard moved from `Utc::now()` to `diesel::dsl::now`; `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on `UpdateWorkflowScheduleRequest` with the body extractor switched to `Result<Json<…>, JsonRejection>` → a typo'd field is a structured 400 JSON error with a failed audit row (never axum's plain-text 422, never a silent 200 no-op; serde unit tests pin the tri-state fields still working); the window-only catchup re-window branch's `.ok()` (a latent silent-clear if window validation is ever added) replaced with a match → 400 like its sibling; the pinned `schedule_update_route_is_classified*` guard tests added in both `audit.rs` and `contract_regression.rs` (the audit mutual cross-check can't catch a route dropped from BOTH lists); `input: null` semantics documented (JSON `null` = unchanged — the one JSON value that cannot be set) in the contract field description and the CLI `--input-json` help. **Corrupt-row coercions:** a timezone-only PATCH on a row whose stored `schedule_expr` is NULL/unparseable now returns 400 "stored schedule expression is unparseable" instead of leniently coercing to `manual` and NULLing `next_run_at` as a side effect (a stored `"manual"` expression remains a legitimate tz no-op); `merge_schedule_patch` now errors (`Config` → 400) when an unrelated patch would silently round-trip a corrupt stored `retry_policy` JSON to NULL — repair by explicitly setting or clearing `retry_policy` (unit-tested both ways). **Coverage gaps closed:** write-path tests for every remaining AC2 field (`timezone` cron→`cron_tz` re-anchor with `next_run_at` recompute + tz-on-interval no-op, `queue_name`, `buffer_all_max` incl. the #241 buffered-runs trim, `skip_policy`, legacy `catchup` bool, `consecutive_failure_limit`, window-only re-window, `retry_policy` set AND clear); cadence-kind transitions (cron→manual NULLs `next_run_at`, manual→cron populates it); auto-paused-schedule PATCH (unrelated patch preserves `auto_paused_at`; `consecutive_failure_limit: null` clears it); the AC11 money test strengthened with the AC11c cadence proof (post-edit fire advances `next_run_at` by the NEW 120 s interval), AC10 list assertions on the recomputed `next_run_at` AND the edited input (a new additive `workflow_input` field on `ScheduleEntry`, registered in the response-field registries + contract for the list/get/create/patch schedule-entry routes), the AC6 `sched:{schedule_id}:` workflow-id-prefix pin, and the AC9 zero-`harvest_events`-writes pin; HTTP 400 tests for invalid `overlap_policy`/`skip_policy`/`catchup_policy` values (+ row byte-identical); the dead `load_missing` test contrivance removed and the deliberately-omitted `20260706000001_harvest_start_throttle` INIT_SQL entry documented (the tick's `to_regclass` probe tolerates the missing table).
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Grafana starter dashboard pack for the full metric catalogue — see issue #754. Closes the alert → dashboard → runbook loop the alert pack (Phase 3.8) left open: operators had 60+ catalogued metrics, 18 alert rules, and seed PromQL in `docs/telemetry.md`, but no importable dashboard. New `docs/dashboards/starter-pack-v0.1.0.json`: a **single raw importable Grafana dashboard** (stable `uid: harvest-starter-pack` so re-import upgrades in place, `schemaVersion: 39`, tags `["harvest","starter-pack","v0.1.0"]`), 15 rows / 69 panels — an always-expanded curated **Overview** row (start rate, terminal outcomes, failure ratio, queue depth, schedule-to-start p99, DLQ depth, slot utilization) plus 13 collapsed per-subsystem rows and a final **Readiness checks** row of text panels for the 5 CLI/API-only alert rules (preflight, no-active-workers, worker-saturation, shard-unready, no-compatible-worker) — honest text panels carrying each rule's `first_action`/API/runbook link instead of dishonest proxy metrics. Query conventions: counters always inside `rate()`/`increase()` (`$__rate_interval` for rates, `[1h]`/`[5m]` `increase` for rare-event counters); every `histogram_quantile` panel carries a sibling `_sum`/`_count` **bucket-less fallback target** (metrics-exporter-prometheus emits no `_bucket` series without `set_buckets_for_metric`); gauges read bare with `max by (queue)` on sampler gauges to avoid replica double-count; `key`-labelled series (concurrency/rate-limit) get `topk(10, …)` and deliberately **no** template variable (author-disciplined cardinality). Template variables: `$datasource` (datasource-type — **no hardcoded datasource uid anywhere**), `$workflow`/`$queue`/`$shard` (multi + include-All `label_values` queries) applied **only** to series that carry the label (`$shard` touches exactly `harvest_dlq_entries`/`harvest_retention_deleted`/`harvest_shard_stranded_pending`; `workflow_type`-labelled series use `workflow_type=~"$workflow"`). All 18 alert-pack rules map to panels via `Alert: <rule_id>` panel descriptions plus the 18-row mapping table in the new `docs/dashboards/README.md` (prerequisites — `metrics-rs` feature + `MetricsRsRecorder` + the `set_buckets_for_metric` recipe pointer to `docs/telemetry.md` — import steps, coverage definition, template-variable notes incl. the `$shard` limitation, versioning policy, and the alert pack's “starter defaults, not universal SLOs” framing). **CI-enforced coverage** (the anti-drift device, red-phase-first TDD): `autumn-harvest/tests/integration/dashboard_pack_docs.rs` (15 tests, registered in `tests/integration/mod.rs`, no DB/feature gate) extracts the catalogue **dynamically from `src/telemetry.rs` at test runtime** (≥55-constant sanity floor; + the 2 literal-named concurrency gauges) and asserts: every catalogue metric has a panel; every `harvest_*` token in every expr resolves to a correctly-type-suffixed series from an 82-entry allowlist (counters `_total`, histograms `_bucket`/`_count`/`_sum`, gauges bare — the `docs/alerts/README.md` normalization table); no forbidden/unbounded label (`execution.id`, dotted pre-normalization selectors) in any expr or legend; counters rated, `_bucket` only inside `histogram_quantile` with a same-panel fallback; per-series label-applicability of `workflow=~`/`queue=~`/`shard=~` selectors; unique panel ids/gridPos/titles + schemaVersion ≥ 36 + templated datasource; and — data-driven off the alert pack's `rules[]`, never a hardcoded 18 — every rule id appears in both the README mapping table and the dashboard JSON, and **every rule's runbook anchor resolves** to a real `##` section with the 5 standard subsections (closing the 12-of-18 enforcement gap `alert_pack_docs.rs` left open). **Bug fixes shipped with the slice:** (1) three catalogued metrics were unreachable from a Prometheus scrape because `metrics_rs_adapter.rs` never bridged them, contradicting its own module-doc contract (“All `METRIC_*` names … forwarded verbatim”) — `record_workflow_timeout` (`harvest.workflow.timeout{workflow,queue}` counter), `record_payload_observed` (`harvest.payload.bytes{payload.kind,workflow.type[,activity.name]}` histogram, optional-label two-arm pattern per `record_external_signal_sent`), and `record_payload_rejected` (`harvest.payload.rejected{payload.kind,workflow.type}` counter) are now bridged, with capturing-local-recorder tests in `tests/integration/metrics_rs_adapter.rs` (genuinely red-first — the file's prior “does not panic” style cannot fail for a missing bridge; the capturing recorder also records the *instrument kind* per key, so a histogram silently registered as a gauge fails). **Bridged ≠ emitted (post-review correction):** `harvest.workflow.timeout` is thereby reachable end-to-end (`timeout.rs` already calls the recorder), but `record_payload_observed`/`record_payload_rejected` still have **zero engine emission call sites** — a pre-existing issue #252 gap (the cap sites construct `HarvestError::PayloadTooLarge` without a recorder in a single choke point), so the two payload-cap panels stay empty until emission is wired; the panels and the dashboards README say so, and wiring the emission is a known follow-up; `record_external_cancel_sent` stays a documented no-op (no `METRIC_*` constant exists). (2) The alert pack's `harvest_workflow_failure_rate` rule linked to a **dangling runbook anchor** — `docs/runbooks/harvest-alerts.md` gains the missing `## harvest_workflow_failure_rate` section (5 standard subsections) plus a top-of-file first-responders pointer at the dashboard pack. Cross-links: `docs/telemetry.md` “Full importable dashboard pack” section, `docs/alerts/README.md` install-step pointer, ADR-0001 §7 snapshot-vs-authoritative note (no table rewrite). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no replay surface, no metric renames/relabels** — pure docs artifacts plus the 3-method adapter bridge. Out of scope (per plan): Grafana provisioning/Helm, grafana.com publication, Datadog/CloudWatch packs, recording rules or Grafana-managed alert rules inside the dashboard, Vantage UI work, a `key` template variable, and automated Grafana import in CI.
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): HashMap/HashSet iteration-order determinism guardrail HVG011/DET010 — see issue #785. Closes the guardrail-catalog gap for the classic replay footgun of iterating a `std::collections::HashMap`/`HashSet` inside a `#[workflow]` body: hash-iteration order is randomized per process (`RandomState` seeds differ across workers/restarts), so a replay can visit entries in a different order than the original run, and commands scheduled inside the loop are recorded in history in iteration order — a reordered replay diverges (non-determinism error / #603 nd-block). **ID remap (reconciled with prior art, #543/#593 precedent):** the issue text proposed HVG010/`RuleCategory::NonDeterministicIteration`, but HVG010 was already permanently assigned to SelectMacro (issue #600) and guardrail IDs are never reused — the rule ships as **HVG011** in the guardrail catalog / `#[workflow]` macro lint and **DET010** in `det_check` (DET009 was the prior maximum). Three surfaces kept in sync: (1) `guardrail.rs` — new `RuleCategory::NonDeterministicIteration` variant + HVG011 `RuleEntry` (catalog severity HardBlocker = the class's worst case) + doc-table row; (2) `det_check.rs` — bespoke DET010 pass over extracted workflow bodies (the substring `Rule` table cannot express binding tracking + loop-body extent), wired into the same `check_source`/`check_file`/`check_dir` engine and reusing `DetFinding`/`DetSeverity`/`DetSuppression`/`find_suppression`/`strip_unparseable_content`; (3) `determinism_lint.rs` — syn-AST visitor state (`visit_local` binding tracking, `visit_expr_for_loop` flagging) plus an entry in `load_catalog_metadata()` (explanation/alternative text byte-identical to the guardrail.rs CATALOG, per convention), with a new `add_finding_with_severity` override path that leaves the HVG008 rewrite logic untouched. **Command-aware severity:** both detection surfaces scan the loop body for context-param command calls (`execute_activity*`, `spawn_child_workflow*`, `execute_local_activity*`, `timer`, `side_effect`, honoring a renamed ctx param) — a command-emitting loop is a HardBlocker (compile error / `DetSeverity::Error`); a command-free loop is downgraded to a Warning (HVG009's deprecated-fn mechanism). **Syntactic boundary (deliberately narrow — a HardBlocker false positive breaks user builds, so unknown binding shapes default to NOT flagging):** only locally `let`-bound collections are tracked — hash-typed via explicit type annotation (any path prefix), a `HashMap::new()`/`HashSet::from(..)`/`with_capacity`-variant constructor call, or a `.collect::<HashMap<..>>()` turbofish — with last-binding-wins per ident (re-binding to a non-hash type untracks it); only a bare tracked ident, `&ident`/`&mut ident`, or exactly one argument-free iteration method call (`iter`/`iter_mut`/`keys`/`values`/`values_mut`/`drain`/`into_iter`/`into_keys`/`into_values`) is flagged, so longer chains (`map.keys().sorted()`, `.collect::<Vec<_>>()`) and already-sorted iterators are never flagged, and function parameters/fields are never flagged. BTreeMap/BTreeSet/Vec/IndexMap/arrays are never tracked. **Suppression:** det_check honors `// harvest-suppress: DET010 "reason"` (preceding standalone or same-line trailing comment) and echoes it into `DetCheckReport::suppressions`; the macro path has no per-site suppression — the whole-fn `#[workflow(allow_nondeterministic_apis)]` flag bypasses HVG011 like every other rule (fixture-covered), and iteration inside a `ctx.side_effect(..)` closure is not flagged (the existing closure-traversal skip). Docs: HVG011 section + doc-table updates in `docs/workflow-determinism-guide.md` (before/after migration to BTreeMap or sorted-keys Vec). Tests, TDD red→green→refactor: 15 det_check integration tests (`det_check_tests.rs`), HVG011 catalog presence test (`guardrail_catalog_tests.rs`), 12 visitor unit tests (`determinism_lint.rs::hvg011_tests`), trybuild compile-fail fixture `tests/compile_fail/hvg011_hashmap_iteration.rs` (+ committed `.stderr`), and `suppressed_guardrails.rs` t.pass additions (bypass proof + ordered/sorted negative controls with commands). Zero-false-positive sweep: `cargo check --workspace` compiles every `#[workflow]` in the tree clean under the new lint, and a det_check `check_dir` sweep over `autumn-harvest/src` + both example trees reports 0 DET010 findings (~104 ms). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no runtime code touched** — purely a static-analysis/catalog slice. **Post-review hardening (PR #970, four review agents + Gemini):** the macro lint's flat function-wide binding set was replaced with a lexical scope stack (blocks/match arms/closures/for-patterns push scopes; pattern-bound idents are masked, not deleted — fixes confirmed HardBlocker false positives on pattern-shadowed idents and inner-block binding leaks), and det_check's word-containment binding tracking was replaced with a positional parser (root-type annotation check, real constructor/collect-turbofish detection — `Vec<HashMap<..>>`/`Option<HashMap<..>>`/generic-call-turbofish mentions no longer track) plus depth-scoped, positional-event binding eviction with for-pattern masking (match-arm/closure-param masking stays a documented, test-pinned line-based residual; the multi-line `for`-header miss stays a documented+pinned limitation while multi-line `let` statements are now join-parsed); the loop-body severity scan now starts at the `for` token and uses precomputed per-line brace-delta/marker metadata (adversarial unbalanced-brace input: ~21.6 s → <1 s, closing the <5 s success metric); AC5 is now truly satisfied — `// harvest-suppress: HVG011` is honored as a DET010 alias and echoed as written; `.collect::<Result<HashMap<..>, E>>()?`/`Option`-wrapped turbofish forms are tracked on both surfaces; `HashMap::default()`/`HashSet::default()` constructors and `ctx.race()` as a loop-body command (macro method list + `.race(` det_check marker) were added per Gemini review; a committed `check_dir` self-scan over `autumn-harvest/src` pins AC7; ~30 new pinning tests across `hvg011_tests`/`det_check_tests.rs`.
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Saga compensation observability — first-class, alertable signals for rollback spikes and dangling-state compensation failures — see issue #801. Two new counters: `harvest.saga.compensated` (`METRIC_SAGA_COMPENSATED`; incremented **exactly once per real compensation sequence** — a non-empty `compensate_all`/step-failure unwind actually running forward, counted at unwind start as the earliest downstream-outage signal) and `harvest.saga.compensation_failed` (`METRIC_SAGA_COMPENSATION_FAILED`; exactly once per unwind finishing with ≥1 compensation error — the `HarvestError::SagaCompensationFailed` dangling-state case, **distinct from `harvest.workflow.terminal{outcome=failed}`** so operators can page on it alone). Labels: `workflow` + `queue` only; steps are anonymous closures so no step label exists, and `execution.id` is excluded by construction (ADR-0001 §7). **Exactly-once across replays** (the load-bearing design cost — the documented idempotency contract re-runs compensations wholesale on every replay): each unwind allocates a deterministic per-context `saga_seq` (the `fan_out_seq` idiom; numbers never released) and is keyed to durable dedup markers reusing the **existing** `MarkerRecorded` event variant — `saga_compensated:{seq}` recorded at unwind start (a plain `RecordMarker` bookkeeping command persisted in the same batch as the first compensation's own dispatch, so a worker crash at any point mid-unwind resumes silent) and `saga_compensation_failed:{seq}` at failed-unwind end; the counter fires only on the marker's first live recording (`SagaMarkerMatch::LiveFrontier`), never on replay. New three-state `HistoryMatcher::match_saga_marker` (+ `SagaMarkerMatch` enum, re-exported from `lib.rs`) clones `match_patch_marker`'s tolerant scan discipline (the mechanism built precisely for retrofitting markers): the `Absent` arm is **non-mutating and non-diverging**, so a pre-#801 marker-less saga history — including one captured mid-unwind — replays untouched and uncounted (backward compat pinned by `pre_801_history_mid_unwind_replays_clean_and_uncounted` and replayer fixtures). Emission lives in `Saga::run_compensations` (the single choke point both `compensate_all` and `rollback_after` funnel through) via new `pub(crate) WorkflowContext::observe_saga_unwind_start/observe_saga_unwind_failed` helpers; **in-Saga rather than worker-terminal-boundary emission** is deliberate — it catches author-caught `SagaCompensationFailed` (the workflow goes on to COMPLETE; the worker only ever sees a stringified error) and cancel-and-compensate unwinds in runs that never terminally fail, both structurally invisible at the terminal boundary. New `WorkflowContext.queue_name` field (+ `with_queue_name` builder) threaded from `WorkflowExecuteSpanMeta.queue_name` in `executor.rs` so in-context engine metrics carry the `queue` label. `record_saga_compensated`/`record_saga_compensation_failed` added to `MetricsRecorder` as no-op defaults (additive, no breaking change), bridged in `metrics_rs_adapter`. AC6 by construction: `run_compensations` early-returns on an empty stack (no seq, no marker, no metric) — the success path is byte-identical to pre-#801. Accepted, documented edges (docs/saga.md + docs/telemetry.md): an unwind entered with unconsumed non-marker events at the cursor is conservatively uncounted; a pure in-memory unwind crash-resumed within one decision cycle can re-count (at-least-once — the metric mirror of the re-run contract); forward compat matches every marker feature (a #801+ history nd-blocks under pre-#801 builds; roll forward). Alert pack gains `harvest_saga_compensation_spike` (ticket, baseline-relative rate) and `harvest_saga_compensation_failed` (page on any increase) with full five-subsection runbook entries; ADR-0001 §7 catalogue + docs/telemetry.md updated; docs/saga.md gains an Observability section and the stale `tests/saga_tests.rs` path fix (actual: `tests/integration/saga_tests.rs`). **Zero new `WorkflowEvent` variants, zero migrations, zero worker.rs changes** (the markers ride existing bookkeeping persistence untouched) — the AC7 escape clause (a durable append-only marker) is used and explicitly flagged for spec-owner review in the PR. `Saga` public API unchanged. Tests, TDD red→green: telemetry constant/no-op/bridge unit tests, 5 `match_saga_marker` matcher unit tests (incl. the non-mutating `Absent` and trailing-signal arms), context.rs emit-once/label unit tests, 6 no-DB integration tests in `tests/integration/saga_tests.rs` (crash-replay exactly-once money test, distinct-and-once failure counter, author-caught emission, AC6 success/empty paths, legacy mid-unwind backward compat), 3 `WorkflowReplayer` fixtures in `replayer_tests.rs` (marker-bearing + pre-marker histories replay clean; replay emits zero samples), `metrics_coverage.rs` cardinality test, alert-pack doc-test enforcement (`REQUIRED_ALERTS` + `STABLE_PROMETHEUS_METRICS` extended), and the Docker-gated e2e `worker_saga_unwind_emits_compensated_counter_exactly_once_across_decision_cycles` in `integration_e2e.rs` (activity-backed compensations across real decision cycles; counter == 1, each compensation activity ran once, exactly one marker in history) — compile-checked in this sandbox (no Docker), executed by CI's Docker-backed step, per the #543/#544/#601 precedent. **Post-review hardening (three-angle adversarial review — replay determinism, test quality, idioms/docs — applied before merge):** the root cause of both replay P2s was **two independent positional marker matches for one logical unwind**, whose signal-drain side effects changed each other's pre-drain view of the same code position. Fixed by resolving the unwind's disposition **once** at unwind start and coupling the failure counter to it: `observe_saga_unwind_start` now returns a `pub(crate) SagaUnwindObservation { seq, counted }` that `observe_saga_unwind_failed` follows, establishing the per-unwind invariant **`failed ≤ compensated`**. `SagaMarkerMatch` gained a fourth variant, `DrainedSignalFrontier` (post-drain past-end — only trailing un-awaited signals stood between the cursor and the frontier), and `match_saga_marker` gained a bounded, non-destructive lookahead that treats command-less `WorkflowCancelled` lifecycle events as transparent (finding a marker past them consumes it out-of-order via `consumed_out_of_order_events`, the `deprecate_patch` mechanism; reaching the frontier past only cancels reports `LiveFrontier`). Concretely: (1) **P2-1 fixed** — a trailing un-awaited signal at the failed-unwind end (e.g. a retried cancel webhook ingested at the final unwind cycle's wake) no longer permanently suppresses the page-severity `compensation_failed` counter for a counted unwind; the failure marker is recorded past the drained signal, replay-consistently (`trailing_unawaited_signal_does_not_suppress_the_failure_counter`, `replayer_succeeds_for_failed_unwind_history_with_trailing_signal`).
