(2) **P2-2 fixed** — the signal-with-start shape can no longer produce `failed=1, compensated=0` or an asymmetric marker set: an unwind entered at a drained-signal frontier is conservatively uncounted **as a whole** (the `ctx.patched()` signal-with-start caveat, inherited and now documented; `signal_with_start_shape_keeps_the_failed_counter_coupled_to_compensated`). (3) **Genuine discovery beyond the reviews** — extending the pre-existing cancel-pattern test with a recorder (the reviews' mutant-k pin) exposed that the canonical cancel-and-compensate unwind was **actually uncounted** (not merely unpinned): the never-consumed trailing `WorkflowCancelled` resolved the marker match to `Absent`, contradicting the docs' load-bearing claim; the cancellation-transparency lookahead above makes the claim true (`saga_compensate_all_on_cancel_pattern` extended with `count == 1` + marker assertions; `observe_saga_unwind_counts_the_cancel_and_compensate_frontier`; `replayer_succeeds_for_cancel_and_compensate_history_with_marker`). (4) **Seq-counter pin** (review mutant l, previously surviving the whole suite): `two_saga_unwinds_in_one_workflow_count_independently` drives two unwinds through one context asserting distinct `saga_compensated:1`/`saga_compensated:2` markers, counter == 2, and a zero-emission replay — mutants k, l, and j were each re-applied and confirmed caught. (5) The `metrics_rs_adapter` saga bridge test was upgraded from the file's no-panic smoke idiom to a real label-content assertion via `metrics::with_local_recorder` (metrics 0.24), catching a swapped/dropped label in the bridge. (6) `WorkflowTestEnv` gained `with_workflow_name`/`with_queue_name` builders (threaded through `run_workflow_with_state_advancing_clock`'s span-meta plumbing, mirroring the caps variant) so harness users can assert real metric label content; the e2e recorder was converted to record-then-assert so a label regression reads as a clear assertion failure instead of an in-worker poison-pill panic. (7) Docs hardened: the crash-window caveat generalized to both counters and durable unwinds alike (emit→persist gap of one decision cycle; the #383 pause-race decision discard documented as the same at-least-once class), the runbook's "re-driving re-runs the compensation" safe-action scoped to in-memory closures only (durable activity-backed compensations replay their recorded `ActivityFailed` — remediation is manual reconciliation or reset #148), `--workflow` corrected to `--workflow-name` in the four new alert/runbook occurrences, and the ADR §7 rows trimmed to the neighbor-row label shape. No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration — the hardening is entirely matcher/observe-layer plus tests and docs. **Post-review hardening, round 2 (PR #973 Codex bot review, P2 — verified and reproduced):** the cancellation-transparency lookahead (item 3 above) made `match_saga_marker` report `LiveFrontier` for a **pre-#801 marker-less cancelled history** whose terminal event is the trailing `WorkflowCancelled` — byte-identical, at the matcher level, to the genuinely-live cancel-and-compensate cycle — so a `WorkflowReplayer` strict/canary probe of such a history retroactively emitted `harvest.saga.compensated` AND pushed a fresh `RecordMarker` command mid-replay, which strict mode correctly flagged as `NonDeterminismDetected(expected "<no new commands>", got "<new commands emitted>")` (reproduced red-first at both the replayer and context levels). The matcher *cannot* distinguish the two cases from history alone; the reliable, deterministic signal is the context's execution mode: the engine never sets `strict_replay`/`canary_mode` (a terminal execution gets no further decision cycles, so an engine-side `LiveFrontier` at a cancel frontier is always genuinely live — including the documented at-least-once crash-recovery re-count), while every `WorkflowReplayer` context sets them. Fixed with a `const fn WorkflowContext::is_replay_probe()` (`strict_replay || canary_mode`) gating the frontier-recording arms of `observe_saga_unwind_start`/`observe_saga_unwind_failed`: in a probe, `LiveFrontier`/`DrainedSignalFrontier` resolve to uncounted/silent/command-free (a probe is a pure read), while `Recorded` still resolves `counted=true` so the failure counter's coupling sees the same disposition the engine saw. All three genuinely-live cancel tests and all marker-bearing replay fixtures stay green. Regression tests: `replayer_pre_marker_cancelled_history_stays_uncounted` (replayer fixture — ReplaySucceeded, zero samples), `observe_saga_unwind_is_silent_in_a_strict_replay_probe_at_the_cancel_frontier` and `observe_saga_unwind_probe_still_reads_recorded_markers_as_counted` (context.rs); docs/saga.md's cancel-and-compensate bullet gains the probe-exception sentence. **Post-review hardening, round 3 (PR #973 Codex bot review of the round-2 commit, P2 — mechanics confirmed, implication refuted with baseline evidence; docs + pin test, no code change):** Codex flagged that when a cancelled workflow reaches an **activity-backed** compensation, the lookahead's cancel-transparency is saga-marker-local — `observe_saga_unwind_start` counts the unwind and queues its marker, but the cursor stays on the unconsumed `WorkflowCancelled`, so the very next `execute_activity_raw` diverges (`expected ActivityScheduled(..), got WorkflowCancelled`) and no durable compensation is ever scheduled (“counted an unwind that cannot run forward”). Investigated empirically before deciding: the identical flow was run against **pristine trunk-dev (pre-#801)** in a worktree, and it fails with the **byte-identical** error — `SagaCompensationFailed { compensation_errors: ["non-deterministic replay: activity mismatch: expected ActivityScheduled(cancel_flight), got WorkflowCancelled"] }` — because `match_activity`/`execute_activity_raw` are untouched by this branch. **Durable compensation after a recorded `WorkflowCancelled` is a pre-existing engine limitation** (cancellation is a terminal seal; only in-memory compensation closures can execute post-cancel), not something the lookahead introduced or worsened. Codex's implied fix — consuming the skipped `WorkflowCancelled` so `match_activity` sees the frontier — would be an engine-semantics change (letting a sealed run schedule new durable work, converting an immediate visible error into a forever-parked coroutine / dead task row for a CANCELLED execution) and touches worker-layer territory; the alternative — not counting at a cancel frontier — is unscopeable (closures are opaque; unwind-start cannot know a compensation is durable) and would uncount the *working* in-memory cancel-and-compensate population, regressing the round-1 mutant-k fix. Resolution: **count-at-unwind-start-by-design stands, and the counter pair is truthful** — such an unwind emits `compensated=1` (it began; the closures were invoked) *and* `compensation_failed=1` (it failed; the state is genuinely dangling and the page-severity alert firing is exactly correct — the same signature as any unwind whose first durable compensation fails, cancel or not), with `failed ≤ compensated` intact. Pinned by `known_limitation_durable_compensation_after_recorded_cancel_fails_and_is_counted` (saga_tests.rs, with the trunk-dev baseline evidence in its doc comment); docs/saga.md gains a “Known limitation — durable compensations cannot run after cancellation” section (in-memory vs durable vs step-failure-rollback scoping) and a “Cancel + durable compensation” bullet in the per-unwind-coherence list. Follow-up candidate (not this slice): classify the post-cancel dispatch divergence as a typed `HarvestError::Cancelled` instead of `NonDeterministic` so author code can catch it naturally. **Round-4 hardening (Codex review round 3 on PR #973, two doc/alert-pack findings):** the spike alert's baseline-relative PromQL now defaults an absent baseline to a zero-valued, label-matched series (`or <current> * 0`) so a new/long-dormant workflow's FIRST rollback wave still trips the absolute 1/min floor (pinned textually by `alert_pack_docs.rs::saga_spike_alert_absolute_floor_fires_without_a_baseline`; no other alert in the pack uses the baseline-relative pattern, so nothing else shares the bug), and the `compensation_failed` first-triage guidance (JSON `first_action`/`management_checks` + runbook triage step 2) now also covers author-caught unwinds in COMPLETED runs (`harvest workflow list --state COMPLETED --workflow-name <name> --limit 20` plus searching history for the `saga_compensation_failed:` marker / `SagaCompensationFailed` error string) so the operator always gets an execution id to inspect.
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Idempotency key on plain signal delivery — reconciled with prior art, gaps closed — see issue #753. **Issue #753 asks for the capability issue #521 (Phase 3.36) already shipped**: the plain `POST /workflows/{id}/signal/{signal_name}` route accepts an out-of-band exactly-once key via the `Idempotency-Key` header (wins) or `?idempotency_key=` query param (`signal_workflow` in `autumn-harvest-plugin/src/api.rs`; empty/non-UTF-8 header → 400, never silent degradation), backed by `signal::send_signal_idempotent` (`.on_conflict_do_nothing()` against the pre-existing `uq_harvest_signals_idem` partial unique index — dedupe scope `(workflow_exec_id, idempotency_key)`, exactly matching the issue's AC), a `202 { ok, signal_delivered }` response distinguishing a fresh queue (`true`) from a deduped replay (`false`, still 2xx), byte-for-byte legacy at-least-once behavior when the key is omitted (a `NULL` key is excluded from the partial index), terminal-execution semantics preserved with one **deliberate carve-out** (`signal.rs`: the keyed insert deliberately runs *before* the state validation) — an unkeyed signal or a fresh-keyed signal to a terminal execution keeps the existing terminal/404 error semantics (the fresh insert rolls back with the error), while a keyed **retry** whose key already landed while the run was active dedupes to a no-op `202 { signal_delivered: false }` even after the run has since gone terminal (the retry acknowledges a delivery that already happened), the typed-stub sibling `signal_{name}_idempotent` generated by the `#[signal]` macro, and the untyped client call `send_signal_idempotent` itself — all covered by the existing DB tests in `tests/integration/signal_tests.rs` (same-key→1 row, distinct keys→N, unkeyed→N, per-execution scoping, plain `send_signal` unchanged) and the HTTP tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/signal_with_start_integration.rs` (header dedupe, query-param dedupe, header-wins, unkeyed at-least-once, empty-header 400). Following the #543/#593/#609 precedent, issue #753 was resolved by mapping its ACs onto the existing #521 implementation rather than re-deriving it. **Two genuine gaps found and closed, TDD red→green** (red = clap rejecting the unknown flag, verbatim test failures captured): (1) **CLI parity** — the issue's client-parity AC names three non-HTTP surfaces and only the CLI was missing: `harvest workflow signal <exec-id> <name> --idempotency-key <key>` now maps onto the `?idempotency_key=` query param of the same contract route (RFC 3986 query-encoded via the existing `query_encode` helper; the body stays the raw signal payload, matching the issue's "the key must not be smuggled into the payload" constraint; omitting the flag sends no param at all, preserving at-least-once). An empty/whitespace-only `--idempotency-key` is **rejected at clap parse time** (`parse_idempotency_key` value-parser, mirroring the `--percent` precedent) rather than silently degraded: the server treats an empty `?idempotency_key=` as omitted, so passing it through would turn an intended exactly-once delivery into at-least-once — exactly the failure the server's header path 400s on. Tests: four mapping tests in `autumn-harvest-cli/tests/integration/request_mapping.rs` (key→query param, reserved-character encoding, omission sends nothing, empty/whitespace key rejected) plus `workflow_signal_with_idempotency_key_is_covered` in `contract_coverage.rs` (route-coverage + flag parsing; the wire shape is pinned by the mapping tests, since `assert_covered` strips the query string). CI gap closed alongside (the #687-hardening-item-7 class): `.github/workflows/ci.yml` previously never ran the CLI crate's tests or clippy on PRs — new `cargo test -p autumn-harvest-cli` and `cargo clippy -p autumn-harvest-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings` steps added. (2) **Docs** — the issue names `06-idempotency.md` + the management-API reference as the documentation home (and explicitly rules out `docs/search-attributes.md`, which was never touched): new "Idempotent signal delivery" section in `docs/getting-started/06-idempotency.md` enumerating all five surfaces (HTTP header/param, CLI flag, typed stub, untyped call, signal-with-start for the first-delivery case) with cross-links; new "Signal delivery" section in `docs/management-api.md` (idempotent-delivery contract, response shape, CLI usage); CLI-parity subsection + cross-links added to the existing #521 section of `docs/getting-started/04-signals.md`. The `docs/api-contract.json` entry already described key-based idempotency (header + query param, `signal_delivered` response field) from #521 — no contract change needed. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no core/plugin code change — this slice is CLI + docs only.**
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Replay-safe custom business metrics from workflow code — reconciled with prior art, gaps closed — see issue #758. **Issue #758 asks for the capability issue #532 (Phase 3.39) already shipped**: `ctx.metrics().counter/gauge/histogram` on both `WorkflowContext` and `ActivityContext`, suppressed when `ctx.is_replaying()` (workflow side) and never suppressed on activities (each retry attempt emits — the issue's at-least-once-per-attempt AC), routed through three additive no-op-default `MetricsRecorder` methods (`record_user_counter/gauge/histogram`) bridged in `metrics_rs_adapter`, zero overhead when telemetry is off (`is_enabled()` short-circuit), auto-namespaced under `harvest.user.*` so `harvest.*` engine dashboards are never polluted, bounded `&[(&str, &str)]` label slices with `validate_user_metric` rejecting the high-cardinality keys (`execution.id`/`workflow.id`/… per ADR-0001 §7), and the crash-replay best-effort at-least-once caveat already documented honestly in `docs/telemetry.md` ("At-least-once in crash-recovery scenarios" callout). Following the #543/#593 precedent, the issue's literal method names (`ctx.record_counter`/`record_histogram`) were reconciled onto the shipped `ctx.metrics()` handle rather than adding alias methods — the AC's actual bar (one line, replay-correct, both contexts, no new event variant) is met verbatim by the existing API; likewise the namespace AC's suggested `app.` prefix is satisfied by the shipped, documented `harvest.user.` convention. **Three genuine gaps found and closed, TDD red→green** (red = compile error, per the #593 precedent — the tests referenced the fixture/recorder before they existed): (1) **The falsifiable replay-counting proof** the issue's success metric demands did not exist anywhere (no test in the repo used `WorkflowReplayer::with_metrics` or `record_user_counter`): new section in `tests/integration/replayer_tests.rs` with a `CountingMetrics` recorder (asserts the `harvest.user.` prefix on every emission) and a one-activity-then-one-counter-plus-one-histogram fixture — `live_execution_emits_workflow_counter_exactly_once` (via `WorkflowTestEnv::with_metrics`; the env's multiple executor iterations still net exactly 1 counter and 1 histogram sample), `five_cycle_replay_emits_zero_workflow_metrics` (5 `WorkflowReplayer` cycles over a recorded history containing **no** metric event → `ReplaySucceeded` every cycle, 0 counter and 0 histogram emissions — 0 double-counts across ≥5 cycles, the issue's exact bar), and `live_loop_hitting_increment_twice_emits_exactly_two` (a legitimate two-iteration live loop emits exactly 2 — no over-suppression). Runs in the existing no-DB CI step (`--no-default-features --features testing --test integration`). (2) **`docs/workflow-determinism-guide.md`** still carried the pre-#532 guidance the issue explicitly asks to replace: the HVG007 "Emit metrics/updates inside activities, not in workflow code" allowed-row now reads "`ctx.metrics().counter(...)`/`ctx.metrics().histogram(...)` (replay-safe) for workflow-body metrics", plus a paragraph covering replay suppression, per-attempt activity semantics, the `harvest.user.*` namespace, the cardinality anti-pattern, and the crash-replay at-least-once caveat, pointing at `docs/telemetry.md` and the new example. (3) **Example** — new `autumn-harvest/examples/business_metrics.rs` (registered as a plain `[[example]]`): a counter emitted from a workflow **branch** (the auto-reject orchestration decision an activity can't observe) and a histogram + counter from an activity, with three embedded `#[cfg(test)]` tests (happy-path `orders_fulfilled` counter exactly-once via `WorkflowTestEnv::with_metrics`, 5-cycle replay-of-recorded-history → 0 emissions via `WorkflowReplayer::with_metrics` over `outcome.events()`, auto-reject branch counter exactly-once) mirroring the `current_details_status.rs` example-test pattern, and executed in CI by a new `cargo test -p autumn-harvest --features testing --example business_metrics` step. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no production code change — this slice is tests + docs + example only.** Out of scope per the issue and untouched: gauges with lifetime/callback semantics (the shipped push-style `gauge` stays as-is), exactly-once-across-crash delivery, per-`execution.id` labels, app-metric dashboards (#754).
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Output-guarded completion triggers — gate the fire decision on the source workflow's output content — see issue #810. Completion triggers (#517) previously fired on terminal *state* only; the most common pipeline dependency is conditional ("run high-value fulfillment only when `amount > 1000`"), forcing either a throwaway target run that no-ops on its own input or re-coupling the branch back into the upstream workflow. New **bounded, declarative `TriggerCondition` AST** in `completion_trigger.rs` (compiled OUTSIDE the `db` cfg — pure-unit-testable in the no-default-features suite), adjacently tagged `#[serde(tag="type", content="data")]` mirroring `InputMapping`: leaf operators `Eq`/`NotEq`/`GreaterThan`/`GreaterThanOrEq`/`LessThan`/`LessThanOrEq`/`In`/`Exists`/`IsNull` over a dotted JSON path (the same `project_json_path` machinery `InputMapping::Projection` uses — refactored onto a new borrowing `project_json_path_opt` that distinguishes missing (`None`) from present-null (`Some(Null)`), behavior of the public fn unchanged), composing with `All`/`Any`/`Not`. An unknown operator is rejected with the documented `400` JSON error by the handler's explicit `decode_trigger_condition` (the request carries `condition` as raw JSON — a typed field would hand the failure to axum's `Json` extractor, which surfaces data-shape errors as a `422` with a plain-text body). **Pinned evaluation semantics** (doc-commented on the enum): missing path OR explicit null ⇒ every comparison operator is `false` (absence/nullness tested only via `Exists`/`IsNull`/`Not(Exists)`); `Exists` = path present incl. explicit null, `IsNull` = present AND null — so the two genuinely distinguish null from missing; numeric comparison is **integer-exact** for int/int pairs (`i128` fast path over the full `i64`/`u64` range incl. mixed signs — distinct integers above 2^53, e.g. snowflake IDs, never collapse) and coerces both sides to `f64` only when at least one side is a genuine float (`1 == 1.0`; mixed int/float follows `f64` precision), else strict `Value` equality; ordering operators are numeric-only (else `false`); `All([])` = true, `Any([])` = false; a `NULL` source output (Failed/Cancelled sources) evaluates as literal JSON null at the root, so comparisons are false but `Not(Exists(..))` guards can meaningfully fire (guards are output-only by design — the failure-cause envelope is #748's story). **Boundedness caps** (`TriggerCondition::validate`, enforced at BOTH registration surfaces so a hostile registration can never DoS the terminal-commit path): `MAX_CONDITION_DEPTH = 8`, `MAX_CONDITION_NODES = 64`, `MAX_CONDITION_IN_VALUES = 64`, plus malformed-dotted-path rejection (empty segments); builder → new `HarvestBuilderError::InvalidCompletionTriggerCondition` from `validate_completion_triggers`, HTTP → `400` in `create_completion_trigger`. `CompletionTrigger.condition: Option<TriggerCondition>` (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if)]` — legacy JSON round-trips unchanged) + fluent `with_condition(...)`; `TriggerCondition`/`ConditionGate`/cap constants re-exported from `lib.rs`. **Evaluation slot**: in `evaluate_triggers_for_execution`, right after the terminal-state match and BEFORE input mapping/schema validation — the guard reads the RAW `execution.output` (never the mapped target input) so #748 can change input assembly without touching guard semantics; the pure `gate_stored_condition(Option<&Value>, &Value) -> ConditionGate {Pass|Unmet|Invalid}` is the whole decision. **Exactly-once skip recording (AC5)**: an **unmet** condition-skip inserts the `(source_exec_id, trigger_id)` fires row through the SAME `on_conflict_do_nothing` PK path as a real fire, with the reason in the new additive `outcome TEXT NULL` column on `harvest_completion_trigger_fires` (`NULL` = fired — every pre-#810 row; `condition_unmet` = resolved-skipped; `fired_at` on a skip row records the resolution time), so cascade re-entry (#605's re-evaluation) dedupes a skip identically to a fire and a skipped terminal can never late-fire — even if the condition is later loosened; a redelivered resolved skip records the existing `deduped` outcome on the fires counter. **Fail-closed on invalid conditions**: an unparseable or over-cap stored condition (e.g. a row written by a different build during a mixed-version deploy) skips with the distinct reason `condition_invalid` + a `tracing::warn!` and writes **no fires row** (mirroring the `validation_failed` precedent) — never fires on an unintelligible guard, never errors the terminal commit, and never *durably* resolves the pair (an `Unmet` decision is final because it is a correct, pure function of recorded output; an `Invalid` one is "this binary cannot understand the guard"). Honest recovery contract (post-review round 2 below): evaluation runs only inside a source terminal commit and no scanner re-visits unresolved pairs, so the fire is **lost for that terminal** unless some later path re-enters evaluation for the same execution (rare, not guaranteed) — the warn + counter are the operator's re-trigger-by-hand detection signal, and conditions using newly added operators should be registered only after the whole fleet runs the new binary (`docs/completion-triggers.md` "Fail-closed on invalid stored conditions"); a durable re-evaluation queue is a named follow-up. Deliberate deviation from the `unwrap_or_default()` leniency of `terminal_states`/`input_mapping` parsing, documented here. **Observability (AC4)**: NEW counter `harvest.completion_trigger.skipped{trigger, reason}` (`METRIC_COMPLETION_TRIGGER_SKIPPED`, no-op `MetricsRecorder::record_completion_trigger_skipped` default + `metrics_rs_adapter` bridge — the three-touchpoint recipe, with the constant asserted in `metric_name_constants_have_correct_names` and a dedicated adapter no-panic test per the #519/#606 pattern) — deliberately NOT an `outcome` on the existing fires counter, whose `outcome="skipped"` already means workflow-id-reuse dedupe. Counter coverage: emitted on the worker, timeout-scanner, poison-pill, history-cap, and workflow-task-timeout terminal paths (the latter three gained metrics threading in the post-review pass); on the operator cancel/terminate and parent-close-cascade paths in `execution.rs` no recorder is in scope (threading one would re-plumb the metrics-less cancel/terminate/cascade call graph), so there the counter is best-effort and the fires-row `outcome` column is the authoritative skip record. Storage: migration `20260708000001_harvest_completion_trigger_condition` (additive only: `condition JSONB NULL` on `harvest_completion_triggers` + `outcome TEXT NULL` on `harvest_completion_trigger_fires`); all FOUR upsert/rollback `.set()` column lists updated (`sync_completion_triggers` + the create handler's upsert + its two rollback-restore lists — a missed one would silently drop conditions on trigger update); `condition` registered in `management_api_request_fields()`/`management_api_response_fields()` and `docs/api-contract.json`; all 72 hand-rolled test INIT_SQL bundles that include the 20260603 trigger migration updated (scripted, #601/#603 precedent; `integration_e2e.rs`'s `LEGACY_INIT_SQL` verified to carry no trigger tables — no change needed). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no replay surface, no shard-semantics change** — this is a server-side pre-start gate evaluated inside the existing terminal transaction; guard evaluation is an in-memory JSON walk on an already-loaded row (< 1ms trivially). Docs: `docs/completion-triggers.md` "Conditional Triggers — Output Guards" section (operator table, JSON example, semantics, caps, fail-closed + exactly-once-skip contracts); `docs/telemetry.md` metric + label rows. Tests, TDD red→green (red confirmed as compile errors per the #593 precedent): 16 pure unit tests in `completion_trigger.rs` (every leaf operator hit/miss/missing/null, combinator nesting incl. empty identities, numeric coercion incl. integer-exactness above 2^53 / mixed-sign / bool-vs-number / mid-path-scalar traversal, adjacent-tagging serde round-trip, unknown-operator rejection, cap boundaries at exactly-cap vs cap+1 for both `All` and `Not` chains, malformed paths incl. nested, non-object/string/array/null outputs, `gate_stored_condition` truth table incl. stored JSON-null → `Invalid`, `with_condition` + legacy-serde-default); builder rejection test `harvest_builder_rejects_invalid_trigger_condition` (`builder.rs`); plugin unit test `create_completion_trigger_request_condition_serde` (`api.rs`); telemetry constant assertions for both trigger counters + `record_completion_trigger_skipped_does_not_panic_for_all_reasons` adapter bridge test; 3 DB integration tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/completion_triggers_integration.rs` (`test_condition_gate_fire_skip_and_exactly_once` — met-fires + unmet-skips + missing-path-skips + legacy-control-fires + redelivery-stays-skipped + skip-then-loosen-never-late-fires with fires-row `outcome` and both counters asserted via a capturing recorder; `test_invalid_stored_condition_fails_closed` — garbage condition written directly to the column: no fires row, counter emitted, then repaired-condition re-entry fires; `test_condition_registration_rejects_invalid_with_400` — unknown operator/over-cap/malformed path all `400`, valid condition echoes through POST+GET, re-POST without `condition` clears the guard) — compile-checked in this sandbox (no Docker daemon) and executed in CI, which gained a `completion_triggers_integration` compile step + Docker-backed Linux run step (the #608 `read_path_decode_integration` precedent). **Post-review hardening (PR #972 review, five findings fixed):** (1) integer-exact comparison fast path in `values_eq`/`values_cmp` (was: unconditional f64 coercion collapsing distinct >2^53 integers — a wrong-fire vector on snowflake-style IDs); (2) `condition_invalid` no longer writes a permanent fires row (was: a mixed-version deploy window could permanently suppress a pair; now left unresolved, mirroring `validation_failed` — recovery contract honesty-checked in round 2 below); (3) metrics threaded into the poison-pill, history-cap, and workflow-task-timeout trigger-evaluation sites so AC4's skip counter emits there (the `execution.rs` cancel/terminate/cascade sites remain best-effort, documented); (4) the claimed-but-missing telemetry constant assertion + adapter bridge test now exist, and the pure-test count was corrected; (5) `completion_triggers_integration` added to CI (it was never compiled or executed by any CI step). Plus: `gate_stored_condition` deserializes via `&Value` (no per-trigger clone at terminal commit), is exported from `lib.rs` alongside `ConditionGate`, and the docs gained the coercion/`NotEq`-vs-`Not(Eq)`/full-replacement-upsert/`fired_at`-resolution-time/counter-coverage clarifications. **CI fix pass (first Docker-backed run of the new suite):** `test_condition_registration_rejects_invalid_with_400` failed on the unknown-operator leg — the request struct typed `condition: Option<TriggerCondition>`, so the failure happened inside axum's `Json` extractor, which rejects data-shape errors (`JsonRejection::JsonDataError`) with **422 Unprocessable Entity** and a plain-text body (400 is only for JSON *syntax* errors), contradicting the documented 400-JSON contract. Fixed at the root rather than by relaxing the test: `condition` is now `Option<serde_json::Value>` on the request, decoded + validated by the new pure `decode_trigger_condition` helper in the handler, so every invalid-condition shape (unknown operator, over-cap, malformed path) uniformly returns the documented `400` JSON error; the `api.rs` unit test now pins the helper (including that the request-level deserialization ACCEPTS the raw unknown-operator JSON so the extractor can never 422 it), and the docs' "serde error → 400 for free" claims were corrected. **Post-review hardening, round 2 (PR #972 Codex review, P1): "retryable" over-claimed the `condition_invalid` recovery contract — docs made honest, remedies deliberately scoped out.** Codex correctly observed that the `condition_invalid` branch logs + `continue`s while the surrounding terminal transaction commits, and — verified by auditing every `evaluate_triggers_for_execution` call site (`worker.rs`, `timeout.rs`, `execution.rs` cancel/terminate/parent-close-cascade, `poison_pill.rs`) — every one is gated on the execution row *just* transitioning to terminal (`updated == 0` returns before evaluation), so there is **no guaranteed redelivery** for an already-terminal execution: absent a rare re-entry race, a fire skipped during a mixed-version window is genuinely lost for that terminal even after the fleet upgrades or the row is repaired. Calling the skip "retryable" therefore over-claimed. The finding's suggested *remedies* were deliberately rejected: rolling back / failing the terminal transaction would let a corrupt trigger condition wedge unrelated workflow closes (the one invariant the fail-closed design exists to protect), and a new pending-re-evaluation table + scanner is out of proportion for this slice with issue #748 stacking on the same file — a durable re-evaluation queue is instead a named follow-up in the PR. Behavior is unchanged (no fires row remains correct — it is what keeps a re-entry, when one happens, able to fire); what changed is the documented contract, everywhere it was stated: `ConditionGate::Invalid` rustdoc + the evaluation-slot comment + the `tracing::warn!` (now also naming `source_exec_id` so an operator can re-trigger by hand from the log line alone) in `completion_trigger.rs`, `docs/completion-triggers.md`'s fail-closed section (rewritten with the honest "lost unless evaluation re-enters" contract plus detection guidance — alert on `skipped{reason="condition_invalid"}` — and prevention guidance — the mixed-version window is new-binary registrations racing old-binary workers, so register conditions using newly added operators only after the whole fleet runs the new binary), `docs/telemetry.md`'s catalogue row, the `test_invalid_stored_condition_fails_closed` doc comments (its manual second evaluation pins the "a re-entry can still fire" half, not an automatic retry), and this entry's own two "retryably" claims above. **Post-review hardening, round 3 (PR #972 Codex review, P1): pre-#810 workers fire guarded triggers unconditionally — rollout-ordering contract documented, registration gating rejected as unreliable.** The finding's observation is correct, verified by code reading: a pre-#810 worker binary's Diesel DSL query loads `harvest_completion_triggers` with an explicit compiled-in column list that simply omits `condition` (the new column is invisible to it, not an error), and its `evaluate_triggers_for_execution` has no gate — so during a rolling deploy where the API node upgrades before the workers, a guarded trigger registered in that window is fired **unconditionally** by any old worker that commits the source's terminal, with zero telemetry (that binary never sees the guard: no warn, no skip counter, no fires-row `outcome`) — strictly worse than the round-2 `condition_invalid` window, which at least fails closed and is detectable. The finding's suggested remedies were assessed and rejected: "make older workers fail closed" is impossible by definition (those binaries are already built and deployed), and "reject guarded registrations until the worker fleet is known to run this build" has no reliable substrate — `harvest_workers.build_id` is an optional, free-form operator label (often empty) with no mapping to "understands guard operator X", heartbeat rows go stale, the check would be per-shard, and a pre-#810 worker can join the fleet the moment after any registration-time check passes — so the gate would be theater rather than protection, and it would also be a novel fleet-state-gating primitive that no other additive behavioral column in this repo's history has needed (#605 completion callbacks, #487 sla, #499 debounce, #484 catchup all shipped with documented rollout ordering instead). Fix (docs-layer, matching that precedent): new "Rollout ordering — guards are enforced by workers, not at registration" section in `docs/completion-triggers.md` stating the general contract — a guarded trigger is only enforced by workers running a build that understands its condition; **upgrade the entire worker fleet first, then register guarded triggers** — distinguishing the two mixed-version windows (initial pre-#810 rollout: fire-unconditionally, undetectable; future new operators: `condition_invalid`, fail-closed, detectable), why registration gating is deliberately not attempted, and a cross-reference from the round-2 Prevention bullet. No code change: there is no reliable enforcement point, and an unreliable one would be worse than an honest contract. **Same round, P2 (valid, fixed): two rebase-inherited test fixtures missing the condition migration.** The "all 72 hand-rolled INIT_SQL bundles updated" scripted sweep predated the rebase onto trunk-dev `f6708b7`, which added two new fixtures (`autumn-harvest/tests/integration/schedule_update_tests.rs`, `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/schedule_update_integration.rs` — issue #771's schedule-update slice) that include the 20260603 trigger migration without `20260708000001_harvest_completion_trigger_condition` — since `CompletionTriggerDb` loads now select the `condition` column explicitly, any worker-driven completion in those suites would fail with `column ... does not exist` and roll back the terminal commit. Both fixtures gained the migration include (placed directly after the 20260603 line, matching every sibling bundle), and a repo-wide re-sweep confirmed no other fixture references the trigger table without it.
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Operator force-fail for a single hung in-flight activity — see issue #765. Completes the per-activity operator toolkit started by #516's `retry-now`: where `retry-now` skips a *backing-off* (PENDING) activity's backoff, `fail-now` force-fails an *in-flight* (RUNNING) one — an activity wedged against a dead downstream that will never heartbeat-timeout (no `heartbeat_timeout`/`start_to_close` configured) or whose remaining retry curve the operator wants to stop dead. New admin-gated management route `POST /api/harvest/workflows/{id}/activities/{activity_exec_id}/fail-now` (handler `fail_activity_now` in plugin `api.rs`, optional body `{ "reason": "…" }` parsed via the raw-`Bytes` pattern from #504's terminate so a body-less POST works) delegating to the new core primitive `timeout::force_fail_activity(conn, workflow_exec_id, task_id, reason) -> ForceFailActivityOutcome { task_id, queue_name, activity_name, forced, already_forced }`. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration**: the forced failure is recorded through the existing `ActivityFailed` recording path — a new `ActivityFailure::operator_force_failed(reason)` constructor (`failure.rs`, mirrors `circuit_open`) synthesises a non-retryable typed failure with the new stable `ERROR_TYPE_OPERATOR_FORCE_FAILED = "OperatorForceFailed"` constant, serialized through the standard `harvest_activity_failure_v1` wire envelope and decoded into the event via `parse_error_payload_full` — the exact decoder `worker::finalize_activity_failure` uses, so the two recording paths cannot diverge (`worker::task_attempt` bumped `pub(crate)` for the same reason). The workflow reads the **distinct failure cause** via the existing threading (`HistoryMatch::Failed` → `HarvestError::ActivityFailed`) plus the new `HarvestError::is_operator_force_failed()` accessor (mirrors `is_circuit_open()`), and advances to its own failure/compensation path — it is **not** terminated. Semantics: **RUNNING-only, non-terminal-execution-only** (the pure `classify_force_fail_target(task_type, state, stored_error)` truth-table helper decides 404/409/idempotent/happy-path: unknown id or wrong workflow → 404 `NotFound`; an owning execution already in a **terminal state** → 409 `Config` — a sealed run's history must never grow another `ActivityFailed`, and a stray RUNNING activity row on a FAILED execution is reachable because a plain workflow failure does not fail open activity rows; the guard is checked after task existence (unknown task still 404s first) and *after* the zero-write idempotent already-forced short-circuit, so a retried fail-now stays idempotent even once the run seals (round-2 hardening below); an existing row that is a *workflow* task → 409 `Config`; PENDING/COMPLETED/CANCELLED or FAILED-with-a-*genuine*-error → 409; FAILED whose stored error parses as the `OperatorForceFailed` envelope → **idempotent no-op success** with `forced: false, already_forced: true` and zero writes). The operator `reason` is truncated at the API boundary to 500 chars via the shared `truncate_operator_reason` helper (extracted from the erase-payloads inline precedent, now used by both handlers) since it is durably recorded in the event message, `details.reason`, and the task-row envelope. **One transaction, documented lock ordering** (execution row `FOR UPDATE` first via `lock_workflow_execution_row_and_load_history`, then the task row — same order as `enforce_activity_timeout` and the worker's finalize paths, so a concurrent late worker result serializes instead of racing): resolve the pending activity id from history (`pending_activity_id_for_task`; a RUNNING row whose history already carries a terminal event for the activity → 409, guaranteeing a second terminal event for the same `activity_id` is never appended), append `ActivityFailed { error_type: "OperatorForceFailed", non_retryable: true, details }`, `queue::fail_task` with the envelope, `queue::wake_workflow_task` — the owning workflow advances within one worker poll cycle. **Retries skipped by construction** (`non_retryable: true` regardless of retry policy); **late results are no-ops** on the FAILED row: a late `Ok` hits `complete_task`'s `state = 'RUNNING'` filter, a late retryable `Err` hits `requeue_for_retry`'s `state = 'RUNNING'` filter (verified already-safe — no resurrect fix was needed; pinned by `late_retryable_error_cannot_resurrect_failed_row`), a late non-retryable `Err` no-ops in `finalize_activity_failure`'s guards. **Pause is deliberately not a blocker** (in-flight enforcement is pause-blind, mirroring Heartbeat/StartToClose in `pause_suppresses_timeout_enforcement`); the wake is durably recorded and deferred by the PAUSED claim gate. **No DLQ row** (matches `finalize_activity_failure`'s deliberate non-DLQ posture); no new metric (none required by the issue). Local activities have no task-queue row → 404. Audited: `OP_ACTIVITY_FAIL_NOW = "activity.fail_now"` with `TARGET_ACTIVITY`, registered in `AUDITED_OPERATIONS`/`CLASSIFIED_ROUTES` (Mutating)/`ALL_MUTATION_ROUTES`; route in all three parallel plugin registries (`management_api_routes()`, request-fields `["reason"]`, response fields) and `docs/api-contract.json` (202 response `{ ok, execution_id, activity_exec_id, activity_name, queue, forced, already_forced, error_type }` — `error_type` always the constant so scripts can key on it). CLI: `harvest workflow fail-activity <workflow_id> <activity_exec_id> [--reason <text>]`. Tests, TDD red→green (red = compile error per the #593 precedent): failure.rs/error.rs unit tests (constructor fields, envelope round-trip, non-retryable-regardless-of-policy, `is_operator_force_failed`), 5 pure truth-table tests for `classify_force_fail_target` plus 4 for `named_activity_has_terminal_event` (timeout.rs, no DB), 17 DB integration tests in `tests/integration/force_fail_tests.rs` (happy path incl. event metadata + execution-stays-RUNNING pin, workflow-task wake, retries-skipped, idempotent repeat with exactly one terminal event + first-reason-preserved zero-writes pin — incl. after the run seals (round-2 hardening below), terminal-execution 409 with zero-writes assertions, terminal-in-history 409 (id-resolved and legacy name-resolved), PENDING/COMPLETED/genuine-FAILED/workflow-task conflicts, unknown/wrong-workflow 404s, late-completion + late-retryable-error no-op pins, paused-execution success) alongside the pure envelope-parser pin `forced_envelope_is_recognized_by_the_full_parser` in the same file, the replay-determinism test `replay_force_failed_activity_takes_compensation_branch_deterministically` in `replayer_tests.rs` (testing feature, no DB — a history carrying the forced `ActivityFailed` replays down the workflow's compensation branch), 8 plugin HTTP integration tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/fail_now_integration.rs` (202 + audit row incl. `x-harvest-actor` attribution, idempotent repeat, 409s, unknown-task/unknown-execution 404s, malformed-activity-id 404 + failed audit row, admin-guard rejection) plus the `fail_now_route_is_classified_and_audited` pin in `contract_regression.rs` and `eris_unauthenticated_fail_activity_now_is_blocked` in the plugin's no-DB `security.rs`, and CLI unit + `request_mapping.rs`/`contract_coverage.rs` tests. **Post-review hardening (four-review consolidated pass, same issue #765):** (1) terminal-execution guard added (`erase::is_terminal_state` under the execution row lock — previously the loaded execution row was discarded and a stray RUNNING row on a sealed run could be forced, appending `ActivityFailed` *after* the terminal event); (2) legacy-row 409 consistency — a task row with `activity_id = NULL` whose activity is terminal-in-history previously `?`-propagated the name-based fallback's `NotFound` as a 404, contradicting the documented 409; `force_fail_activity` now maps that case (new pure `named_activity_has_terminal_event` helper; `pending_activity_id_for_task` itself deliberately unchanged for its other callers) onto the same `terminal_history_conflict` 409 as the id-resolved branch, and the misleading "row will reconcile through the worker's own finalize path" comment was corrected (the state is believed unreachable via engine paths; the 409 is a defensive invariant guard, and the row only reconciles if the in-flight attempt eventually returns); (3) 500-char server-side reason cap (shared `truncate_operator_reason`); (4) doc notes pinning that `ERROR_TYPE_OPERATOR_FORCE_FAILED` is engine-reserved (a fabricated envelope makes a later fail-now misreport `already_forced` — harmless, zero writes) and the #523 workflow-retry interaction (a retried run re-dispatches the activity — use cancel/terminate to stop the run). Both behavior fixes were verified red→green against a real local Postgres in this sandbox (scratch harness, deleted after verification) since Docker/testcontainers is unavailable; CI gains dedicated Docker-backed steps for the core `force_fail` filter and the plugin `fail_now_integration` suite, plus a cheap no-DB `contract_regression` run. **Post-review hardening, round 2 (PR #974 Codex review, P2 — valid, fixed): idempotency now survives sealing.** Round 1's terminal-execution guard deliberately ran *before* classification, so it also won over the idempotent already-forced short-circuit — but the common lifecycle of a *successful* force-fail is that the woken workflow consumes the forced `ActivityFailed` and seals its own run (often FAILED) within one poll cycle, so exactly the retry path the idempotency contract exists for (a lost response / script retry seconds later) flipped from the documented 202 no-op to a 409. Fixed by reordering inside `force_fail_activity`: classification runs first and the `AlreadyForced` case (a FAILED row whose stored error is the `OperatorForceFailed` envelope) returns the idempotent no-op success before the terminal guard is consulted — safe by construction, since both paths are zero-writes, so the sealed-history invariant is untouched; a *non*-already-forced row on a terminal execution (the stray-RUNNING-row case round 1 exists for) still gets the 409, and the pure `classify_force_fail_target` truth table is unchanged. Contract/doc text updated in `docs/api-contract.json` (idempotency + 409 descriptions), the `force_fail_activity` doc comment, and the plugin handler doc. Regression test `retry_after_forced_run_sealed_is_idempotent_no_op` (force → seal FAILED → retry returns `already_forced: true` with zero writes: no event appended, first reason preserved, task and execution rows untouched) in `tests/integration/force_fail_tests.rs` — compile-checked in this sandbox (no Docker), executed by CI's Docker-backed `force_fail` step, per the #543/#544/#601 precedent.
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Terminal-outcome input mapping for completion triggers — route the failure cause, not a blind Null — see issue #748. A source workflow that reaches a **non-`Completed`** terminal state has a `NULL` recorded `output`, so a completion trigger's existing `Passthrough`/`Projection` input mapping delivers `Null` to the target and a failure-routed handler (a Slack alerter, a compensation flow, a retry orchestrator) starts blind — with no way to know *why* the source failed short of a separate `GET /workflows/{id}` on the source. New **`InputMapping::Outcome { projection: Option<String> }`** variant in `completion_trigger.rs` (adjacently tagged `#[serde(tag="type", content="data")]` alongside `Passthrough`/`Static`/`Projection`; `projection` is `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` so `None` serializes to `{"type":"Outcome","data":{}}` and a client may omit the field, mirroring how the sibling `condition` field from #810 is handled) assembles a **fixed terminal-outcome envelope** from the already-loaded execution row: `{terminal_state, source_exec_id, source_workflow_id, source_workflow_name, output, error}` — `terminal_state` is the canonical state string (`"FAILED"`/`"COMPLETED"`/`"CANCELLED"`/`"TIMED_OUT"`/`"TERMINATED"`); `output` is the source's recorded output **only on `COMPLETED`** (`execution.output.as_ref()`, gated on `state == TerminalState::Completed`; `Null` on every other terminal); `error` carries the failure message on `FAILED`/`TIMED_OUT` and the cancel/terminate reason on `CANCELLED`/`TERMINATED` (a plain forward of `execution.error`, naturally `None` on `COMPLETED`). Pure read-side builder `build_outcome_envelope(exec_id, state, workflow_id, workflow_name, output, error) -> Value` (`pub(crate)`, `#[cfg_attr(not(feature = "db"), allow(dead_code))]` since its sole non-test caller is `db`-gated). `projection = None` yields the whole envelope; `projection = Some(path)` plucks a dotted field out of it (e.g. `Some("error")` → the bare error string) via the **same `project_json_path`** machinery `Projection` uses (AC2), with the same graceful `Null`-on-missing-path degradation. **AC5 inline/outbox parity holds by construction**: the envelope is assembled at the **single** inline mapping site in `evaluate_triggers_for_execution`, producing one `target_input` value that flows verbatim to BOTH the same-shard `start_or_load_workflow_execution` call and the cross-shard outbox `DeferredTriggerStart.target_input` — there is no second, divergent assembly path. **AC6 reconciliation**: the typed `CreateCompletionTriggerRequest.input_mapping: Option<InputMapping>` round-trips the new variant through serde with **zero plugin change** (the adjacently-tagged encoding is derived), so the HTTP create → list surface and the builder `with_input_mapping(...)` both accept `Outcome` unchanged; there is no `completion-trigger` CLI subcommand, so the CLI-parity AC reconciles to the HTTP + builder surfaces (the #543/#593/#609 prior-art-reconciliation precedent). **Rollout-ordering caveat** (mirrors #810's): a pre-#748 worker binary deserializes the stored `input_mapping` with `serde_json::from_value(...).unwrap_or(InputMapping::Passthrough)`, so it falls back to `Passthrough` on the unknown `Outcome` variant and delivers `Null` for a failed source — **upgrade the entire worker fleet before registering `Outcome` triggers**. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no new route, no shard-routing change** (AC7) — this is a server-side read-side projection evaluated inside the existing terminal transaction (an in-memory JSON build on the already-loaded execution row). Docs: `docs/completion-triggers.md` "Terminal-outcome input mapping" section; `docs/api-contract.json` `input_mapping` example updated; `examples/completion_triggers.rs` demonstrates a failure-routed `Outcome` trigger. Tests, TDD red→green: unit tests in `completion_trigger.rs` (envelope shape per terminal state incl. FAILED/COMPLETED/TIMED_OUT/CANCELLED/TERMINATED, `projection`-plucks-field, `projection`-missing-path-yields-Null, `projection = None`-yields-full-envelope, `Outcome` serde round-trip in both projection shapes with existing-variant encodings unchanged); integration tests in `completion_triggers_integration.rs` — the success-metric `test_outcome_mapping_delivers_failure_cause_to_target` (FAILED source → target input IS the envelope, failure cause present, output Null, no source describe call), the COMPLETED-output positive-branch `test_outcome_mapping_delivers_output_on_completed_source` (recorded output carried verbatim, error Null, `terminal_state == "COMPLETED"`), and `test_create_trigger_with_outcome_mapping_round_trips` (HTTP create→list preserves the adjacently-tagged mapping) — compile-checked in this sandbox (no Docker daemon), executed in CI, per the #543/#544/#601 precedent.
- **Phase 3.49** (implemented): Two reconciled slices — the select-primitive guardrail extension (#799) and the remaining schedule-run-history gaps (#762). Both issues were **mostly already shipped** (by #600 and #534 respectively); this slice closes only the remaining ACs, following the #543/#593 reconciliation precedent. **#799 — combinator FUNCTIONS added to the select guardrail:** the `ctx.race()` guardrail HVG010 (SelectMacro, #600) previously flagged only `tokio::select!`/`futures::select!`/`select_biased!` macros inside `#[workflow]` bodies; it now also flags the `futures::future::{select, select_all, select_ok, try_select}` combinator **functions** on both surfaces — the macro AST visitor (`determinism_lint.rs`, structural method-call exclusion) and the new det_check `DET011` rule (`det_check.rs`, a substring-table `Rule` at `Error` severity — racing ctx-managed awaitables is always a hazard, no command-aware downgrade). The diagnostic now names the sanctioned alternatives by name: `ctx.race()`, `execute_activity_fan_out*`, `receive_signal_timeout`/`wait_for_signal_timeout`, and `await_condition_timeout`. Suppressible via `#[workflow(allow_nondeterministic_apis)]` (whole-fn) or `// harvest-suppress: DET011 "reason"` (det_check per-site, echoed into `report.suppressions`); one genuine example (`examples/collect_approvals.rs`, a fixed-poll-order `futures::future::select` proven replay-safe) opted out via that mechanism. **ID reconciliation**: the issue proposed HVG011, but that id was already taken by #785 (HashMap iteration); guardrail ids are never reused, so #799 extended the existing HVG010 instead (macro id) / added DET011 (det_check id; DET010 was #785's). **DET011 combinator matching is call-position-bounded** (P2 review fix): the combinator substrings (`future::select(`/`select_all(`/`select_ok(`/`try_select(`) match only when NOT immediately preceded by `.` or an identifier char, so a `.method_call()` form (`q.select_all()`) is not a false positive — mirroring the AST visitor's structural exclusion of method calls; the `select!`/`select_biased!` macro matches are unchanged, and bare `select(` remains deliberately omitted (too collision-prone with query-builder `.select(`). The single `future::select(` pattern matches **both** the qualified `futures::future::select(` form (the substring is preceded by `:`) and the short `use futures::future; future::select(` form (#980 Codex P2 follow-up — the det_check `future::select` short form now matches the AST visitor, which already recognized it); since the engine emits at most one finding per rule per line, a qualified call is never double-counted. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no runtime code — static-analysis + catalog only.** **#762 — remaining `GET /admin/schedules/{id}/runs` gaps closed** on the shipped #534 endpoint: per-row terminal `outcome` (collapse of the run's terminal state) + `error` (first-line, terminal-failed runs only), a top-level `next_run_at` echo, a real `404` on an unknown schedule id (never a silently-empty run list), and **newest-SLOT-first ordering** (`COALESCE(scheduled_for, started_at) DESC, id DESC`) with the keyset cursor + cross-shard merge comparator kept consistent; default limit **20**, max **200**. **Down-shard existence is INDETERMINATE, not 404** (P2 review fix): the existence/`next_run_at` lookup now tracks whether any shard was unreachable; not-found **and** a shard unreachable returns `503` (existence cannot be determined) rather than lying with a definitive `404`, while not-found with all shards reachable stays `404` — preserving "never 500". Plugin HTTP integration test (`schedule_runs_integration.rs`, seeds the schedule on the DOWN shard) added and compile-checked (Docker unavailable in this sandbox, per the #543/#544/#601 precedent). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant; one additive index-only migration** `20260708000002_harvest_schedule_runs_slot_index` (#980 Codex P2) adds a partial expression index `idx_harvest_wfx_schedule_runs_slot ON harvest_workflow_executions (schedule_id, (COALESCE(scheduled_for, started_at)) DESC, id DESC) WHERE schedule_id IS NOT NULL` matching the slot-key `ORDER BY`/keyset predicate — the pre-existing `started_at`-keyed `idx_harvest_wfx_schedule_runs` no longer covered the new ordering, so a high-frequency schedule would sort all retained runs before `LIMIT`; the old index is retained for the #534 summary/origin queries. The double per-shard connection-acquire (existence lookup + runs fan-out) was left as-is — folding it would risk the reviewer-verified ordering/cursor/merge correctness for no functional gain.
- **Phase 3.48** (implemented): Atomic `max_runs` reservation in the schedule backfill path — see issue #688 (follow-up to #488/#677). Replaces the non-dry-run workflow/DAG dispatch loops' stale in-memory budget gate (`schedule.runs_started + dispatched_this_call >= max_runs`, computed against the request-entry snapshot) — which let two concurrent non-dry-run backfill requests with one remaining `max_runs` slot each pass their gate and both dispatch, over-running the bounded-runs budget (#478) and feeding an out-of-budget run into scheduled carryover (#488) (Codex P2 from PR #677) — with a per-slot atomic conditional `UPDATE harvest_schedules SET runs_started = runs_started + 1 WHERE exhausted_at IS NULL AND (max_runs IS NULL OR runs_started < max_runs) RETURNING runs_started` on the schedule's own shard (`reserve_backfill_budget_slot`, mirroring `trigger_schedule_now`'s pre-increment; the statement is shared with a no-DB `debug_query` shape test via the `reserve_backfill_budget_slot_stmt!` macro so editing the guard/increment/RETURNING breaks the test). The reservation is placed AFTER the prior-run and admission checks (a duplicate/blocked slot consumes no budget); `Ok(None)` (0 rows = exhausted) flips a `budget_hit` fast-path skipping every remaining slot as `max_runs_exhausted`. Every non-committing post-reservation path releases via a dedicated **decrement-only** `release_backfill_budget_slot` (NOT `rollback_manual_trigger_budget` — a backfill release provably never set `exhausted_at` itself, so clearing it could resurrect a concurrent actor's foreign exhaustion or strand `next_run_at = NULL`; conservative by design — a released slot at worst leaves a spare slot, never an over-run); committing paths (`start Ok(created)`, throttle `Deferred{fresh:true}`) call `transition_backfill_exhausted_if_reached` (guarded on `exhausted_at IS NULL`, sets `exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason` only — see the post-review note below on why it deliberately does not touch `next_run_at`). Both release and transition warn-on-error so a leaked/failed `+1` is diagnosable rather than silently drifting `runs_started`. The post-loop batch increment is removed (budget is now consumed per-slot). A cheap in-memory attribution gate is retained at loop position 1 (after the `budget_hit` fast-path, before the `max_active_runs`/admission gates) so single-request `skipped_reasons`/`status` envelopes are byte-preserved — the atomic reservation is the authoritative race-safe gate; the snapshot gate only preserves single-request reason attribution and is stale under concurrency. Also adds `WorkflowEvent::workflow_started(input, timestamp)` (`const fn`, `#[must_use]`) defaulting the #488 carryover (`last_completion_result`/`last_error`) and #508 (`scheduled_time`) additive fields to `None`, giving downstream constructors a source-compatible path. **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no route/registry/audit change.** Pool-connection note (corrected by Codex round-2 F4 below): on a single-shard deployment the backfill route holds exactly **one** pool connection at a time — budget accounting reuses the per-slot execution connection (`same_pool`), so a web pool of size 1 is safe (restoring the pre-#688 single-connection invariant); a dedicated schedule-shard connection is held across the loop **only** for a genuinely different (multi-shard) schedule pool, which does not contend with the execution pool. Known residual gaps (pre-existing, out of scope): the scheduler *tick* still gates `max_runs` with an in-memory `live_runs_started` snapshot, so a tick firing concurrently with a backfill on a near-capacity schedule can still jointly over-run (identical for `trigger_schedule_now`-vs-tick — a complete fix converts the tick to the same atomic reserve); a throttle `Deferred{fresh:true}` backfill slot keeps its reservation even if the throttle scanner later drops the pending row past `schedule_to_start` (as in the pre-#688 `dispatched_this_call` accounting). Tests: pure `backfill_budget_reached` truth table (incl. the `max_runs = 1` money-test boundary) + a `debug_query` shape test bound to the real reservation query via the shared macro; integration tests in `autumn-harvest-plugin/tests/api_scheduler_integration.rs` (the two-concurrent-requests-dispatch-exactly-one money test under a multi-thread runtime with genuinely spawned tasks — falsifies the pre-fix stale-snapshot code; single-request over-window budget cap for workflow + DAG; unlimited-schedule observability increment; a deterministic reserve-then-release-leaves-budget-intact test via the oversized-input path proving `release_backfill_budget_slot` returns every reservation) — compile-checked in this sandbox (no Docker), CI runs them Docker-backed, matching the #543/#544/#601 precedent. **Post-review hardening (PR #979, two Codex P2 findings):** (F1) the reservation guard predicate was `max_runs IS NULL OR runs_started < max_runs`, so a row with a literal `max_runs = 0` reserved 0 rows (`0 < 0` false) and the whole backfill wrongly skipped every slot as `max_runs_exhausted`, even though `backfill_budget_reached`, the up-front exhaustion check, and the dry-run projection all treat non-positive `max_runs` as UNLIMITED — fixed by adding a `max_runs <= 0` clause to the predicate (`max_runs IS NULL OR max_runs <= 0 OR runs_started < max_runs`), so `max_runs = 0` = unlimited everywhere; the reservation shape test's asserted substring was updated to match, and a new integration test `backfill_max_runs_zero_is_treated_as_unlimited` writes `0` to the column directly (the builder normalizes `0 → None`) and asserts every slot dispatches with `exhausted_at` staying NULL. (F2) a stale `max_runs_exhausted` could stick after a concurrent near-cap release: when two concurrent backfills reserve near `max_runs`, one may transition the row to exhausted on the temporary count, then the other's slot hits a release path (`AlreadyExists`/oversized-input/admission-error) that decrements `runs_started` below the cap — but the decrement-only release left `exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason` set, stranding the schedule exhausted with a free slot. Fixed in two parts: **(2a)** `transition_backfill_exhausted_if_reached` no longer nulls `next_run_at` (it set only `exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason` — restoring the original pre-#688 post-loop batch-increment behavior; backfill dispatches historical slots and does not own the forward-cadence pointer, and the `exhausted_at IS NULL` due-list filter already excludes an exhausted row, so nulling it only stranded the schedule off the due-list once un-exhausted); **(2b)** `release_backfill_budget_slot`, after its decrement, runs a second best-effort guarded UPDATE (shared with a new `clear_stale_max_runs_exhaustion_stmt!` macro + `clear_stale_max_runs_exhaustion_generates_the_guarded_clear_update` shape test) that clears `exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason` only when `exhausted_reason = 'max_runs_exhausted'` AND the below-cap predicate holds — so an `end_at` exhaustion set by a concurrent tick is NEVER resurrected, and `next_run_at` is left intact (thanks to 2a) so the row returns to the due-list correctly once un-exhausted. The cross-actor case where a concurrent *tick* nulls `next_run_at` while exhausting the row mid-backfill is **now fixed** by round 2 F3 below (the release re-arms `next_run_at`), no longer a documented limitation. The F2b release-clears-exhaustion path is a concurrent-only scenario not deterministically reproducible in a single-request test (once a slot transitions exhausted intra-request, subsequent reservations fail the `exhausted_at IS NULL` guard and set `budget_hit`, so no release-after-transition occurs within one request) — covered by the shape test + code reasoning, matching the no-Docker precedent. **Post-review hardening, round 2 (PR #979, two more Codex P2 findings):** (F3) **re-arm `next_run_at` when the release clears a tick-nulled exhaustion.** The scheduler tick's `max_runs` exhaustion path nulls `next_run_at` (`tick_one_workflow_schedule` in `scheduler.rs`), and the due-list query requires `next_run_at IS NOT NULL` — so when a concurrent tick exhausts the schedule DURING a backfill and the backfill then releases below cap, F2b's `exhausted_at`/`exhausted_reason` clear left the schedule un-exhausted yet off the due-list (previously documented as a known limitation). Fixed by giving `release_backfill_budget_slot` a new `rearm_next: Option<DateTime<Utc>>` parameter — **corrected by round 3 F5 below** to the pre-tick due `next_run_at` snapshot (`schedule.next_run_at`, loaded at backfill request start) rather than a recompute from `now`. The clear UPDATE now captures its rows-affected, and **only when it actually un-exhausted a `max_runs_exhausted` row** does a follow-up guarded UPDATE re-arm `next_run_at` — guarded on `next_run_at IS NULL` (so the common intra-backfill case, where our own transition never nulled it, is a no-op) and `exhausted_at IS NULL` (so only a just-un-exhausted row is touched), via a new `rearm_backfill_next_run_stmt!` macro + `rearm_backfill_next_run_generates_the_guarded_rearm_update` no-DB shape test. Every `release_backfill_budget_slot` call site in both dispatch loops passes the per-slot `rearm_next`. **Post-review hardening, round 3 (PR #979, Codex F5):** the round-2 F3 re-arm computed `rearm_next` as `next_run_after_pub(parsed_schedule.as_ref(), now)` — the first slot AFTER `now` — which, when a concurrent tick exhausted an already-DUE catchup schedule (`next_run_at <= now`) mid-backfill and the backfill then released below cap, advanced PAST the due slot the tick skipped, silently dropping that scheduled run. Fixed by re-arming to the pre-tick due `next_run_at` **snapshot** (`schedule.next_run_at`, the `HarvestSchedule` loaded once at the top of `schedule_backfill`, immutable within the handler and captured before any concurrent tick nulled it) instead of a recompute from `now`, so a catchup schedule retries the exact slot the tick skipped; `next_run_after_pub` is now unused in `api.rs` and its import was removed. The `rearm_backfill_next_run_stmt!` macro, its shape test, and the guards are unchanged — only the source of the bound value changed. Manual-schedule correctness is preserved (a manual schedule has `next_run_at = NULL` → `rearm_next = None` → no re-arm). (F4) **connection self-deadlock at pool size 1 (regression from #679's own fix).** #679 acquired a loop-level `sched_conn` on the schedule shard held across the ENTIRE dispatch loop while each slot also checked out a per-slot exec `conn`; on a **single-shard** deployment with a web pool of size 1 (`pool.rs` only rejects size 0), the schedule and exec shards are the SAME pool, so holding `sched_conn` while acquiring `conn` self-deadlocks (the route waits on a connection it already holds). The original pre-#688 backfill held only one connection at a time and was pool-size-1 safe. Fixed by choosing the budget connection per loop arm: each arm computes `let same_pool = schedule_shard == exec_shard;` (workflow: `exec_shard = runtime.router.default_shard()`; DAG: `exec_shard = shard_id` from `pick_for_dag`) and acquires a separate `sched_conn_opt = if same_pool { None } else { Some(acquire_conn(pool.pool_for(schedule_shard)).await?) }` held across the loop ONLY for a genuinely different (multi-shard) pool; at each reserve/release/transition call site `let budget_conn = sched_conn_opt.as_mut().unwrap_or(&mut conn);` reuses the per-slot exec `conn` in the `same_pool` case. When `same_pool`, all budget ops run sequentially on the single `conn` (never two concurrent connections → pool-size-1 safe); when different-pool, the two connections are from distinct pools so concurrent holds don't contend. Borrow-ordering: the two throttle-refund start arms now call `refund_rate_limit_token(&mut conn, ...)` FIRST (that borrow ends) before taking `budget_conn` for the release, and each `budget_conn` borrow is scoped to a single statement so `conn` is free for the following throttle/start work. Assumption: distinct `ShardId`s map to distinct pools in standard single/multi-shard topologies (single-shard always has `schedule_shard == exec_shard`). Regression guard: `backfill_single_shard_pool_size_one_does_not_deadlock` (a non-dry-run backfill against a size-1 pool wrapped in a `tokio::time::timeout` so a deadlock is a clear failure, not a hung binary). Both fixes: no new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration; DB/HTTP tests compile-checked here (no Docker), CI runs them Docker-backed. **Post-review hardening, round 4 (PR #979, Codex F6): exhaust the backfill from the LIVE row, not a request-start snapshot.** `transition_backfill_exhausted_if_reached` decided exhaustion from `backfill_budget_reached(reserved_runs_started, schedule.max_runs)` — a `max_runs` value snapshotted at request start — while the reservation UPDATE already evaluated `max_runs`/`runs_started` from the live row. A concurrent `PATCH /admin/schedules/{id}` (issue #771) changing `max_runs` between the reservation and the transition could therefore wrongly exhaust on a raised/removed cap (disabling the schedule from a stale higher `max_runs`) or wrongly skip exhausting an at-cap row on a lowered cap. Fixed by rewriting the transition to a single conditional UPDATE whose WHERE clause reads `max_runs`/`runs_started` from the row itself (`WHERE exhausted_at IS NULL AND max_runs IS NOT NULL AND max_runs > 0 AND runs_started >= max_runs`), so it always matches the same live row the reservation touched — both budget decisions (reserve and exhaust) are now snapshot-free. `next_run_at` is still deliberately untouched (backfill doesn't own the forward-cadence pointer). The transition helper drops its `new_runs_started`/`max_runs` params (all three call sites in both dispatch loops updated), the reservation match no longer captures the returned `runs_started` (`Ok(Some(_)) => {}`, value unused), and the now-dead pure `backfill_budget_reached` helper + its `backfill_budget_reached_truth_table` unit test were removed (the SQL `max_runs > 0 AND runs_started >= max_runs` predicate encodes the same rule; non-positive/NULL = unlimited). The UPDATE is extracted into a `transition_backfill_exhausted_stmt!` macro (mirroring the sibling `reserve_backfill_budget_slot_stmt!` / `clear_stale_max_runs_exhaustion_stmt!` / `rearm_backfill_next_run_stmt!`) so the live path and the new no-DB `transition_backfill_exhausted_generates_the_live_row_guarded_update` shape test share one source of truth. No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration; the existing integration tests (`backfill_over_window_*`, `backfill_max_runs_zero_*`, the money test) still assert exhaustion when `runs_started` reaches `max_runs`, compile-checked here (no Docker), CI runs them Docker-backed.
- **Phase 3.50** (implemented): `ctx.sleep_until(id, deadline)` — absolute-time durable timer (issue #749). The blessed, replay-safe companion to the relative, whole-second `ctx.timer(id, secs)`: a workflow waits until an *absolute instant* (24h before an expiry, midnight UTC, a coupon lapse) with one call instead of the hand-rolled `ctx.timer(id, (deadline - ctx.system_now()).num_seconds() as u64)` pattern, which carries two foot-guns — reaching for `chrono::Utc::now()` breaks replay (guardrail HVG001), and a deadline already in the past underflows `as u64` into a multi-year sleep. **Design A (system_now + delegate to timer):** `sleep_until` captures the current wall clock **once** via the deterministic `WorkflowContext::system_now` (frozen into history as an existing `SideEffectRecorded { kind: Now }` event, issue #384 — so every replay recomputes the identical remaining duration from that frozen instant and resolves at the same instant on every worker regardless of the replaying worker's clock), then delegates to `self.timer(timer_id, remaining_secs)` — reusing the whole-second suspension, `match_timer_strict` divergence detection (a non-deterministic author-supplied `deadline` surfaces as ordinary timer non-determinism, not a panic), and the #526 virtual-clock advance uniformly. The remaining duration is computed by the pure, unit-tested `pub(crate) context::remaining_secs_until(deadline, now)`: a past-or-equal deadline clamps to `0` (fires on the next poll — AC3, never a wrapped multi-year sleep); any positive sub-second remainder rounds **up** to the next whole second (nanosecond-precise via `chrono::Duration::subsec_nanos`, matching `wait_for_signal_timeout` — harvest timers have whole-second granularity, so the wait never resolves *before* the deadline); a defensive `u64::MAX` fallback covers a delta whose whole seconds overflow `i64`→`u64` (unreachable for any chrono-valid `DateTime<Utc>` pair, since chrono's own range caps the delta far below that; were it hit, the worker's `chrono_duration_from_secs` would surface it as a `Config` error at persist, exactly like an overflowing `ctx.timer` duration). **No new `WorkflowEvent` variant, no migration, no worker/event/schema change** — a `sleep_until` timer is byte-identical to a `ctx.timer` timer of the same computed duration; like `ctx.timer`, exactly one timer suspends per call and it cannot share a suspension batch with activity/child-workflow/signal commands. Tests, TDD red→green: pure `remaining_secs_until` truth table (incl. sub-millisecond micro/nanosecond round-up cases) + three context-level unit tests (live emits `RecordSideEffect(Now)` + `StartTimer`; past deadline → zero-duration timer; recorded history replays with no new commands) in `context.rs`; the falsifiable #749 bar `sleep_until_replays_deterministically` (**1000 distinct** fixtures — the frozen `system_now()` instant and the deadline offset both vary with the iteration, walking sub-second jitter through the round-up boundary — each replaying `ReplaySucceeded` with zero divergences) plus a past-deadline-clamp replay, a mutated-duration `TimerMismatch` test (`sleep_until_duration_mismatch_surfaces_as_timer_nondeterminism`), and a genuine structural-divergence test (`sleep_until_missing_side_effect_surfaces_as_nondeterminism` — dropping the frozen `SideEffectRecorded(Now)` event surfaces as `NonDeterminismKind::SideEffectDrift`, not a panic) in `tests/integration/replayer_tests.rs`; `WorkflowTestEnv` fixtures (future deadline resolves without real sleep, with the recorded `TimerStarted.duration_secs` reconstructed against the frozen `SideEffectRecorded(Now)` capture to prove it equals `remaining_secs_until(deadline, frozen_now)`; past deadline records a zero-duration timer — the concrete AC3 test) in `tests/integration/workflow_test_env_tests.rs`. Example `autumn-harvest/examples/sleep_until_renewal_reminder.rs` (a "send a renewal reminder 24h before expiry" workflow with embedded `WorkflowTestEnv` tests). All executed locally (no DB required — the entire slice is `context.rs` + `testing`-gated tests). **Post-review hardening (PR #985):** (1) `remaining_secs_until` switched from millisecond truncation to nanosecond-precise round-up (`chrono::Duration::subsec_nanos`) so any positive sub-second remainder rounds up, with sub-millisecond unit-test coverage; (2) the `WorkflowTestEnv` resolves-without-real-sleep test now reconstructs the expected timer duration from the frozen `system_now()` capture (a genuine `sleep_until` assertion, not the test-env clock-advance invariant); (3) the mutated-timer test renamed to `..._duration_mismatch_...` (it mutates duration, not order) and a genuine structural-divergence test added; (4) the 1000-pass money test builds 1000 *distinct* fixtures instead of cloning one; (5) rustdoc/CLAUDE.md wording clarified — the `u64::MAX` case is a defensive fallback, plus usage notes on deriving `deadline` deterministically, preferring `ctx.timer` for relative waits, the `WorkflowTestEnv` real-vs-virtual-clock caveat, and the reserved `__`-prefixed `timer_id` namespace.
- **Phase 4** (next): production hardening -- sharding, observability, metrics, dashboard (Vantage UI — Workers tab shipped in issue #142; DLQ, schedules, and DAG visualization pages remain); Step 5 of issue #256 (remove classic DAG executor, drop `harvest_dag_runs`). Note: the cancellation primitive and `Saga` both ship; their interaction semantics, idempotency contract, and replay-determinism contract are documented in `docs/saga.md` and locked in by three integration tests in `tests/saga_tests.rs` (issue #238).

---

## Architecture

### Crate Relationship

`autumn-harvest` is the core engine crate. It re-exports everything from `autumn-harvest-macros` through `prelude.rs`. Autumn-specific integration lives in the separate `autumn-harvest-plugin` crate, which provides `HarvestPlugin`, the management API router, and app lifecycle wiring.

Macro-generated code must use `::autumn_harvest::` paths for everything. The proc-macro crate has no dependency on `serde_json` or `autumn-web` itself; it emits token streams that resolve via the `::autumn_harvest::` path. `lib.rs` re-exports `serde_json` at `::autumn_harvest::serde_json` and exposes its own local `task_duration()` parser at `::autumn_harvest::task_duration` for exactly this reason.

Do not change macros to emit `::serde_json::` or `::autumn_web::` paths — downstream crates will not have those as direct dependencies and the code will fail to compile.

### Companion Function Pattern

`#[workflow]` generates a hidden companion function alongside the user's function:

```
pub fn __autumn_workflow_info_{name}() -> ::autumn_harvest::WorkflowInfo
```

`#[activity]` generates:

```
pub fn __autumn_activity_info_{name}() -> ::autumn_harvest::ActivityInfo
```

`workflows![name1, name2]` expands to `vec![__autumn_workflow_info_name1(), __autumn_workflow_info_name2()]`.

`activities![name1, name2]` expands to `vec![__autumn_activity_info_name1(), __autumn_activity_info_name2()]`.

`#[query(workflow = "name")]` generates:

```
pub fn __autumn_query_handler_info_{name}() -> ::autumn_harvest::QueryHandlerInfo
```

`#[update(workflow = "name")]` generates:

```
pub fn __autumn_update_handler_info_{name}() -> ::autumn_harvest::UpdateHandlerInfo
```

`queries![name1, name2]` expands to `vec![__autumn_query_handler_info_name1(), __autumn_query_handler_info_name2()]`.

`updates![name1, name2]` expands to `vec![__autumn_update_handler_info_name1(), __autumn_update_handler_info_name2()]`.

### Key Design Decisions

**1. UUID PKs for execution IDs, not i64**

`ExecutionId`, `ActivityExecId`, and `HarvestTimer`/`HarvestSignal`/`DagRun`/`TaskQueueItem` all use `Uuid` as their primary key. Execution IDs must be generated before DB insert (distributed, shard-safe). The i64 convention for application domain tables does NOT apply here.

The `harvest_events` table is the exception: its `id` column is `BIGSERIAL i64` because events are strictly local and append-only — the sequence is never shared across shards.

**2. `db` feature gates all Diesel code**

`schema.rs` and `models.rs` are compiled only when `features = ["db"]`. `default = ["db"]`, so it compiles in by default. Tests on Windows run `--no-default-features` to avoid OpenSSL dependency. CI tests the `db` feature on Linux.

**3. Adjacently-tagged event JSON**

`WorkflowEvent` uses `#[serde(tag = "type", content = "data")]`. This emits `{"type": "ActivityScheduled", "data": {...}}`. Postgres can extract the event type with `payload->>'type'` without parsing the full payload. Never change this tagging — stored events depend on it.

**4. Append-only event invariant**

Never remove or reorder `WorkflowEvent` variants. Stored JSON in `harvest_events.event_data` must always deserialize into the same variant names after deployment. Add new variants at the end.

**Sanctioned in-place mutation exception — payload erasure (`erase.rs`, issue #495):** `erase_workflow_payloads` is the **only** operation (alongside heartbeat checkpoints in `queue::record_heartbeat`) permitted to mutate existing `harvest_events.event_data` rows in-place.
